6. Design Principles

P1 — Information-Theoretic Threshold Security

Statement. Any t valid shares reconstruct the secret exactly (I1). Any t-1 or fewer shares are statistically independent of the secret — the conditional distribution of the secret given t-1 shares is uniform over F_p (I2).

Mechanism. Sample a uniformly random degree-(t-1) polynomial p(x) over F_p with p(0) = secret. Evaluate at n distinct non-zero points x_1, …, x_n. The t-1 evaluations leave one degree of freedom (the constant term), so the posterior distribution on secret is uniform.

Tradeoff table.

Property Shamir SSS Computational Secret Sharing
Secrecy below threshold Information-theoretic Computational (break cipher)
Share size = secret size (one field element) Can be smaller
Adversary model Unbounded Polynomial-time
Reconstruction Exact (Lagrange) May require decryption

Operational implication. The CSPRNG sampling polynomial coefficients is security-critical; a biased sampler downgrades from information-theoretic to computational secrecy.

Violation consequence. A degree-(t-2) polynomial means one fewer coefficient of randomness; t-1 shares now uniquely determine the secret. A non-uniform sampler creates a distinguishable bias that leaks the secret.

P2 — Verifiability Without a Trusted Dealer

Statement. Every recipient checks its share against public commitments (Feldman or Pedersen). DKG removes the dealer entirely — no single party is trusted with or learns the joint secret.

Mechanism — Feldman. Dealer publishes {C_j = g^{a_j}}. Recipient with id i checks: g^{s_i} == product_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j^{i^j}. Binding under discrete log.

Mechanism — Pedersen. Dealer publishes {C_j = g^{a_j} h^{b_j}} with independent h = H-to-point("h"). Recipient checks with companion share. Perfectly hiding: C_j reveals nothing about a_j.

Mechanism — DKG. Each of n parties runs a Pedersen VSS as dealer. Qualified set = parties whose shares all pass verification. Final share of party i = sum of sub-shares from qualified dealers. Joint public key = sum of qualified dealers' C_0 commitments.

Tradeoff. Feldman commitments are more compact (one vector of points) but binding-only. Pedersen requires double the commitment material but provides information-theoretic secrecy of the shared value. DKG multiplies communication by O(n^2).

Operational implication. The complaint round in Phase 8 must run even when no complaints are expected; skipping it opens a vector where a malicious party pre-commits to a bad polynomial and avoids detection.

Violation consequence. Skipping share verification during DKG allows a malicious dealer to distribute shares summing to a chosen value, while announcing a fake joint public key, fully controlling signing.

P3 — No Party Ever Holds the Full Secret

Statement. In DKG and threshold signing, the joint secret key p(0) is never reconstructed in any single memory location during normal operation. It exists implicitly as the sum of distributed shares.

Mechanism. DKG: final share_i = sum_{qualified d} sub-share_{d,i}. The joint secret = sum of all dealers' secrets, never summed in one place. Signing: partial z_i computed from share_i; aggregator sums z_i values (scalars), never the shares themselves.

API constraint. tss-dkg must not expose a reconstruct_joint_secret function in its public API. tss-cli's reconstruct subcommand operates on a separate, explicitly user-initiated offline path.

Tradeoff. Signing requires O(t) round-trip communication. Emergency reconstruction for key recovery must be done offline with explicit operator intent and quorum consent.

Violation consequence. If any node reconstructs the full key to produce a signature, that node is a single point of compromise; compromise of that node after reconstruction retroactively voids the threshold guarantee.

P4 — Constant-Time Secret Handling

Statement. All operations whose execution path or timing could vary with secret values (share content, nonces, Lagrange coefficients, field inverses) execute in data-independent time.

Mechanism.

Operation Constant-Time Method
Field inverse (Fermat) Square-and-multiply with fixed exponent (p-2); no branching on bit value
Lagrange coefficient CT multiply and CT inverse via subtle::Choice
Scalar multiplication Montgomery ladder or fixed-window; subtle::ConditionallySelectable for point selection
Share equality subtle::ConstantTimeEq; never == on secret Scalar
Share comparison for dedup Sort by ShareId (public), then CT equality check on value

Tradeoff. CT field operations are 1.5–3× slower than variable-time on modern CPUs. Some LLVM optimizations must be suppressed via core::hint::black_box.

Operational implication. Run dudect harness in CI on tss-field::inv, tss-shamir::lagrange_coeff, and tss-group::scalar_mul with 10^6 sample pairs per function. Fail CI if t-value exceeds 4.5.

Violation consequence. A timing leak in Lagrange inversion (denominator prod(x_i - x_j)) allows a network adversary measuring response time to recover share values via lattice-based timing attacks.

P5 — Binding Factors Stop Rogue-Nonce Attacks

Statement. FROST computes a per-signer binding factor rho_i = H1(i || msg || commitment-list) before partial signing. Each partial sig is bound to this nonce list, preventing Drijvers-style forgery.

Attack prevented. Without binding: a corrupt signer participates in k signing sessions simultaneously, collects k sets of nonce commitments, and solves a system of linear equations over ROM outputs (ROS problem) to produce a valid aggregate signature without knowing any share. With binding factors, each session's partial sig is bound to its specific commitment list, making cross-session manipulation impossible.

Derivation.

rho_i = H1(encode(i) || msg || encode(commitment-list))
R     = sum_i (D_i + rho_i * E_i)
c     = H2(R || group_pubkey || msg)
z_i   = d_i + rho_i*e_i + lambda_i * share_i * c

Tradeoff. The aggregator must broadcast the full commitment list to all signers before round 2 begins. This adds one message-passing step or requires a larger round-1 message carrying all commitments.

Operational implication. The commitment-list used to compute rho_i must be deterministically encoded (sorted by ShareId, canonical point encoding) to ensure all parties compute identical rho_i values.

Violation consequence. Any deviation in commitment-list encoding between parties causes rho_i mismatch, producing z_i values that do not aggregate to a valid signature — a non-obvious correctness failure masquerading as a security feature.

P6 — Robustness with Identifiable Abort

Statement. When a party submits an invalid VSS share, an invalid partial signature, or an invalid DKG contribution, the protocol identifies that party by cryptographic evidence so the honest majority can exclude it and proceed.

Detection mechanisms by phase.

Phase Misbehavior Detection Evidence
Phase 6 (Feldman VSS) Share fails commitment check g^{s_i} != product C_j^{i^j} commitment vector + claimed share
Phase 8 (DKG) Sub-share fails VSS verify Pedersen check fails dealer id + share + companion share
Phase 10 (FROST round 2) Partial sig z_i invalid g^{z_i} != R_i * Y_i^c (D_i, E_i, z_i) triple
Phase 11 (BLS) Partial sig sigma_i invalid e(sigma_i, g) != e(H(m), Y_i) sigma_i + Y_i

Tradeoff. Identifiable abort requires each complaint to be broadcast and verified by all parties (O(n) messages per complaint). Pure abort is simpler but enables indefinite DoS by a single malicious party.

Operational implication. tss-protocol must maintain a disqualified: HashSet<ShareId> across rounds. Once disqualified, a party's future messages are ignored. The qualified set must have size >= t for signing to proceed.

Violation consequence. Without identifiable abort, a single malicious party can abort signing sessions indefinitely and deny service without accountability or recourse.

P7 — Proactive Refresh Bounds Exposure

Statement. Shares are periodically re-randomized via additive resharing of zero (constant term = 0). The joint secret is unchanged; old shares from a prior epoch are computationally useless in the new epoch.

Refresh protocol.

Each party i samples a fresh degree-(t-1) polynomial r_i(x) with r_i(0) = 0.
Party i sends r_i(j) to party j for all j.
Party j updates: new_share_j = old_share_j + sum_i r_i(j).
New sharing: sum_i r_i(0) = 0, so secret unchanged.

Epoch model. An attacker who compromises fewer than t parties within epoch e gains nothing about the secret even with all shares from epochs e-1, e-2, …

Tradeoff. Refresh requires full n-party interaction; a party offline during refresh must be caught up via lost-share recovery. Epoch boundaries must be externally enforced (Phase 13 timeout).

Operational implication. After refresh, all prior epoch share material must be zeroized (P9). Epoch number must be authenticated in the refresh transcript to reject replayed prior-epoch refresh messages.

Violation consequence. Without refresh, a slow-moving attacker can compromise t parties across disjoint time windows (e.g., one party per year in a 5-year deployment) and eventually reconstruct the secret.

P8 — Threshold Signatures Are Indistinguishable from Single-Signer

Statement. An aggregate FROST signature (R, z) and a BLS threshold signature sigma each verify under the joint public key Y with the standard verification equation, indistinguishable from a single-party signature.

Verify equations.

Scheme Verify equation Key type
FROST (Schnorr) g^z == R * Y^c where c = H(R, Y, msg) Y = EC point (sum of share commitments)
Threshold BLS e(sigma, g) == e(H(m), Y) Y = G2 point (joint public key)

Tradeoff. Joint public key Y must be established via DKG; there is no way to derive it from individual share public keys without a protocol. The verifier holds a single standard key and never needs threshold-aware logic.

Operational implication. tss-cli verify subcommand takes (message, signature, pubkey) and uses the standard verify path from tss-schnorr or tss-bls — no special threshold-aware parameter.

Violation consequence. If partial signatures are included in the final output, verifiers must implement threshold aggregation, breaking the abstraction and expanding the verifier attack surface.

P9 — Secrets Are Zeroized

Statement. All secret-bearing allocations (polynomial coefficients, sub-shares, nonces d_i/e_i, intermediate Lagrange scalars, KeyShare.secret_share) are overwritten with zeros immediately after use, with writes protected from compiler elision.

Types requiring Zeroize + ZeroizeOnDrop.

Type Secret fields
Polynomial coeffs: Vec<Scalar>
Share value: Scalar
KeyShare secret_share: Scalar
NonceCommitment private nonce fields (d_i, e_i as Scalar)
Scalar raw limbs
Temporary buffer Zeroizing<Vec<Scalar>> in Lagrange loop

Nonce reuse catastrophe. Same nonce (d_i, e_i) in two signing sessions with different messages allows solving: z_i - z_i' = lambda_i * share_i * (c - c'). Yields share_i in one division. Nonces must be fresh per session and zeroized after use.

Tradeoff. Compiler may elide writes to memory it believes dead. The zeroize crate uses volatile stores plus memory fences to prevent this. Stack-allocated buffers may persist in core dumps if the OS does not scrub pages on deallocation.

Violation consequence. A heap profiler or crash dump exposing a non-zeroized polynomial buffer allows an attacker with memory-read access to recover the shared secret without any cryptographic attack.

P10 — Fail Closed on Inconsistency

Statement. Duplicate ShareIds, out-of-range field values, failed commitment checks, under-threshold reconstruction, and malformed messages return errors immediately. No partial or approximate result is ever returned.

Error taxonomy.

Condition Error variant Crate
shares.len() < t TssError::BelowThreshold { have, need } tss-shamir
ShareId(0) used TssError::InvalidShareId tss-core
Duplicate ShareId in set TssError::DuplicateShareId(id) tss-shamir
Scalar value >= p TssError::OutOfRangeScalar tss-field
VSS commit check fail VssError::ShareVerificationFailed { share_id, dealer_id } tss-vss
Partial sig invalid SignError::InvalidPartialSignature { signer_id } tss-schnorr / tss-bls
Point not on curve GroupError::NotOnCurve tss-group
Point not in subgroup GroupError::NotInSubgroup tss-group

Operational implication. tss-cli must surface the full error variant with context to the operator. Logs must include the offending ShareId or dealer ID so the party can be identified and excluded.

Violation consequence. Silently continuing after a failed VSS check allows a malicious dealer to have injected a share pointing to a chosen (attacker-controlled) secret while the recipient believes it participated in a legitimate DKG.

P11 — Domain Separation Prevents Cross-Protocol Hash Confusion

Statement. Every hash invocation in the system (hash-to-scalar, hash-to-point, Schnorr challenge, FROST binding factor, transcript append, Pedersen generator derivation) is prefixed with a distinct, fixed domain-separation label. No two protocol contexts ever hash the same encoded bytes under the same label.

Label registry.

Context Label Consumer
Schnorr challenge "FROST-{ciphersuite}-chal-v1" tss-schnorr
Binding factor "FROST-{ciphersuite}-rho-v1" tss-schnorr
Pedersen second generator "tss-pedersen-h-v1" tss-group
BLS hash-to-curve ciphersuite DST per RFC 9380 §8.12.2 tss-bls
Transcript append "tss-transcript-v1" || round-name tss-protocol

Tradeoff. Domain-separation labels add fixed byte overhead per hash call and must be versioned (a label change is a protocol-breaking change, tracked as a semver-major bump per Compatibility & Versioning). The alternative — a single unlabeled hash function reused everywhere — is strictly unsafe: it lets a value hashed in one context be replayed as a valid input in another.

Violation consequence. Reusing an unlabeled H(x) for both the Pedersen generator derivation and the FROST challenge would let an adversary who controls x in one context force a colliding value in the other, breaking the independence assumption that Pedersen hiding and Schnorr unforgeability both depend on. Applied in: Phase 5 (hash_to_point), Phase 6–7 (generator derivation), Phase 9–10 (challenge/binding factor), Phase 11 (BLS hash-to-curve), §8.12.2 domain-separation labels.

Where enforced. tss-group/src/hash.rs; every call site passes an explicit domain: &[u8] argument — there is no zero-argument hash helper in the public API, so omitting a label is a compile error, not a runtime one.

P12 — Complaint Evidence Must Be Independently Verifiable

Statement. Every DKG complaint carries evidence (the disputed share and blinding value) that any third honest party can re-verify against the public commitment without trusting either the complainant or the accused dealer. Guilt is never established by majority vote or accusation alone.

Why this is separate from P6. P6 says the protocol can identify a misbehaving party and restart around it. P12 is the narrower guarantee that makes P6 possible without a trusted judge: the re-verification pedersen_verify_share(complainant_id, share, blinding, commitments) is a pure function of public data, so every honest party reaches the same verdict independently — there is no step where parties must agree by discussion or by trusting a coordinator.

Consequence for the complaint round (§8.3.1, ADR-30). Exactly one of {dealer, complainant} is disqualified per complaint, and the choice follows deterministically from the same equation every party evaluates locally. This is what prevents a coalition of colluding parties from removing an honest dealer by lying: a false complaint fails the same public check an honest complaint passes.

Tradeoff. Independent verifiability requires revealing the disputed share and blinding value in the clear as complaint evidence — a small, bounded information leak (one party's one sub-share becomes public) traded for the ability to resolve disputes without a trusted arbiter. A zero-knowledge alternative (Alt-listed under ADR-30's rejected column) avoids the leak but adds proof-generation cost disproportionate to the low frequency of real complaints.

Violation consequence. If complaint evidence were only checkable by a designated coordinator (rather than by every party independently), that coordinator becomes a single point of trust and failure — exactly the trusted-dealer assumption this whole system exists to remove. Applied in: Phase 8 (round_complaint.rs), §8.3.1 DKG Complaint Resolution Detail, ADR-30, §T12 threat (malicious participant in DKG complaint round).

7. System Invariants

Invariant Summary Table

ID Invariant Crate / Type How Detected Error / Telemetry Recovery
I1 reconstruct(any_t_shares) == original_secret tss-shamir::reconstruct Property test: 10 000 random (t,n,s); fuzz TssError::ReconstructionMismatch (test) Fix Lagrange loop; re-audit field ops
I2 Conditional distribution of secret given t-1 shares is uniform tss-shamir::split, tss-core RNG Statistical test: fix t-1 shares, vary secrets; assert distribution Panic in split if polynomial.degree() != t-1 Replace RNG; add degree assert
I3 polynomial.degree() == t-1; coeffs[0] == secret tss-shamir::Polynomial::new Unit test every split call TssError::InvalidPolynomialDegree Re-derive polynomial from ThresholdParams
I4 Lagrange at x=0 is exact (no overflow, correct mod p) tss-shamir Lagrange loop; tss-field Cross-check vs. known secret; fuzz extreme ShareId values (u32::MAX) TssError::ArithmeticOverflow (should be impossible if tss-field is correct) Verify field modulus; check denominator sign handling
I5 Share passes commit-verify iff consistent with dealer polynomial tss-vss::verify_share Unit: tamper share +1 → Err; fuzz random bit flips in share VssError::ShareVerificationFailed { share_id } Reject dealer; broadcast complaint; re-run DKG
I6 joint_pubkey == g^(sum qualified dealers' secrets); each share lies on joint poly tss-dkg::finalize Simulated DKG: reconstruct joint secret from t shares, check g^s == joint_pubkey DkgError::JointKeyMismatch; DkgError::DisqualifiedParty Restart DKG; exclude disqualified parties
I7 Aggregate of t valid partial sigs verifies under joint pubkey tss-schnorr::aggregate; tss-bls::aggregate RFC 9591 vectors (FROST); BLS known-answer tests SignError::AggregationFailed; SignError::VerificationFailed Identify invalid partial sig; re-sign with alternate subset
I8 Reshare/refresh produces valid new sharing of same secret tss-proactive::refresh; tss-proactive::reshare Pre/post reconstruct in test env; assert equal ProactiveError::SecretMismatch (test-only) Roll back to last good epoch; re-run refresh
I9 Secret-handling operations are constant-time tss-field, tss-shamir Lagrange, tss-group scalar mul dudect with 10^6 pairs; LLVM IR inspection CI failure: TimingLeakDetected { fn, t_value } Replace branch with subtle::ConditionallySelectable; re-run dudect
I10 Under-threshold / duplicate-id / failed-verify → hard error, never wrong result tss-shamir, tss-vss, tss-core, tss-protocol Unit test each error path; fuzz malformed inputs All error variants in §P10 table Surface error with context; never retry with same bad input
I11 No two protocol contexts ever hash identically-encoded bytes under the same domain label tss-group::hash_to_scalar, hash_to_point; tss-schnorr::binding; tss-protocol::transcript Static audit: every H(...) call site passes a distinct label constant; fuzz cross-context replay Compile error (no zero-argument hash helper exists) Add missing label constant; re-audit all call sites
I12 All honest parties compute an identical qualified set Q and identical joint public key Y after the DKG complaint round tss-dkg::round_complaint, tss-dkg::round_finalize Simulated DKG with one malicious dealer: assert all honest parties' Q and Y are bitwise equal DkgError::QualifiedSetMismatch (test-only; indicates a protocol bug, not a runtime path) Re-run complaint round from transcript; audit disqualification logic for non-determinism

I1 — Reconstruction Correctness (Expanded)

Statement. For any split(secret, t, n) and any subset S of t distinct valid shares: reconstruct(S) == secret exactly. The result is independent of which t-subset is chosen.

Where enforced. tss-shamir::reconstruct; the Lagrange loop over shares[0..t].

How detected.

  • Property test: 10,000 iterations, random secret ∈ F_p, random (t, n) with 1 ≤ t ≤ n ≤ 20, random t-subset selection, assert equality.
  • Conformance test: known (secret, shares) vectors derived by external reference (Python sympy over GF(p)).
  • Fuzz: random byte inputs as shares; assert no panic and correct Err returned for malformed inputs.

Telemetry. In test mode: ReconstructionMismatch { expected_hash, got_hash }. In production: reconstruct returns Ok(Scalar) or Err; no internal assertion.

Recovery strategy. If property test fails: bisect by fixing t and varying n; print polynomial coefficients (non-secret) to isolate Lagrange bug. Check tss-field mul and inv independently. Cross-check Lagrange coefficients against symbolic computation.

Debugging methodology. Evaluate the reconstructed polynomial at two additional points to confirm it matches the original shares; if it does not, the Lagrange loop has a denominator sign error or wrong modular reduction.

I2 — Perfect Secrecy Below Threshold (Expanded)

Statement. Any set of t-1 (or fewer) shares is statistically independent of the secret. For every value v ∈ F_p: Pr[secret = v | shares_{i_1}, …, shares_{i_{t-1}}] = 1/p.

Where enforced. tss-shamir::split — the polynomial sampling in tss-core CSPRNG. All t-1 Lagrange systems are underdetermined by exactly one degree of freedom (the constant term).

How detected. Statistical test (development): for a fixed t-1 share set and a fixed evaluating polynomial skeleton, enumerate 1000 distinct constant terms (secrets); verify that the conditional distribution of reconstructed candidates is uniform. Assert polynomial.coeffs.len() == t at every split call.

Telemetry. assert!(polynomial.coeffs.len() == t) in debug builds. Log CSPRNG source and seed type in test harness.

Recovery strategy. If coeffs.len() != t: the polynomial constructor is broken; fix Polynomial::new. If CSPRNG produces biased output: switch to OsRng or ChaCha20Rng seeded from OsRng.

I3 — Polynomial Degree (Expanded)

Statement. The sharing polynomial has degree exactly t-1. coeffs[0] is the secret. coeffs[1..t-1] are uniformly random in F_p.

Where enforced. tss-shamir::Polynomial::new(secret: Scalar, t: usize, rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> Polynomial.

How detected. Unit: poly.coeffs.len() == t. Unit: poly.coeffs[0] == secret. Unit: poly.evaluate(ShareId(0)) == secret.

Telemetry. TssError::InvalidPolynomialDegree { expected: t, got: coeffs.len() }.

I4 — Lagrange Exactness (Expanded)

Statement. Interpolation at x=0 over any t distinct ShareIds yields the true constant term with no rounding or modular error.

Where enforced. tss-shamir::lagrange_coeff(id: ShareId, ids: &[ShareId]) -> Scalar; field arithmetic in tss-field.

Key formula. lambda_i = product_{j != i} (0 - x_j) / (x_i - x_j) mod p.

Common pitfalls.

Pitfall Consequence Fix
Signed subtraction underflow before modular reduction Wrong denominator sign Reduce (a - b).rem_euclid(p) before multiply
u64 overflow in numerator product Silent wrap-around giving wrong Lagrange coeff Use Scalar mul which always reduces mod p
Denominator = 0 (duplicate ShareId) Division by zero / panic Check for duplicates before Lagrange (I10)

Telemetry. TssError::DuplicateShareId if denominator would be zero. Field ops are infallible (operate mod p).

I5 — VSS Soundness (Expanded)

Statement. A share s_i passes Feldman verification against commitments C iff s_i == f(i) exactly for the dealer's polynomial f. Any modification to s_i (by ±1, bit flip, replacement) causes verification failure.

Where enforced. tss-vss::feldman::verify_share(share: &Share, commitment: &Commitment) -> Result<(), VssError>.

Verification equation. g^{s_i} == product_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j^{i^j} over the EC group.

How detected.

  • Unit: split a secret, tamper share by +1, assert verify_share returns Err.
  • Fuzz: random bit flips in share byte encoding; assert no false positive.
  • DKG integration: every received sub-share is verified before being summed.

Telemetry. VssError::ShareVerificationFailed { share_id, dealer_id }.

Recovery. Broadcast complaint with (share_id, dealer_id, claimed_share, commitment). The dealer is disqualified if the complaint is valid. DKG continues without disqualified dealer if qualified_set.len() >= t.

I6 — DKG Correctness (Expanded)

Statement. After DKG finalization: joint_pubkey == g^{sum_d secret_d} over qualified dealers. party_i.share == sum_d f_d(i) where f_d is dealer d's polynomial. No party knows the joint secret.

Where enforced. tss-dkg::finalize(qualified_set, sub_shares, commitments) -> KeyShare.

How detected.

  • Simulate n-party DKG with known secrets; reconstruct joint secret from t shares; check g^{joint_secret} == joint_pubkey.
  • Verify each party's share lies on the joint polynomial by checking it against the summed commitments.

Telemetry. DkgError::JointKeyMismatch { expected, got }; DkgError::InsufficientQualifiedParties { have, need: t }.

Recovery. Restart DKG, excluding disqualified parties. Require at least t honest dealers to proceed.

I7 — Threshold-Signature Validity (Expanded)

Statement. For FROST: g^z == R * Y^c where c = H(R, Y, msg), z = sum(z_i), R = sum(D_i + rho_i*E_i). For BLS: e(sigma, g) == e(H(m), Y) where sigma = product sigma_i^{lambda_i}.

Where enforced. tss-schnorr::aggregate + tss-schnorr::verify; tss-bls::aggregate + tss-bls::verify.

Conformance test. Phase 14: run all RFC 9591 Appendix B vectors for FROST-Ed25519-SHA-512 and FROST-P-256-SHA-256. FROST round-1, round-2, aggregation, and verification must match exactly.

Partial-sig verification. Before aggregation, verify each z_i: g^{z_i} == D_i + rho_i*E_i + Y_i^c where Y_i = g^{share_i}. Failure → SignError::InvalidPartialSignature { signer_id } → identifiable abort.

Telemetry. SignError::AggregationFailed; SignError::VerificationFailed { scheme }.

I8 — Reshare/Refresh Invariance (Expanded)

Statement. After proactive refresh or additive reshare, the joint secret is unchanged. New shares form a valid t-of-n sharing of the same secret. Old shares are invalidated.

Where enforced. tss-proactive::refresh(parties, t, n) -> Vec<KeyShare>.

How detected.

  • Test: reconstruct secret from old shares, run refresh, reconstruct from new shares, assert equal.
  • Assert: sum of refresh polynomial constant terms == 0 (each party's refresh polynomial has constant term 0).

Telemetry. ProactiveError::ConstantTermNonZero { party_id } if any refresh polynomial has non-zero constant term (catches implementation bugs).

Recovery. If new shares yield wrong secret: roll back to last known-good epoch. Epoch number in transcript prevents replay of old-epoch refresh messages.

I9 — Constant-Time (Expanded)

Statement. All operations on secret data (share values, nonces, field inverses, Lagrange coefficients) execute in time independent of their inputs.

Enforcement points.

Function CT method
Scalar::invert Fixed exponent (p-2) square-and-multiply
lagrange_coeff subtle::Choice-based selection; no secret-dependent branches
Point::mul(scalar) Montgomery ladder with ConditionallySelectable point select
Share::ct_eq subtle::ConstantTimeEq
Polynomial zeroize volatile_set via zeroize crate

CI enforcement. dudect harness runs on tss-field::inv, lagrange_coeff, Point::mul with 10^6 input pairs in Phase 14. Threshold: t-value < 4.5. Block merge on failure.

Telemetry. CI output: timing_test: PASS | FAIL { fn, t_value, samples }.

Recovery. Replace variable-time branch with subtle::ConditionallySelectable::conditional_select. Run cargo asm to inspect generated assembly; look for jnz/jz with secret-dependent register.

I10 — Fail Closed on Inconsistency (Expanded)

Statement. Every inconsistency (under-threshold count, duplicate id, out-of-range value, failed verification) returns a typed error immediately. No function returns Ok(wrong_value).

Enforcement points.

Condition Checked in Error
t > n ThresholdParams::new TssError::InvalidParams
t == 0 ThresholdParams::new TssError::InvalidParams
ShareId(0) ShareId::new TssError::InvalidShareId
shares.len() < t reconstruct entry TssError::BelowThreshold
Duplicate ShareId reconstruct entry TssError::DuplicateShareId
Scalar >= p Scalar::from_bytes TssError::OutOfRangeScalar
Point not on curve Point::from_bytes GroupError::NotOnCurve
Point not in subgroup Point::from_bytes GroupError::NotInSubgroup
VSS verify fail tss-vss::verify_share VssError::ShareVerificationFailed
Partial sig invalid round-2 verify SignError::InvalidPartialSignature

Operational implication. tss-cli logs the full error variant + context (offending ShareId, party ID) to stderr. No silent swallow of errors in any match arm. All unwrap() calls are banned in non-test code (enforced by clippy::unwrap_used).

Recovery. Surface error to operator. Never retry with the same invalid input. For VSS/DKG failures, broadcast complaint and proceed without disqualified party if qualified_set.len() >= t.

I11 — Domain Separation Non-Collision (Expanded)

Statement. For any two distinct protocol contexts (Schnorr challenge, FROST binding factor, Pedersen generator derivation, BLS hash-to-curve, transcript append), the domain-separated hash inputs never collide: H(label_a || x) != H(label_b || y) is assumed safe whenever label_a != label_b, even if x == y.

Where enforced. tss-group/src/hash.rs — every public hash function requires an explicit domain: &[u8] parameter; §6 P11 registers the fixed label set.

How detected. Static audit: grep every H( / hash_to_scalar( / hash_to_point( call site in the workspace and confirm each supplies one of the registered labels from the P11 table; CI lint fails the build if a call site passes an empty or ad-hoc label string. Fuzzing additionally drives cross-context replay attempts (feeding one context's encoded bytes into another context's hash call) and asserts the outputs are never used interchangeably by any verification routine.

Recovery. If a new hash use site is added without a registered label, CI blocks the merge; the fix is to add the label to the P11 registry and re-derive any values computed before the fix (they used an unlabeled or incorrectly-labeled hash and cannot be trusted).

I12 — Qualified-Set Consistency (Expanded)

Statement. After the DKG complaint round (§8.3.1) completes, every honest party's locally computed qualified set Q is bitwise identical, and every honest party's derived joint public key Y = sum_{d in Q} C_{0,d} is bitwise identical.

Where enforced. tss-dkg/src/round_complaint.rs (qualified-set computation) and tss-dkg/src/round_finalize.rs (joint-key derivation); relies on P12 (every complaint is independently and deterministically verifiable from public data alone, so there is no path by which two honest parties evaluate the same complaint differently).

How detected. Simulated n-party DKG test with a deliberately malicious dealer (Phase 8 test suite): after the complaint round, assert Q is the same BTreeSet<ShareId> at every honest party, and assert Y is the same Point at every honest party. A second test seeds two independent lying complainants and confirms Q still converges identically everywhere.

Recovery. A mismatch here indicates the complaint-resolution logic is not a pure function of public transcript data (a P12 violation) — the fix is a code-level audit of round_complaint.rs for any branch that depends on non-broadcast local state, not a runtime recovery path (this must never occur outside a bug).

8. Domain Model

8.1 Core Entities and Their Relationships

ThresholdParams(t, n)
  └─ constrains ─► Polynomial (degree t-1, coeffs[0]=secret)
       └─ evaluated at ─► ShareId(1..n) ─► Share(id, value)
            └─ committed by ─► Commitment (Vec<Point>)  [Feldman/Pedersen]
Share ──────────────────────────────────────────────────►Lagrange Interpolation → secret at x=0

KeyShare(id, secret_share, public_share, group_public)
  └─ produced by ─► DKG (sum of qualified dealers' sub-shares)
  └─ used by ─► FROST signer (NonceCommitment → BindingFactor → PartialSignature)
  └─ used by ─► BLS signer (PartialSignature = H(m)^secret_share)

AggregateSignature(R, z)  [FROST] or AggregateSignature(sigma: Point) [BLS]
  └─ verified under ─► group_public (joint public key)

8.2 Type Taxonomy

Type Kind Field / Encoding Constraints
Scalar F_p element 32-byte big-endian 0 <= x < p; constant-time
FieldElement alias for Scalar in EC instantiation same same
Point compressed EC point 33 bytes (secp256k1 / Ed25519) must lie on curve; reject identity for pubkeys
ShareId(u32) evaluation point x_i u32 → Scalar non-zero; unique per party
Share {id, value} 4 + 32 bytes value in F_p; id != 0
Polynomial Vec<Scalar> of length t coeffs[0]=secret degree exactly t-1
Commitment Vec<Point> of length t t * 33 bytes Feldman: C_j = g^{a_j}
PedersenCommitment Vec<Point> of length t t * 33 bytes C_j = g^{a_j} h^{b_j}
KeyShare {id, secret_share, public_share, group_public} 4+32+33+33 bytes public_share = g^{secret_share}; consistent with group_public
NonceCommitment {D_i: Point, E_i: Point} 66 bytes fresh per round; never reused
BindingFactor rho_i = H(i, msg, commitments) Scalar deterministic; covers full commitment list
PartialSignature z_i: Scalar (FROST) or sigma_i: Point (BLS) 32 or 33 bytes verifiable before aggregation
AggregateSignature (R: Point, z: Scalar) or sigma: Point 65 or 33 bytes verifies with standard equation
DkgRound enum 4 variants 1-byte tag state machine transitions only forward
ThresholdParams {t, n} 8 bytes 1 <= t <= n <= 255

8.2.1 Invariant Mapping to Types

Invariant Type(s) Enforcing It Enforcement Mechanism
I1 — reconstruction correctness Polynomial, reconstruct() property test: reconstruct(split(s,t,n)) == s for all subsets
I2 — perfect secrecy below t Polynomial (random coeffs) statistical: t-1 shares -> uniform conditional distribution
I3 — degree exactly t-1 Polynomial::new(t) len(coeffs) == t enforced at construction
I4 — Lagrange exactness tss-field field ops no float arithmetic; all ops in F_p
I5 — VSS soundness Commitment, VSS verify commitment check at share receipt
I6 — DKG correctness DkgRound::Done, KeyShare group_public == sum(C_0_i) over qualified set
I7 — threshold sig validity AggregateSignature verify(sig, group_public, msg) in tss-schnorr / tss-bls
I8 — reshare invariance tss-proactive::refresh() property test: reconstruct(refreshed) == original
I9 — constant-time Scalar, tss-field subtle CT ops; dudect CI test
I10 — fail closed all entry points Result<T, Error>; no panic on invalid input

8.3 DKG State Machine

States: Init → Commit → Share → Complaint → Finalize → Done | Aborted

Transitions:
  Init        --[broadcast VSS commitments]-->    Commit
  Commit      --[all n commitments received]-->   Share
  Share       --[distribute encrypted shares]-->  Complaint
  Complaint   --[verify all shares; flag bad]--> Finalize  (qualified set agreed)
  Complaint   --[too few honest parties]-------> Aborted
  Finalize    --[sum sub-shares; derive key]---> Done

ASCII state table:

Current State Trigger Next State Side Effect
Init party broadcasts VSS commitment Commit store own Pedersen commitment
Commit all n commitments received Share send encrypted shares to each party
Share receive n-1 shares Complaint verify each share; queue complaints
Complaint broadcast complaint list Finalize disqualify accused if complaint valid
Complaint honest set < t Aborted error: insufficient qualified parties
Finalize all rounds settled Done compute KeyShare; derive group_public
Done — Done terminal; key is live
Aborted — Aborted terminal; restart required

8.3.1 DKG Complaint Resolution Detail

When party j sends a complaint against dealer i:

  • j reveals the blinding value r_ij from the Pedersen share pair (s_ij, r_ij)
  • All parties verify: g^{s_ij} * h^{r_ij} == prod_k C_k^{id_j^k}
  • If the equation fails: dealer i is disqualified; i is excluded from qualified set
  • If the equation holds: j is the liar; j is disqualified
  • Result: at most one of {i, j} joins the qualified set

Qualified set Q formed after complaint round:

  • Q = {parties that (a) broadcast a valid commitment, (b) sent valid shares to all recipients, (c) received no upheld complaint}
  • Finalization proceeds only if |Q| >= t; otherwise abort

8.4 FROST Two-Round Signing State Machine

States: Idle → Round1 → Round2 → Signed | SignAborted

  Idle      --[sign request; pick signing set S, |S|=t]-->  Round1
  Round1    --[each signer samples (d_i,e_i); publish (D_i,E_i)]-->  Round2
  Round2    --[compute rho_i, c, z_i; publish z_i]-->  Signed (if all valid)
  Round2    --[invalid partial sig detected]-->  SignAborted (identifiable abort)

Transition table:

State Actor Input Output Guard
Idle Coordinator sign(msg, signing_set) Round1 init msg
Round1 Each signer sign request NonceCommitment(D_i, E_i) fresh nonces only
Round2 Coordinator all D_i,E_i binding_factor list + c all n commitments present
Round2 Each signer binding factors + c PartialSignature z_i verify own NonceCommitment
Signed Coordinator all z_i valid AggregateSignature(R,z) partial verify passes for all i
SignAborted Coordinator z_i fails verify error(bad_signer: id) identifiable abort

8.4.1 Binding Factor Derivation (FROST, RFC 9591 §4.3)

commitment_list = encode_group_commitment_list(S, {(id_i, D_i, E_i) for i in S})
  -- sorted by id_i, big-endian; length-prefixed

for each i in S:
  rho_i = H1(i || commitment_list)    -- H1 = SHA-512 domain-separated
  -- rho_i binds party i's nonce to the full commitment set and signing group

R_i = D_i + rho_i * E_i               -- per-signer effective nonce point
R   = sum_{i in S} R_i                 -- group nonce

c   = H2(R || group_public || msg)     -- challenge scalar

This construction prevents the Drijvers et al. attack: an adversary cannot grind rho_i without knowing the full commitment set, which is fixed before any partial signature is computed.

8.4.2 Partial Signature Verification (identifiable abort)

Before aggregation, coordinator verifies each z_i:

for i in S:
  R_i_check = z_i * g - c * lambda_i * public_share_i - rho_i * (D_i, E_i terms)
  -- precise form per RFC 9591 sec. 5.3
  assert R_i_check == R_i     (fail → identifiable abort for party i)

Passing individual checks guarantees the aggregate will verify; skipping exposes the coordinator to a denial-of-service from a single malicious signer.

8.5 BLS Partial-Sign and Aggregate Sequence

Signing set S ⊆ parties, |S| = t:

  for i in S:
    sigma_i = H(msg)^{share_i}         -- partial sig (Point)
    verify: e(sigma_i, g) == e(H(msg), public_share_i)  -- optional pre-aggregate check

  Lagrange coefficients lambda_i over {x_j : j in S} at x=0 (exact field arithmetic)
  sigma = prod(sigma_i^{lambda_i})      -- aggregate in group (Lagrange exponent interpolation)

  Verify: e(sigma, g) == e(H(msg), group_public)

8.5.1 BLS Pairing Verification Detail

Pairing check: e(sigma, g2) == e(H(m), pk)
  where e: G1 x G2 -> GT (bilinear map over BLS12-381)
        sigma in G1 = aggregated BLS point
        g2 in G2 = fixed generator
        H(m) in G1 = hash-to-curve output
        pk in G2 = group_public (joint key)

Sub-group membership check required on all G1/G2 inputs before pairing. An invalid point (not in prime-order subgroup) gives wrong pairing output silently; always check.

8.6 Polynomial Evaluation and Lagrange Reconstruction

EBNF for the sharing procedure:

split(secret: Scalar, t: usize, n: usize) -> Vec<Share>
  coeffs[0] = secret
  coeffs[1..t-1] = sample_random_scalars(t-1)   // CSPRNG, zeroize after
  for id in 1..=n:
    share_value = horner_eval(coeffs, Scalar::from(id))  // f(x) = c0 + c1*x + ... + c_{t-1}*x^{t-1}
    shares.push(Share { id: ShareId(id), value: share_value })
  zeroize(coeffs)

reconstruct(shares: &[Share]) -> Result<Scalar>
  // require: shares.len() >= t; all ids distinct; each id != 0
  result = 0
  for i in shares:
    lambda_i = prod_{j != i} ( (0 - x_j) / (x_i - x_j) )  in F_p  // exact, constant-time
    result += lambda_i * share_i.value
  return result   // = f(0) = secret

Worked example (t=2, n=3):

Step Value
secret 42 (mod p)
random c_1 7
poly f(x) = 42 + 7x
Share(1) f(1) = 49
Share(2) f(2) = 56
Share(3) f(3) = 63
reconstruct(S1,S2) lambda_1=(0-2)/(1-2)=2; lambda_2=(0-1)/(2-1)=-1; 249-156=42
reconstruct(S1,S3) lambda_1=(0-3)/(1-3)=3/2; lambda_3=(0-1)/(3-1)=-1/2; = 42

Failure-path example — fewer than t shares:

Input Outcome
t=3, shares=[S1,S2] (only 2) Error::InsufficientShares(have=2, need=3)
duplicate ids [S1,S1,S3] Error::DuplicateShareId(id=1)
id=0 in share Error::ZeroShareId
value out of range Error::InvalidScalar
tampered share (fails VSS) Error::CommitmentMismatch(id=i)

8.6.1 Lagrange Coefficient Computation (constant-time)

lambda_i(S) = prod_{j in S, j != i} (0 - x_j) * inv(x_i - x_j)  mod p

CT requirements:
  - All multiplications: standard field mul (CT)
  - inv(x_i - x_j): Fermat: a^{p-2} mod p (CT exponentiation) or const-time ext-gcd
  - No early exit on zero difference (that case is an error, checked beforehand)
  - No branching on any coefficient value

Pitfall: if any x_i == x_j (duplicate ids), the denominator is zero. This MUST be detected before entering Lagrange computation and rejected with DuplicateShareId. Do not rely on field inverse returning zero for the zero input — reject explicitly.

8.6.2 Horner Evaluation

f(x) = c[0] + x*(c[1] + x*(c[2] + ... + x*c[t-1]))

Horner loop (degree t-1, n evaluations):
  for id_i in 1..=n:
    val = c[t-1]
    for j in (0..t-1).rev():
      val = val * x_i + c[j]   -- each mul/add: F_p constant-time
    shares[i] = Share { id: ShareId(id_i), value: val }

Cost: O(t) field ops per share; O(n*t) total. For t=10, n=20: 200 field multiplications.

8.7 Feldman VSS Layout

Dealer publishes: Commitment = [C_0, C_1, ..., C_{t-1}] where C_j = g^{a_j}

Recipient i verifies share s_i:
  lhs = g^{s_i}
  rhs = prod_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j^{(id_i)^j}   in the group
  assert lhs == rhs  (fail → CommitmentMismatch)

Wire layout (Feldman VSS message):

Offset Size Field
0 4 dealer_id (u32 LE)
4 4 t (u32 LE)
8 t*33 commitment points (compressed, ordered j=0..t-1)

8.8 Pedersen VSS Layout

Dealer publishes: PedersenCommitment = [C_0, ..., C_{t-1}] where C_j = g^{a_j} * h^{b_j}
Dealer sends to recipient i: (s_i, r_i) — main share and blinding share

Verify: g^{s_i} * h^{r_i} == prod_{j=0..t-1} C_j^{id_i^j}
Offset Size Field
0 4 dealer_id
4 4 t
8 t*33 commitment points
8+t*33 32 s_i (encrypted to recipient)
8+t*33+32 32 r_i (encrypted blinding)

8.9 DKG Round Message Taxonomy

Message Type Sender Broadcast/P2P Contents
DkgCommit each party broadcast party_id, Pedersen commitment (Vec<Point>)
DkgShare dealer_i → party_j P2P encrypted (s_ij, r_ij): sub-share pair
DkgComplaint party_j broadcast (accuser_id, accused_id, evidence: revealed r_ij)
DkgFinalize — internal — qualified_set, joint_pubkey, each party's KeyShare

8.10 FROST Round Message Taxonomy

Message Type Sender Contents Size
Round1Commit each signer (signer_id, D_i, E_i) 4+66 bytes
Round2PartialSig each signer (signer_id, z_i: Scalar) 4+32 bytes
AggregateMsg coordinator (R: Point, z: Scalar) 65 bytes

8.11 Transcript Binding

All public values are fed into a running transcript (Merlin/STROBE-based or SHA-3 sponge):

Append order Label Value
1 "protocol" b"tss-frost-v1"
2 "msg" message bytes
3 "group_key" group_public (33 bytes)
4 "nonce_commit_i" each (D_i, E_i) in id-sorted order
5 "binding_factor_i" rho_i = squeeze(id ‖ round4)
6 "R" aggregate nonce R
7 "challenge" c = H(R ‖ group_public ‖ msg)

Transcript prevents cross-round replay and binds all commitments to the message.

8.12 Wire/Serialization Format

All types serialized with canonical little-endian encoding (no padding, no versioning except explicit version byte at message header):

Message header (every network message):
  [0]     u8   version = 1
  [1]     u8   message_type (enum tag)
  [2..5]  u32  payload_len LE
  [6..]   bytes payload

Scalar: 32-byte LE; rejected if >= p. Point: 33-byte compressed SEC1. Vec<T>: u32-length-prefixed then elements. No floating-point fields. All fields fixed-size or length-prefixed.

8.12.1 Canonical Encoding Rules

Rule Detail
Scalar encoding 32-byte little-endian; reject if decoded value >= p
Point encoding 33-byte compressed SEC1 (X + parity bit); reject identity unless explicitly permitted
Length prefixes u32 LE; max 255 elements for Vec<Share> (n <= 255 enforced)
Strings u32-length-prefixed UTF-8; used only in CLI/transcript labels
Enums 1-byte discriminant; unknown discriminants return Error::UnknownMessageType
No implicit padding All fields pack to exact declared size
Version byte Every network message begins with version=1; future versions negotiated out-of-band

8.12.2 Transcript Domain Separation Labels

Label Used In Purpose
b"tss-frost-v1" FROST transcript init Binds transcript to protocol/version
b"msg" FROST challenge Prevents cross-protocol forgery
b"group_key" FROST challenge Binds signing key
b"nonce_commit" binding factor Covers full commitment list
b"challenge" FROST c Fiat-Shamir challenge
b"dkg-v1" DKG transcript Binds DKG round to protocol
b"vss-feldman-v1" VSS verify Domain separates VSS hash
b"vss-pedersen-v1" VSS verify Domain separates Pedersen VSS

8.13 Storage / Key-Share File Format

KeyShare file (tss-cli export): CBOR or binary sequential layout

  [0..3]   magic  = 0x54535348  ("TSSH")
  [4]      version = 1
  [5..8]   share_id (u32 LE)
  [9..40]  secret_share (Scalar, 32 bytes)
  [41..73] public_share (Point, 33 bytes)
  [74..106] group_public (Point, 33 bytes)
  [107..110] t (u32 LE)
  [111..114] n (u32 LE)
  [115..]  optional AEAD envelope (ChaCha20Poly1305, 12-byte nonce + tag)

Share files at rest: AEAD-encrypted with caller-supplied key; authenticated tag covers all fields.

8.13.1 Share File Access Semantics

  • Read: decrypt AEAD envelope, validate magic+version, deserialize fields, check public_share == g^secret_share
  • Write: serialize, AEAD-encrypt with caller key + random nonce, write atomically (write to .tmp, fsync, rename)
  • Share files must never be logged or printed; CLI uses Display trait that redacts secret_share to [redacted]
  • Multiple shares for the same group: distinct files distinguished by share_id; no combined file

8.14 Memory / Ownership Model

Resource Owner Drop/Zeroize
Polynomial.coeffs split() call frame zeroize on drop via zeroize crate
Share.value ShareHolder zeroize on drop
KeyShare.secret_share KeyHolder zeroize on drop
nonce scalars (d_i, e_i) FROST round1 frame zeroize immediately after computing z_i
sub-shares in DKG DkgState zeroize on finalize or abort
Transcript state signing session cleared on complete or abort

All secret types implement ZeroizeOnDrop (via zeroize crate derive). No Clone for secret types. Copy forbidden for Scalar/Share/KeyShare. Move semantics enforce single-owner.

8.14.1 Zeroization Sequence (FROST signing session lifecycle)

1. Round1: sample (d_i, e_i) from OsRng
2. Compute D_i = d_i*g, E_i = e_i*g; publish (D_i, E_i)
3. Round2: receive binding factors + challenge c
4. Compute z_i = d_i + rho_i*e_i + lambda_i*share_i*c
5. IMMEDIATELY after step 4: zeroize(d_i); zeroize(e_i)
   -- do NOT keep nonces alive past their use; leaked nonce reveals share_i
6. Publish z_i; clear z_i from memory after coordinator confirms receipt

Critical: nonce d_i must never be used for two different messages. Nonce reuse in Schnorr exposes the signing share via z_i - z_i' = d_i*(msg-msg') terms cancel, revealing share_i*c difference. The FROST transcript binds (D_i, E_i) to the specific message; reuse detection via transcript uniqueness.

8.15 Edge Cases and Failure Mode Catalogue

Scenario Detection Point Error Variant I/P Reference
t > n ThresholdParams::new InvalidParams I10, P10
t = 0 ThresholdParams::new InvalidParams I10
share id = 0 ShareId::new ZeroShareId I3, I10
duplicate share ids in reconstruct reconstruct() DuplicateShareId I4, I10
shares.len() < t reconstruct() InsufficientShares I2, I10
value >= p Scalar::from_bytes InvalidScalar I10
commitment length != t Feldman verify CommitmentLengthMismatch I5
share fails commitment check VSS verify CommitmentMismatch(id) I5, P2
DKG qualified set < t DkgState::finalize InsufficientHonestParties I6, P6
nonce reuse FROST round1 NonceReuse (detected via transcript) P5, P9
partial sig z_i fails verify FROST round2 BadPartialSig(id) I7, P6
pairing backend absent BLS sign PairingUnavailable Phase 11
message > 2^32 bytes sign() MessageTooLarge —
identity point in commitment VSS verify IdentityPoint I5
sub-group check failure Point::from_bytes NotOnCurve I10

8.16 Proactive Refresh Domain Model

Epoch-based refresh changes all shares without changing the secret:

Refresh protocol (parties P_1..P_n, current threshold t):
  Each party i:
    1. Sample zero-polynomial z_i(x): degree t-1, z_i(0) = 0, random higher coeffs
    2. Run Feldman VSS on z_i: publish commitments A_ij = g^{z_ij_coeff}
    3. Send sub-share z_i(x_k) to each party k
  Each party k:
    4. Verify received sub-shares against Feldman commitments
    5. New share: share_k' = share_k + sum_{i in qualified} z_i(x_k)
    6. New public: group_public unchanged (sum of zero-polys' constant terms = 0)
    7. Zeroize share_k and all z_i(x_k) sub-shares

Key properties:

  • share_k' interpolates to the same secret at x=0 (sum of zero-polys has zero constant term)
  • An adversary who corrupted t-1 parties in epoch e and t-1 different parties in epoch e+1 still cannot reconstruct the secret (no overlap in epoch, sub-threshold each)
  • I8 invariant: reconstruct(new_shares) == secret

Lost share recovery:

  • Party j missing its share can receive t valid shares from honest parties
  • Honest parties interpolate a sub-polynomial sharing of share_j over a fresh t-of-t scheme
  • j assembles t sub-shares, sums them to recover share_j
  • Recovery requires t willing parties; they reveal nothing about the secret (only about share_j, which j already holds legitimately)

8.17 Worked Failure Path: Malicious Dealer in DKG

Scenario: party 2 sends inconsistent shares (s_21 is wrong for party 1, others correct).

Step Action Outcome
DkgCommit Party 2 broadcasts valid commitment C_2 All parties store C_2
DkgShare Party 2 sends s_21 that fails: g^{s_21} != prod C_2j^{id_1^j} Party 1 detects mismatch
DkgComplaint Party 1 broadcasts complaint(accuser=1, accused=2, evidence=r_21) All parties verify
Verification g^{s_21}*h^{r_21} != prod C_2j^{id_1^j} Complaint upheld
Finalize Party 2 excluded from qualified set Q Q = {1,3,4,...,n} (if
Done Each party's share = sum of sub-shares from Q only Party 2's contribution excluded

If |Q| < t after complaint round: protocol aborts with InsufficientHonestParties; all parties discard state and restart.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable, 2021 edition) C, Go, Python Memory safety without GC; zeroize crate; constant-time abstractions; no_std feasibility C wins in hardware-constrained environments (bare-metal, FPGA side-channel ASICs) where Rust toolchain is unavailable; Go wins for rapid prototype in a team with no Rust expertise
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace; Makefile for CI shortcuts Bazel, CMake Cargo native to Rust; workspace unifies 11 crates; no polyglot overhead Bazel wins in a mono-repo mixing Rust with C/C++/Go where hermetic remote caching across languages is required
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal; each dep audited with cargo-deny; lock file committed permissive transitive Cryptographic code: each dep is an attack surface; explicit allow-list Permissive transitives win in rapid-development internal tooling where audit cost exceeds the attack-surface risk
ADR-04 Prime-field arithmetic crate ff crate (arkworks-style) or custom constant-time u64x4 num-bigint, rug num-bigint is not constant-time; ff provides trait abstractions; fallback: hand-rolled Montgomery num-bigint wins for a research/scripting context where side-channels are not a threat and arbitrary precision is needed (e.g., toy RSA experiments)
ADR-05 Elliptic-curve group curve25519-dalek (Ed25519/Ristretto255) as primary; secp256k1 as secondary k256, p256 alone Portfolio alignment with #90; Ristretto255 avoids cofactor issues; dalek is well-audited k256/p256 win when NIST-curve compliance is a regulatory requirement (FIPS 140-3, CNSA 2.0) or when integrating with NIST-only HSMs
ADR-06 Constant-time guarantees subtle crate (conditional selection, CT comparisons) + zeroize manual masking subtle provides ConstantTimeEq, ConditionallySelectable; compiler-proof barriers Manual masking wins on microcontrollers lacking a Rust std/subtle port, or when formal verification (e.g., ct-verif) of the masking pattern is required and the tool cannot reason about the subtle crate
ADR-07 Serialization format Custom binary (fixed-size fields, LE encoding) for wire; CBOR (via ciborium) for file storage protobuf, JSON, MessagePack Deterministic; no schema versioning cost for wire; CBOR self-describing for files protobuf wins in a large organization where multiple teams maintain independent schema definitions and a schema registry is already in place; JSON wins for human-readable debug tooling
ADR-08 AEAD for key-share encryption at rest ChaCha20Poly1305 (chacha20poly1305 crate) AES-GCM, XSalsa20 Software-only constant-time; no hardware dependency; nonce = random 12 bytes AES-GCM wins when hardware AES-NI acceleration is available and throughput-at-scale matters (> 1 GB/s bulk encryption); XSalsa20 wins for deterministic nonces on systems without reliable randomness
ADR-09 Hash function / transcript SHA-3 (sha3 crate) for H(m) in BLS; SHA-512 for FROST challenge; Merlin transcript for binding BLAKE3, SHA-256 FROST RFC 9591 specifies SHA-512 challenge; SHA-3 for BLS hash-to-curve; Merlin for domain separation BLAKE3 wins for bulk-data hashing throughput where latency (not security strength) dominates and the protocol does not require SHA-512 conformance
ADR-10 Hash-to-curve (BLS) hash2curve (draft-irtf-cfrg-hash-to-curve); H(m) mapped to G1 ad-hoc try-and-increment RFC-compliant; avoids timing side-channel in try-and-increment Try-and-increment wins in closed research environments where RFC conformance is not required, the operation is non-secret, and implementation simplicity is the priority
ADR-11 Pairing backend (BLS) bls12_381 crate (optional feature pairing) mcl, blst, bn254 bls12_381 audited; optional feature gate keeps tss-bls compilable without pairing for stub mode blst wins when pairing throughput is the bottleneck (blst is 2–3× faster than bls12_381 in native C/assembly); bn254 wins when Ethereum L2 compatibility requires the BN254 pairing specifically
ADR-12 Random number generation rand_core::OsRng + getrandom thread_rng, custom RNG OS-provided entropy; no seeded PRNG for secrets; getrandom for no_std A deterministic seeded RNG (ChaCha20Rng) wins in test/simulation contexts requiring exact replay; never acceptable for production key material
ADR-13 Zeroization zeroize crate; ZeroizeOnDrop derive on all secret types manual memset Prevents compiler from optimizing away secret wipes; standard in Rust crypto ecosystem Manual memset + compiler barrier wins when the zeroize crate's MSRV is incompatible with the target platform or when a formal audit demands a verifiable, non-macro approach
ADR-14 Async / concurrency model Synchronous library core; std::thread for CLI simulation; no async runtime in core crates tokio, rayon Crypto primitives are CPU-bound; async adds complexity without throughput benefit; simulation uses threads Tokio wins when the library is embedded into an existing async application that must not block its executor; rayon wins for embarrassingly parallel batch signing (many independent sessions)
ADR-15 Testing framework cargo test (unit + integration); proptest for property-based; criterion for benchmarks quickcheck, nextest only proptest shrinking; criterion wall-clock + throughput; nextest as optional runner quickcheck wins in codebases where the simpler API reduces barrier to writing property tests; nextest alone wins in CI environments with strict per-test timeout enforcement
ADR-16 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targeting: fuzz_share, fuzz_reconstruct, fuzz_vss_verify, fuzz_frost_partial, fuzz_dkg_message AFL, honggfuzz as primary libFuzzer integrates with cargo-fuzz; coverage-guided; LLVM sanitizers for UB AFL++ wins for structured grammar-aware fuzzing where custom mutators are needed that libFuzzer cannot express; honggfuzz wins for hardware performance counter-based feedback on AMD CPUs
ADR-17 Property testing scope Field axioms (add/mul/inv); reconstruct(split(s)) == s over random (t,n,subset); VSS soundness (tampered share fails); reshare invariance manual parametric Automated discovery of edge cases; shrinking for minimal counterexample Manual parametric wins when the property space is fully enumerable (small n) and the overhead of a framework exceeds the benefit — e.g., t,n <= 5
ADR-18 FROST conformance RFC 9591 test vectors in tss-schnorr/tests/rfc9591.rs; fail CI on any mismatch cross-implementation differential RFC vectors are normative; CI gate prevents silent divergence Cross-implementation differential wins when interop with a live external implementation (e.g., ZcashFoundation/frost) is the primary goal and no RFC vectors exist for the specific ciphersuite
ADR-19 Logging tracing crate (structured spans per protocol round) log, slog Async-compatible; span hierarchy matches round structure; no secret values in spans log wins in embedded no_std deployments where tracing's runtime overhead and proc-macro dependencies are unacceptable
ADR-20 Metrics metrics crate facade; optional metrics-exporter-prometheus manual counters, statsd Facade decouples core from exporter; key counters: split_ops, dkg_rounds, sign_ops, abort_count Manual counters win in a deployment where the operator controls every binary dependency and wants zero runtime overhead from a metrics crate
ADR-21 CI/CD GitHub Actions; cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy -- -D warnings, cargo audit, cargo-deny check, fuzz nightly Jenkins, CircleCI Standard Rust CI; cargo audit for CVE check; cargo-deny enforces dependency allow-list Jenkins wins in air-gapped enterprise environments without GitHub access; CircleCI wins when advanced parallelism features (test splitting by timing data) are needed at scale
ADR-22 Benchmarking criterion with throughput; benchmarks in benches/; compare splits/s and sign/s across (t,n) divan, iai criterion wall-time + throughput plots; regression CI gate via critcmp iai (instruction-count based) wins in CI where wall-clock instability produces noisy regressions and cycle-exact measurements are preferred; divan wins for simpler API in new projects
ADR-23 Workspace layout Cargo workspace root + 11 member crates (see §10 Repo Layout); single Cargo.lock monolith, multiple repos Atomic version locking; single cargo test --workspace; clear dependency DAG between crates Multiple repos win when crates need independent release cycles, separate security audit scopes, or separate dependency graphs (e.g., tss-bls requires pairing, others do not)
ADR-24 no_std compatibility tss-core, tss-field, tss-shamir, tss-vss are no_std + alloc; higher crates opt into std full std everywhere Enables embedded targets; alloc sufficient for Vec-based types Full std wins when embedded deployment is never a requirement and the engineering cost of maintaining no_std compatibility outweighs the benefit
ADR-25 Licensing Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual) GPL, BSL Maximizes ecosystem compatibility; matches dalek + arkworks conventions GPL wins when the project's purpose is exclusively open-source and copyleft enforcement is desired to prevent proprietary forks
ADR-26 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; rust-toolchain.toml pinning stable channel; Docker image for CI with pinned toolchain floating toolchain Bit-for-bit determinism for audit; eliminates "works on my machine" Floating toolchain wins in early-prototype phase where always-latest-stable is desired and build reproducibility is not yet a security requirement
ADR-27 Constant-time audit tooling dudect test harness for Lagrange coefficient and scalar-mul paths; manual review of any branch on secret TIMECOP, Valgrind alone dudect statistical timing test detectable in CI; complementary to code review TIMECOP wins when a formal binary-level information-flow analysis (rather than statistical inference) is required by a security certification body
ADR-28 Error handling thiserror-derived enums per crate; no panics on malformed input in library code; ? propagation anyhow in library code thiserror structured; library callers can match variants; anyhow only in tss-cli anyhow wins in application-level code (final binaries, not libraries) where rich contextual error chains matter more than matchable error variants
ADR-29 Packaging / distribution cargo install tss-cli; optional Docker image for CLI; library crates published to crates.io pre-built binaries only crates.io source-based; Docker for reproducible CLI usage in CI pipelines Pre-built binaries win when users cannot run a Rust toolchain (air-gapped ops environments) and reproducible binaries are signed and delivered via a secure artifact store
ADR-30 Identifiable abort evidence Complaint message includes revealed Pedersen blinding value r_ij; verifier re-derives commitment to check zero-knowledge proof of misbehavior Simple reveal is sufficient for VSS complaint; avoids ZKP complexity; see I10, P6 ZKP-based misbehavior proofs win when evidence must not reveal ANY additional information about the blinding value (e.g., in post-quantum contexts where the Pedersen blinding carries long-term secrets)
