11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types below are normative. Downstream phases implement exactly these names and shapes. All field types reside in tss-field or tss-core; group types in tss-group. Types are #[derive(Clone)] unless noted as non-Clone for zeroize reasons.

11.1 tss-field — Scalar and FieldElement

Scalar

/// Element of the prime field F_p (curve scalar field).
/// Invariant: value is always reduced mod p; no negative representation.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct Scalar(/* [u8; 32] canonical little-endian */);

impl Scalar {
    pub fn zero() -> Self;
    pub fn one() -> Self;
    pub fn from_bytes_mod_order(bytes: &[u8; 64]) -> Self;   // wide reduction
    pub fn from_canonical_bytes(bytes: &[u8; 32]) -> Option<Self>;
    pub fn to_bytes(&self) -> [u8; 32];                       // canonical encoding
    pub fn invert(&self) -> CtOption<Self>;                   // Fermat: self^(p-2)
    pub fn is_zero(&self) -> Choice;                          // constant-time
}
impl Add<Scalar> for Scalar { type Output = Scalar; /* ... */ }
impl Mul<Scalar> for Scalar { type Output = Scalar; /* ... */ }
impl Neg for Scalar    { type Output = Scalar; }
impl Sub<Scalar> for Scalar { type Output = Scalar; }
  • Ownership: Copy; stack-allocated 32 bytes; no heap.
  • Mutation: immutable after construction; all ops produce new values.
  • Concurrency: Send + Sync (plain bytes, no interior mutability).
  • Persistence: canonical 32-byte little-endian; same format as RFC 8032.
  • Zeroize: ZeroizeOnDrop; call zeroize() explicitly after nonce/share use.
  • Compatibility: encoding matches curve25519-dalek Scalar; interoperable.

FieldElement

/// Alias — in the EC instantiation the secret-sharing field equals the curve scalar field.
pub type FieldElement = Scalar;

Rationale: ADR 0001 — using a separate large prime would require a distinct modular-reduction path; the curve scalar field suffices for all Shamir operations.

11.2 tss-core — Identity, Share, Params, Errors

ShareId

/// Non-zero evaluation point x_i in F_p identifying one participant.
/// x=0 is reserved: it is the secret (the polynomial constant term).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct ShareId(pub(crate) u32);   // 1-indexed; 0 is invalid

impl ShareId {
    pub fn new(v: u32) -> Result<Self, TssError>;   // rejects 0
    pub fn as_scalar(&self) -> Scalar;               // embed u32 into F_p
}
  • Ownership: Copy.
  • Mutation: immutable; created once per party and reused across rounds.
  • Concurrency: Send + Sync.
  • Persistence: serialized as 4-byte big-endian u32; zero is an encoding error.
  • Compatibility: stable across reshares; a party keeps its id across epoch boundaries unless explicitly re-keyed.

Share

/// One party's evaluation f(id) of the sharing polynomial.
/// MUST be zeroized after reconstruction or resharing.
#[derive(Clone, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct Share {
    pub id: ShareId,
    pub value: Scalar,   // f(id); secret portion — never log or serialize unencrypted
}
  • Ownership: heap-free (two stack values); Clone is explicit — avoid accidental copies.
  • Mutation: value is write-once after split; resharing creates a new Share.
  • Concurrency: not Copy to prevent silent duplicates; wrap in Arc<Mutex<>> if shared across threads.
  • Persistence: serialized as id ‖ value (4 + 32 bytes); must be encrypted at rest.
  • Zeroize: ZeroizeOnDrop; caller must call zeroize() before passing to untrusted code.
  • Compatibility: wire format versioned in tss-protocol; Share::to_bytes / Share::from_bytes are stable.

ThresholdParams

/// Threshold policy: t-of-n.
/// Invariants: 1 <= t <= n; n >= 1.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ThresholdParams {
    pub t: usize,   // reconstruction threshold; polynomial degree = t-1
    pub n: usize,   // total number of shares / parties
}

impl ThresholdParams {
    pub fn new(t: usize, n: usize) -> Result<Self, TssError>;
    pub fn degree(&self) -> usize;   // = t - 1
    pub fn is_sufficient(&self, share_count: usize) -> bool;
}
  • Ownership: Copy.
  • Mutation: immutable; a reshare with new params creates a new ThresholdParams.
  • Persistence: serialized as two 8-byte big-endian usize (or u32 for compact wire encoding).
  • Compatibility: changing t/n requires a reshare ceremony; old and new params are recorded in BLUEPRINT_LOG.md.

TssError

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum TssError {
    InvalidThreshold { t: usize, n: usize },
    InvalidShareId(u32),              // id = 0
    DuplicateShareId(ShareId),
    InsufficientShares { have: usize, need: usize },
    VerificationFailed { party: ShareId },
    InvalidScalar,                    // non-canonical bytes
    InvalidPoint,                     // not on curve / not in prime-order subgroup
    DkgComplaint { accuser: ShareId, accused: ShareId },
    DkgInsufficientQualified { qualified: usize, threshold: usize },
    PartialSignatureInvalid { party: ShareId },
    AbortWithEvidence(Vec<ShareId>),  // identifiable-abort: list of misbehaving parties
    SerializationError(String),
    InternalError(&'static str),      // invariant violation; always a bug
}
  • Invariant: InternalError variants indicate I1–I10 violations; callers treat them as fatal.
  • Concurrency: Send + Sync; no Rc or raw pointers.
  • Serialization: Display impl for human-readable logs; no serde by default (avoid leaking context in serialized errors).

DkgRound

/// State machine states for the distributed key generation protocol.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub enum DkgRound {
    Commit,      // Round 1: broadcast VSS commitments
    Share,       // Round 2: send encrypted sub-shares point-to-point
    Complaint,   // Round 3: broadcast complaints; disqualify bad dealers
    Finalize,    // Round 4: aggregate qualified shares; derive joint key
}
  • Transitions: Commit → Share → Complaint → Finalize; reverse transition is an InternalError.
  • Persistence: round state is checkpointed in tss-protocol transcript before advancing.

11.3 tss-group — Point and Commitment

Point

/// Prime-order elliptic-curve group element (Ristretto255 over Ed25519 field).
/// Commitments and public keys are Points.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Zeroize)]
pub struct Point(/* RistrettoPoint */);

impl Point {
    pub fn identity() -> Self;
    pub fn generator() -> Self;                          // canonical G
    pub fn second_generator() -> Self;                   // H; NUMS, independent of G
    pub fn compress(&self) -> [u8; 32];                  // canonical encoding
    pub fn decompress(bytes: &[u8; 32]) -> Option<Self>; // None if not on curve
    pub fn is_identity(&self) -> Choice;
}
impl Add<Point> for Point    { type Output = Point; }
impl Neg for Point           { type Output = Point; }
impl Sub<Point> for Point    { type Output = Point; }
impl Mul<Scalar> for Point   { type Output = Point; }   // constant-time scalar mul
  • Ownership: Copy (32-byte compressed or 64-byte internal; copy is safe).
  • Mutation: immutable; ops produce new Points.
  • Concurrency: Send + Sync.
  • Persistence: canonical 32-byte Ristretto255 compressed encoding; reject non-canonical on deserialization.
  • Compatibility: encoding matches ristretto255 IETF draft; interoperable with portfolio #90.

Commitment

/// Feldman VSS commitment: vector of Points g^{a_j} for j in 0..t.
/// Index 0 is the commitment to the secret (g^{a_0} = g^{secret}).
pub type Commitment = Vec<Point>;
  • Length invariant: commitment.len() == params.t (degree t-1 polynomial has t coefficients).
  • Persistence: serialized as [u32 length][Point; t]; length must match ThresholdParams.t at deserialization.
  • Security note: Commitment[0] leaks g^secret; not suitable where hiding is required (use PedersenCommitment).

PedersenCommitment

/// Pedersen VSS commitment: vector of Points g^{a_j} h^{b_j} for j in 0..t.
/// Perfectly hiding (given discrete-log hardness of G, H).
pub type PedersenCommitment = Vec<Point>;
  • Invariant: produced only with a companion blinding polynomial; standalone PedersenCommitment without the blinding shares is computationally binding but reveals nothing about coefficients.

11.4 tss-shamir — Polynomial

Polynomial

/// Degree-(t-1) polynomial over F_p with coeffs[0] = secret.
/// Drop impl calls zeroize — do not clone or persist.
#[derive(Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct Polynomial {
    pub(crate) coeffs: Vec<Scalar>,  // coeffs[0] = f(0) = secret; len = t
}

impl Polynomial {
    pub fn new(secret: Scalar, degree: usize, rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> Self;
    pub fn eval(&self, x: &Scalar) -> Scalar;     // Horner; constant-time
    pub fn degree(&self) -> usize;                 // coeffs.len() - 1
}
  • Ownership: heap Vec; non-Copy; must not be cloned.
  • Mutation: coeffs set at construction; no post-init mutation.
  • Zeroize: ZeroizeOnDrop; Drop calls zeroize(); explicit call required before any unsafe move.
  • Concurrency: not Send (contains secret material; caller must not share across threads without a mutex and explicit zeroize discipline).
  • Persistence: never serialized; if a Polynomial is serialized it is a security defect.

11.5 tss-dkg — KeyShare

KeyShare

/// A party's long-term key material output from a DKG ceremony.
/// Persisted encrypted; never reconstructed into one place.
#[derive(Clone, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct KeyShare {
    pub id: ShareId,
    pub secret_share: Scalar,     // f(id) on the joint polynomial; MUST be zeroized after use
    pub public_share: Point,      // g^{secret_share}; used for partial-sig verification
    pub group_public: Point,      // joint public key Y = g^{joint_secret}
    pub params: ThresholdParams,
    pub epoch: u64,               // incremented on each proactive refresh
}
  • Ownership: non-Copy; Clone is available but discouraged; prefer moving.
  • Mutation: epoch incremented on refresh; secret_share replaced with new epoch value.
  • Concurrency: wrap in Arc<RwLock<>> for multi-threaded signers; lock held only during signing round.
  • Persistence: serialize as id ‖ public_share ‖ group_public ‖ params ‖ epoch ‖ encrypted(secret_share); secret_share encrypted with a KDF-derived key before any I/O.
  • Compatibility: epoch field allows proactive-refresh history; old epoch key shares are invalid after refresh.

11.6 tss-schnorr — Nonce and Signature Types

NonceCommitment

/// FROST per-round nonce commitment pair published in Round 1.
/// (D_i, E_i) = (g^d_i, g^e_i) where d_i, e_i are ephemeral nonces.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct NonceCommitment {
    pub hiding:  Point,   // D_i = g^{d_i}
    pub binding: Point,   // E_i = g^{e_i}
    pub id: ShareId,
}
  • Lifetime: single signing round; must be deleted after Round 2 completes.
  • Persistence: broadcast over the network; not stored long-term.

BindingFactor

/// rho_i = H("frost-binding", i, msg, commitment_list)
/// Derived deterministically per signer per round; never stored.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct BindingFactor(pub(crate) Scalar);
  • Invariant: recomputed from public data; must match across all aggregators.

PartialSignature

/// A signer's contribution to a threshold signature.
/// For FROST: z_i = d_i + rho_i*e_i + lambda_i*share_i*c
/// For BLS: sigma_i = H(m)^{share_i}
#[derive(Clone, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct PartialSignature {
    pub id: ShareId,
    pub value: PartialSigValue,
}

#[derive(Clone, Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub enum PartialSigValue {
    Schnorr(Scalar),  // z_i scalar
    Bls(Point),       // sigma_i group element
}
  • Verification: each partial signature is verified against the signer's public_share before aggregation (P6, I7).
  • Persistence: ephemeral; transmitted in Round 2; zeroized after aggregation.

AggregateSignature

/// Final threshold signature valid under the joint public key.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct AggregateSignature {
    pub variant: SigVariant,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SigVariant {
    Frost { R: Point, z: Scalar },   // standard (R,z) Schnorr signature
    Bls { sigma: Point },            // aggregated BLS point
}
  • Verifiability: verify(sig, group_public, msg) uses the same equation as single-signer Schnorr/BLS; no knowledge of threshold required by verifiers (P8).
  • Persistence: 64 bytes (FROST) or 48 bytes compressed G1 (BLS); canonical encoding.
  • Compatibility: FROST signature is compatible with standard Ed25519/Schnorr verifiers when using Ed25519 curve parameters.

11.7 Cross-Cutting Type Properties Summary

Type Crate Copy Zeroize Send Sync Serialized
Scalar / FieldElement tss-field yes yes yes yes 32 B LE
ShareId tss-core yes no yes yes 4 B BE
Share tss-core no yes yes no* 36 B encrypted
ThresholdParams tss-core yes no yes yes 8 B
TssError tss-core no no yes yes Display only
DkgRound tss-core yes no yes yes 1 B enum
Point tss-group yes yes yes yes 32 B compressed
Commitment tss-vss no no yes yes 4+32t B
PedersenCommitment tss-vss no no yes yes 4+32t B
Polynomial tss-shamir no yes no no never
KeyShare tss-dkg no yes yes no* encrypted
NonceCommitment tss-schnorr yes no yes yes 65 B
BindingFactor tss-schnorr yes yes yes yes ephemeral
PartialSignature tss-schnorr no yes yes no* 32 or 32 B
AggregateSignature tss-schnorr yes no yes yes 64/48 B

* Wrap in Mutex/RwLock for concurrent access; do not implement Sync directly.

11.8 Serialization Contract

  • All serializations are big-endian for integer fields, canonical little-endian for scalars (matching RFC 8032 / Ristretto255 spec).
  • No serde derives on secret-bearing types by default; serialization is explicit via to_bytes() / from_bytes().
  • Version byte prepended to all wire messages in tss-protocol; current version = 0x01.
  • Commitment vectors prefixed with a u32 length; mismatch vs ThresholdParams.t is TssError::SerializationError.
  • KeyShare encrypted at rest: version ‖ id ‖ public_share ‖ group_public ‖ params ‖ epoch ‖ AEAD(secret_share); AEAD = ChaCha20-Poly1305 with a KDF-derived key.

Phase 1 — Core Types + Prime-Field Arithmetic

1.1 Objectives

  • Establish tss-core and tss-field as the foundation crates; all other crates depend on them.
  • Define canonical types: Scalar, FieldElement, ShareId, Share, Polynomial, ThresholdParams.
  • Implement constant-time F_p: add, sub, mul, inv (Fermat's little theorem or extended-GCD); enforce I9.
  • Provide a secure CSPRNG wrapper (SecureRng) that sources from OsRng; expose sample_scalar().
  • Enforce ThresholdParams validity: reject t=0, t>n, n=0 at construction time (I10, P10).
  • Property-test field axioms: associativity, commutativity, identity, inverse, Fermat's identity.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P1/I1/ADR-01/ADR-06 — workspace skeleton; tss-core type definitions; zeroize crate ADR-02; B3/P15

tss-core/src/lib.rs — public re-exports; no business logic.

tss-core/src/types.rs — type definitions (P1/I1/ADR-01):

pub struct Scalar([u8; 32]);            // opaque bytes, little-endian canonical form
pub type FieldElement = Scalar;
pub struct ShareId(pub u32);            // invariant: non-zero
pub struct Share { pub id: ShareId, pub value: Scalar }
pub struct Polynomial { pub coeffs: Vec<Scalar> }   // degree = coeffs.len()-1
pub struct ThresholdParams { pub t: usize, pub n: usize }

tss-core/src/error.rs — TssError enum (P10/I10/ADR-28): InvalidThreshold, InvalidShareId, DuplicateShareId, UnderThreshold, VerificationFailed, InsufficientShares, PairingUnavailable.

tss-core/src/params.rs — ThresholdParams::new(t,n) (I10/P1): returns Err(InvalidThreshold) if t==0 || t>n || n==0.

tss-field/src/lib.rs — F_p arithmetic over the curve scalar field prime p (B3/P15/I9):

  • fp_add(a,b), fp_sub(a,b), fp_mul(a,b) — 256-bit Barrett or Montgomery reduction; constant-time (P4/ADR-06).
  • fp_inv(a) — Fermat: a^(p-2) mod p via square-and-always-multiply (B3 Ch.4); or constant-time ext-GCD (Bernstein-Yang). Constant-time required by I9.
  • fp_neg(a) — p - a if a != 0 else 0.
  • fp_pow(a, e) — constant-time square-and-always-multiply for arbitrary exponents (P4).

tss-field/src/rng.rs — SecureRng wrapping rand::rngs::OsRng; sample_scalar() -> Scalar — rejection-sample until 0 < x < p.

tss-core/src/zeroize.rs — impl Drop for Scalar: calls zeroize::Zeroize::zeroize on the inner array; derive ZeroizeOnDrop via the zeroize crate.

tss-field/tests/field_axioms.rs — proptest/quickcheck strategies: arb_scalar() samples field elements; checks:

  • (a+b)+c == a+(b+c) (add assoc); a+b == b+a; a+0 == a; a+(-a) == 0.
  • (a*b)*c == a*(b*c); a*b == b*a; a*1 == a; a*(b+c) == a*b+a*c.
  • a != 0 => a * inv(a) == 1; inv(1) == 1.
  • Fermat: a^(p-1) == 1 mod p for 1000 random a.

Cargo.toml constraints: zeroize = { version="1", features=["derive"] }, rand = "0.8", subtle = "2" (for ct_eq, ConditionallySelectable).

1.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p tss-core -p tss-field passes with zero failures.
  • Property tests exercise 1000+ random field element triples per axiom.
  • Scalar drops zero its memory on scope exit (verified by valgrind/miri).
  • ThresholdParams::new(0,5), new(6,5) both return Err(InvalidThreshold).

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
All unit + property tests pass 100%
fp_inv timing variance (with constant-time impl) < 1% relative stddev over 10k samples
sample_scalar output distribution (chi-squared) p-value > 0.05 over 10k samples
Scalar zeroization under Miri no use-after-free, no secret in heap
ThresholdParams rejects invalid params Err returned, no panic
Field axiom property tests 1000 iterations each, zero failures

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Key checks
fixtures/field/fp_inv_vectors.json Known (a, inv_a) pairs for p=secp256k1 and p=Ed25519 Exact match
fixtures/field/fp_mul_vectors.json (a, b, a*b mod p) — 50 pairs Exact match
fixtures/field/fermat_check.json 100 random scalars; expected a^(p-1) = 1 Boolean pass
fixtures/core/threshold_reject.json (t,n) pairs expected to fail construction All return Err
fixtures/field/inv_zero.json fp_inv(0) — expected: Err(InvalidShareId) or defined as 0 Error or 0, never UB

Malformed: scalar bytes with value >= p (must reduce or reject); zero scalar inversion.

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Non-constant-time branch on fp_inv Side-channel leaks scalar if x==0 guard before Fermat Timing histogram bimodal
Rejection-sample loop termination Infinite loop if RNG broken OsRng failing on some platforms loop_count metric > 1000
Scalar bytes not reduced mod p Silent wrap-around in arithmetic Deserialize without range check Failing axiom tests
Polynomial coeffs not zeroized Secret lingers in heap Missing ZeroizeOnDrop on Vec<Scalar> Miri detects use-after-drop
ThresholdParams t==n silently accepted n-of-n is degenerate (no fault tolerance) Missing semantic validation Warn at construction if t==n
Overflow in fp_add intermediate Incorrect reduction 256-bit carry not propagated Axiom test a+b != expected

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • fp_inv dominates cost per share verification; constant-time ext-GCD (Bernstein-Yang) is ~3x faster than Fermat square-and-multiply on 256-bit fields — prefer it.
  • sample_scalar rejection probability < 2^-128 for 256-bit fields; amortized cost is one OsRng call.
  • Avoid heap allocation inside fp_mul; keep intermediates on stack (six 64-bit limbs for 256-bit arithmetic).
  • Polynomial with Vec<Scalar> incurs one heap allocation per split; acceptable for t <= 100.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • t == n: valid but provides no fault tolerance; construction succeeds, documentation warns.
  • n == 1, t == 1: trivial secret — entire polynomial is constant; split returns one share equal to secret.
  • Scalar value 0: field element zero; fp_inv(0) is undefined in F_p — return Err or the field convention (0); document and test.
  • ThresholdParams with t or n overflow usize on 32-bit targets: clamp to u32; document platform constraint.
  • Field prime mismatch: if caller mixes secp256k1 scalars with Ed25519 scalars, arithmetic silently wraps; enforce via type-level curve parameter or feature flags.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
OsRng unavailable sample_scalar returns Err All operations requiring randomness fail Propagate error; no fallback to ThreadRng
Wrong field prime compiled in Field axiom test failures Entire library produces wrong results Feature flag selects prime; CI tests both
Scalar bytes corrupted in memory Axiom tests fail; signature verify fails Incorrect shares; undetectable without VSS Miri run on CI catches memory bugs
ThresholdParams constructed unsafely (bypass new) Downstream panics or wrong reconstruction Full secret exposure risk Make inner fields private; no public struct literal

1.10 Operational Notes

  • No I/O in tss-core or tss-field; no tracing spans needed at this layer.
  • Expose #[cfg(feature="hazmat")] gates on raw scalar constructors to prevent accidental use in production.
  • Log ThresholdParams values (t, n) at DEBUG level on construction; never log Scalar values.
  • Miri target in CI: cargo +nightly miri test -p tss-core -p tss-field.
  • Benchmarks: criterion bench for fp_inv, fp_mul, sample_scalar; report ns/op; gate with --bench.

Phase 2 — Shamir Split

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement tss-shamir::split(secret: Scalar, params: ThresholdParams, rng: &mut SecureRng) -> Result<Vec<Share>>.
  • Sample a uniformly random degree-(t-1) polynomial with coeffs[0] = secret.
  • Evaluate the polynomial at n distinct non-zero ShareId values using Horner's method.
  • Zeroize Polynomial (all coefficients) immediately after the last evaluation.
  • Guarantee I3 (degree exactly t-1) and I2 (perfect secrecy for t-1 shares) by construction.
  • Randomness quality is the single security-critical input; document and audit the RNG path.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P1/I1/I2/I3/ADR-12 — polynomial split; Horner evaluation; perfect secrecy from uniform coefficients; B2 Ch.3

tss-shamir/src/split.rs (P1/I1/I2/I3/ADR-12):

  • split(secret, params, rng) workflow:
    1. Validate params (delegate to ThresholdParams::new guard — already enforced by type; I10).
    2. Allocate Polynomial { coeffs: vec![Scalar::zero(); t] }; set coeffs[0] = secret (P1 — secret is constant term; Shamir 1979).
    3. For i in 1..t: coeffs[i] = rng.sample_scalar() — each coefficient independently uniform (I2 — required for perfect secrecy; B2 Ch.3).
    4. For each id in 1..=n (cast to ShareId): evaluate f(id) using Horner (B1 sec. 12.7; R9): val = coeffs[t-1]; for j in (0..t-1).rev(): val = fp_add(fp_mul(val, id_scalar), coeffs[j]).
    5. Collect Share { id: ShareId(id), value: val } into result Vec<Share>.
    6. Call poly.zeroize() — zeroes all coeffs elements; drop poly (P9/I9/ADR-13).
    7. Return Ok(shares).
  • ShareId derivation: default to sequential 1..=n; caller may supply a custom id_list: &[ShareId] (overload) if non-sequential ids are needed (e.g., additive resharing; Phase 4).

tss-shamir/src/polynomial.rs:

  • Polynomial::evaluate(x: Scalar) -> Scalar — Horner; constant-time (no early exit).
  • impl ZeroizeOnDrop for Polynomial — zeroizes coeffs vec element-wise.
  • Polynomial::degree() -> usize — coeffs.len() - 1.

tss-shamir/src/lib.rs — re-exports split, reconstruct (Phase 3), reshare (Phase 4).

tss-shamir/tests/split_tests.rs:

  • Round-trip: split then reconstruct returns original secret.
  • Degree check: evaluating the polynomial at t known points and running reconstruct yields the correct secret.
  • Randomness: two calls to split with the same secret produce statistically different shares (trivially, p=1 - 1/p^n).
  • Edge: t=1 produces n identical-value shares (the constant polynomial); verify reconstruction returns secret.
  • Edge: t=n — reconstruction requires all n shares; dropping any one fails.

tss-shamir/tests/zeroize_tests.rs — under Miri: confirm polynomial bytes are zeroed after split returns.

Horner's method invariant: f(x) = a_{t-1} * x^{t-1} + ... + a_1 * x + a_0; Horner form: ((...((a_{t-1})*x + a_{t-2})*x + ...)*x + a_0) — avoids explicit exponentiation, O(t) multiplications.

2.3 Deliverable

  • tss-shamir::split callable from tss-cli split subcommand.
  • Outputs n Share values each with distinct ShareId.
  • Polynomial zeroized by Drop; Miri clean.
  • cargo test -p tss-shamir passes; round-trip test with reconstruct (stubbed or Phase 3) green.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Round-trip split→reconstruct correctness 100% over 1000 random (secret, t, n) triples
Distinct share ids All n shares have pairwise-distinct ShareId
Polynomial coefficients zeroized Miri: no read of zeroed memory
Horner evaluation matches naive polynomial eval Byte-exact over 500 random test vectors
t=1 special case All n shares equal secret; passes reconstruct
split with t=n Exactly n shares; reconstruct with n-1 must fail
Timing of split with secret=0 vs secret=rand < 1% relative difference (constant-time coefficients)

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
fixtures/shamir/split_vectors.json 20 (secret, t, n, seed, shares) tuples with deterministic RNG Exact share values match
fixtures/shamir/horner_eval.json (poly_coeffs, x_values, expected_y) — 50 cases Exact field match
fixtures/shamir/zeroize_check Miri test script asserting no coeff bytes remain after split All zero post-split
fixtures/shamir/edge_t1.json t=1, n=5, secret=X → 5 shares all equal X Exact values
fixtures/shamir/rng_quality.json Chi-squared test on 10k coefficient samples p-value > 0.05

Malformed: split called with t=0 (rejected by ThresholdParams), n=0 (rejected), secret bytes out of range (reduce or reject).

2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug
Polynomial not zeroized before return Secret survives on heap poly moved into iterator, Drop not triggered Add explicit zeroize call before Ok return; Miri test
ShareId(0) generated f(0) == secret, exposing secret as a share Off-by-one in id loop Assert id >= 1 in ShareId constructor
Horner loop index reversal Wrong polynomial evaluation for j in 0..t-1 instead of (0..t-1).rev() Fixture comparison catches immediately
Weak RNG substituted in tests Test vectors appear to pass but production RNG differs Using StdRng::seed_from_u64 in non-test code Gate seeded RNG behind #[cfg(test)] only
t shares all from same consecutive range Structural bias in subset selection Not a split bug but a downstream usage error Document that caller controls share distribution
Large n (e.g., n=1000) causes slow split O(t*n) multiplications; t=500,n=1000 = 500k muls Expected complexity; document threshold Benchmark; add n <= 255 soft cap in params

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Horner evaluation: O(t) fp_mul + O(t) fp_add per share; total O(t*n) multiplications for a split.
  • For t=3, n=5: 15 multiplications — negligible; for t=100, n=200: 20k multiplications — ~1ms on modern hardware.
  • Vec<Share> allocation: one allocation of n * size_of::<Share>() bytes; avoid per-share alloc.
  • Polynomial allocation: one Vec<Scalar> of length t; freed (and zeroized) at end of split.
  • Batched split of multiple secrets (e.g., for DKG sub-shares): caller loops; no internal batching needed.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • n == t == 1: degree-0 polynomial; single share equals the secret; reconstruction trivial.
  • secret == 0: valid field element; polynomial with coeffs[0]=0; shares are non-zero with overwhelmingly high probability.
  • ShareId values not starting at 1: if caller supplies custom ids, Horner still works because x is just a non-zero field element; validate distinctness.
  • Polynomial of degree t-1 where all random coefficients happen to be zero (probability p^{-(t-1)}): degenerate polynomial; still correct; reconstruction still works; do not special-case.
  • Platform usize narrower than u32 for ShareId(u32): u32 is always 32 bits; safe across platforms.
  • Split called twice with the same secret: produces independently sampled shares; the two share sets reveal nothing more than one alone (I2 holds per set independently).

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
OsRng entropy failure sample_scalar returns Err propagated from split Split returns Err; no shares created Caller must not retry with degraded RNG
Memory allocator OOM during Vec<Share> construction Panic (Rust default allocator) Split aborts; no partial shares Document n <= 255 recommendation; pre-check
Polynomial zeroize silently skipped by compiler optimizer Secret survives in memory Potential memory disclosure Use zeroize crate which prevents optimization of zeroing
ShareId collision if caller provides duplicate ids Duplicate shares; reconstruction fails Wrong secret recovered silently Validate id list before evaluation; return Err(DuplicateShareId)

2.10 Operational Notes

  • split emits a DEBUG log: "split: t={}, n={}, share_ids={:?}" — never logs Scalar values.
  • No spans needed at this layer; latency is sub-millisecond for typical (t,n).
  • tss-cli split subcommand: reads secret from stdin (not CLI arg to avoid shell history); writes shares as newline-delimited hex to stdout.
  • Deployment: split is typically called by the dealer once; no retry logic needed inside the function.
  • Audit trail: log the hash of the commitment (Phase 6) rather than the secret or shares when recording a split event.

Phase 3 — Shamir Reconstruct

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement tss-shamir::reconstruct(shares: &[Share]) -> Result<Scalar>.
  • Lagrange-interpolate the polynomial at x=0 over any t-subset of valid shares.
  • Computation is constant-time in the share values and Lagrange coefficients (I9, P4).
  • Enforce: shares must be distinct (ShareId uniqueness), count >= t (from ThresholdParams context).
  • Prove by property test that any t-subset reconstructs the same secret (I1) and any (t-1)-subset gives an independent distribution (I2).
  • Zeroize any intermediate secrets (the reconstructed secret itself is returned; caller's responsibility to zeroize).

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P1/I1/I4/ADR-06 — Lagrange interpolation at x=0; constant-time inverse; batch inversion Montgomery trick; I9/B5 Ch.2

tss-shamir/src/reconstruct.rs (P1/I1/I4/ADR-06):

  • reconstruct(shares: &[Share]) -> Result<Scalar> workflow:
    1. Validate shares.len() >= 1 (minimum); the exact >= t enforcement is caller's responsibility or enforced by a ThresholdParams-aware wrapper reconstruct_with_params (I10).
    2. Check pairwise-distinct ShareId: collect ids, sort, check adjacent pairs; return Err(DuplicateShareId) if any match (I4 — duplicate id causes zero denominator).
    3. Compute Lagrange interpolation at x = 0 (P1/Shamir 1979/B1 sec. 12.7):
      • For each share i: lambda_i = product_{j != i} (-x_j) / (x_i - x_j) all in F_p.
      • secret = sum_i (lambda_i * y_i) where y_i = shares[i].value.
    4. Return Ok(secret).

Lagrange coefficient computation in tss-field/src/lagrange.rs (I4/P4/I9):

  • lagrange_coefficient(i: usize, ids: &[ShareId]) -> Scalar — computes lambda_i at x=0:
    • Numerator: product_{j != i} (0 - x_j) = product_{j != i} (-x_j) — negate each x_j.
    • Denominator: product_{j != i} (x_i - x_j).
    • Return fp_mul(numerator, fp_inv(denominator)).
  • Constant-time requirement (I9/ADR-27): use subtle::ConditionallySelectable for index comparisons; never branch on i == j in a secret-dependent way; fp_inv is already constant-time (Phase 1/ADR-06).
  • all_lagrange_coefficients(ids: &[ShareId]) -> Vec<Scalar> — computes all n coefficients; O(n^2) multiplications; use Montgomery batch inversion (B5 Ch.2) to reduce to 3(n-1) muls + 1 inv.

tss-shamir/src/reconstruct.rs — reconstruct_with_params(shares, params): checks shares.len() == params.t exactly; returns Err(UnderThreshold) if too few.

tss-shamir/tests/reconstruct_tests.rs:

  • t=3, n=5: try all C(5,3)=10 subsets; each must return identical secret (I1).
  • t=2, n=5: all 10 pairs; all equal.
  • t-1 shares: 1000 random experiments; recovered value must not equal original secret (statistical; not a hard check — check distribution instead).
  • Duplicate ShareId: returns Err(DuplicateShareId).
  • Single share with t=1: returns that share's value as secret.

tss-shamir/tests/info_theoretic.rs — information-theoretic secrecy check:

  • For 1000 random secrets s, split with t=3, n=5, take any 2 shares; compute the distribution of reconstruct outputs assuming the remaining 2 shares are unknown — verify the recovered distribution is uniform over F_p (chi-squared test).
  • This is a statistical test, not a proof; it catches degenerate polynomial bugs.

3.3 Deliverable

  • tss-shamir::reconstruct and tss-shamir::reconstruct_with_params callable.
  • All C(n,t) subsets for t in {2,3,5}, n in {t..=10} reconstruct correctly.
  • tss-cli reconstruct subcommand reads shares from stdin (one per line, hex), outputs secret hex.
  • Miri-clean: no use-after-free on share data.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
All C(n,t) subset reconstruction tests 100% exact match for t in {2,3,5,10}, n up to 15
Duplicate ShareId rejection Returns Err(DuplicateShareId), never a wrong secret
Under-threshold rejection (reconstruct_with_params) Returns Err(UnderThreshold)
Lagrange coefficient correctness vs naive reference Byte-exact for 500 random share sets
Timing of reconstruct (secret=0 vs secret=rand) < 1% relative timing difference
Information-theoretic secrecy statistical test Chi-squared p-value > 0.05 on 1000 trials with t-1 shares
t=1 reconstruction Returns share value; O(1)

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
fixtures/shamir/reconstruct_vectors.json 30 (shares_subset, expected_secret) from deterministic split Exact scalar match
fixtures/shamir/all_subsets.json t=3, n=5, all 10 subsets, secret S All 10 outputs == S
fixtures/shamir/under_threshold.json t=3, only 2 shares provided Err(UnderThreshold)
fixtures/shamir/duplicate_id.json Two shares with ShareId(3) Err(DuplicateShareId)
fixtures/shamir/lagrange_coeffs.json (ids, expected_lambda_i) — 20 cases Exact field values
fixtures/shamir/secrecy_distribution.json 1000-trial chi-squared result with t-1 shares p-value stored; must be > 0.05

Malformed: zero share list (return Err(UnderThreshold)); share with ShareId(0) (return Err(InvalidShareId)).

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug
Division by zero in Lagrange denominator fp_inv(0) called; undefined behavior or panic Two shares with same ShareId passed Duplicate check before Lagrange loop
Non-constant-time if i == j in Lagrange loop Side-channel on share index Explicit index comparison inside secret loop Use subtle::ConstantTimeEq for mask; sum with select
Lagrange sum accumulated in wrong order Associativity holds in F_p; result still correct Not a bug; note it for clarity Verify with fixture
Shares provided in arbitrary order Lagrange is order-independent; but sort by ShareId for determinism No bug; add sort for canonical form Fixture tests with shuffled order
reconstruct called with len > t shares Overdetermined system; Lagrange still gives correct result (polynomial is unique) Lagrange handles any subset >= t Document: caller may pass exactly t or all n shares
Secret = 0 treated as error fp_mul or check treats zero as invalid Wrong guard if secret == 0 { Err(...) } Secret zero is valid; never special-case on secret value

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Lagrange coefficient computation: O(t^2) multiplications + O(t) inversions; bottleneck is fp_inv.
  • Batched inversions: use Montgomery's batch inversion trick — 1/a_i for all i in O(t) multiplications + one fp_inv; reduces cost from O(t) inversions to O(t) muls + 1 inv.
  • For t = 100: naive is 100 inversions (~200us each at ~2us/inv); batch trick reduces to ~200us total.
  • Avoid allocating Vec<Scalar> for Lagrange coefficients when t <= 16; use stack array [Scalar; 16] via const generics or smallvec.
  • reconstruct with n >> t shares (over-determined): performance degrades as O(n^2); document that callers should pass exactly t shares.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • shares.len() == 1, t == 1: trivial reconstruction; return shares[0].value directly (still run through Lagrange for code uniformity — the product is just the single coefficient 1).
  • shares.len() > t: valid; polynomial interpolation is still unique; result correct; no error.
  • All shares have value == 0: pathological but valid; polynomial could be the zero polynomial; reconstruction returns 0 (the secret was 0).
  • ShareId values not contiguous (e.g., ids = {1, 5, 23}): Lagrange handles arbitrary non-zero distinct ids; no assumption of contiguity.
  • Overflow in Lagrange numerator/denominator intermediate: all arithmetic is mod p; no native integer overflow possible if field ops correctly reduce.
  • Shares from different splits (different polynomials, same ShareId): reconstruction returns garbage; no error detectable at this layer — VSS (Phase 6) is needed for detection.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Wrong secret reconstructed Downstream operation fails (e.g., signature invalid) Silent; no cryptographic check at this layer Add VSS verification (Phase 6) before use
fp_inv(0) panic Runtime panic Crash; no secret disclosure Duplicate-id guard prevents this reaching fp_inv
Corrupted share value Wrong reconstruction; no error Silent wrong result VSS verification (Phase 6); Feldman check catches it
Memory leak of reconstructed secret Secret survives in heap Disclosure if attacker reads memory Caller must zeroize returned Scalar; document contract
Under-threshold called silently reconstruct_with_params bypassed Wrong partial result Always use reconstruct_with_params in production paths

3.10 Operational Notes

  • reconstruct emits DEBUG log: "reconstruct: using {}/{} shares, ids={:?}" — never logs share values or recovered secret.
  • tss-cli reconstruct: reads shares from stdin (hex lines), writes secret to stdout; stdin/stdout are the only I/O; process exits after one invocation.
  • Secret returned as Scalar; caller responsible for zeroize after use — document in rustdoc.
  • Metrics: record reconstruct_duration_us histogram; alert if > 10ms for t <= 50 (indicates field performance regression).
  • No persistent state in reconstruct; no rollback needed; stateless pure function.
  • Cross-reference Phase 6 (Feldman VSS) for share integrity verification before reconstruction in production deployments.

Phase 4 — secret-sharing edge cases + reshare

4.1 Objectives

  • Harden tss-shamir against all malformed / adversarial inputs: duplicate ShareIds, zero ShareIds, out-of-range share values, under-threshold subsets.
  • Implement additive resharing: each current holder splits their share into a new degree-(t'-1) polynomial, distributes sub-shares to new parties, new parties sum received sub-shares to obtain a new sharing of the same secret with possibly different (t', n').
  • Guarantee zeroization of all intermediate secrets (polynomial coefficients, sub-shares, partial sums) before they are dropped.
  • Enforce I10 (fail-closed) on every input path.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P1/I3/I4/ADR-12/ADR-13

Module: tss-shamir/src/validation.rs

Function Contract
validate_params(p: ThresholdParams) -> Result<()> reject t=0, t>n, n=0
validate_share_ids(ids: &[ShareId]) -> Result<()> reject zero id, duplicate ids; O(n log n) sort-and-scan
validate_share(s: &Share) -> Result<()> id nonzero; value in [1, p-1] (nonzero field element)
validate_subset(shares: &[Share], t: usize) -> Result<()> length >= t; all ids distinct; each value in range

Module: tss-shamir/src/reshare.rs

Additive resharing algorithm:

  1. Each current holder i with share s_i calls split(s_i, t', n'), producing sub-shares {(j, f_i(j))}.
  2. Party j receives one sub-share from each holder in the qualified set; sums them: new_share_j = sum_i f_i(j).
  3. Because each f_i has constant term s_i and the s_i form a valid (t-1)-degree sharing of the secret, the combined polynomial has constant term = secret and degree max(t-1, t'-1); to achieve exactly degree t'-1 the protocol requires all holders to synchronize.
  4. Verifiability: each sub-share distribution runs through tss-vss Feldman commitments; party j verifies every sub-share before summing.

Zeroization:

  • Polynomial implements Drop via zeroize::Zeroize on coeffs; all temporary Vec<Scalar> holding sub-shares use Zeroizing<Vec<Scalar>>.
  • Share.value is a Zeroizing<Scalar> wrapper at the field level.

Error variants added to tss-core::error::TssError: DuplicateShareId, ZeroShareId, ShareOutOfRange, BelowThreshold, ReshareVssMismatch, QualifiedSetTooSmall.

4.3 Deliverable

  • tss-shamir compiles with validate_* guards on all public entry points.
  • reshare function: signature fn reshare(shares: &[Share], old_params: ThresholdParams, new_params: ThresholdParams, rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng) -> Result<Vec<ReshareBundle>> where ReshareBundle contains sub-shares + Feldman commitments.
  • All intermediate secrets zeroized; confirmed via Zeroizing wrappers and Drop audit.
  • cargo test -p tss-shamir passes including reshare round-trip and all negative tests.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Expected
Split then reshare, reconstruct from new shares same secret
Duplicate ShareId in input Err(DuplicateShareId)
Zero ShareId Err(ZeroShareId)
Share value = 0 Err(ShareOutOfRange)
Reconstruct with t-1 shares Err(BelowThreshold)
Reshare with new (t', n') where t'=1, n'=1 allowed; verify result
Property test: 500 random (t, n, subset) triples reconstruction matches original
Zeroize drop test: Polynomial memory cleared post-drop (test with miri or manual memcheck) pass

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose
fixtures/reshare_roundtrip.json old (t=3,n=5) -> new (t=2,n=4); original and reconstructed secret
fixtures/reshare_threshold_change.json t increases: (2,5) -> (4,7)
fixtures/reject_duplicate_id.json two shares with same ShareId; expect error
fixtures/reject_zero_id.json ShareId=0; expect error
fixtures/reject_below_threshold.json only t-1 shares presented; expect error
fixtures/share_out_of_range.json share value = p (field modulus); expect error

All fixture files: { "input": { ... }, "expect": "ok" | "err:<variant>" }.

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Silent duplicate ID wrong secret reconstructed Lagrange denominator = 0 (division by zero in field) field inv of 0 returns an error; I10 catches it only if validation runs first
Reshare constant-term drift reshared secret differs sub-shares not all committed to same old-share; a party used a polynomial with wrong constant term Feldman commitment check on each sub-share; mismatch -> ReshareVssMismatch
Partial-sum zeroize race sub-share value leaks after summation partial_sum Vec not wrapped in Zeroizing miri / valgrind shows stale bytes post-drop
Under-threshold masquerading t-1 shares + 1 forged share no validation of share id uniqueness accept only if validation passes before Lagrange
New t' > old n protocol stalls: not enough sub-share sources caller misconfigures new_params QualifiedSetTooSmall returned before any computation
ShareId 0 is the secret a party claims id=0 and receives f(0)=secret protocol omits zero-id rejection reject in validate_share_ids

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Validation: sort_unstable on share ids is O(n log n); acceptable for n <= 1000.
  • Reshare sub-share generation: each holder invokes split for every new party; O(n * n') scalar multiplications; batching Horner evaluations amortizes loop overhead.
  • Zeroizing large Vec<Scalar>: zeroize iterates the vec; for n=1000 shares (32 bytes each) = 32 KB — negligible.
  • Cloning polynomials for fault isolation: avoid; pass by reference and zeroize in place.

4.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
n=1, t=1 valid 1-of-1 split; no threshold security; warn via log
shares from different dealings mixed ids distinct but commitments mismatch; VSS check catches this
reshare with n'=0 or t'=0 validate_params returns error before any allocation
caller passes empty slice BelowThreshold
ShareId values non-contiguous allowed; Lagrange works on any distinct nonzero field elements
Scalar = p-1 (max valid) accepted; must not be treated as out-of-range

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • Bad VSS commitment on reshare sub-share: detected by Feldman check; party broadcasts complaint; reshare round aborted; old shares remain valid.
  • Memory disclosure of intermediate polynomial: Zeroizing drop handles; if process crashes mid-drop, OS reclaims pages; beyond the threat model of this crate.
  • Reconstruction returns wrong secret: root cause is duplicate or forged share id; I10 + validation prevents; if it somehow occurs, verifier comparing against a known commitment detects.
  • Blast radius: reshare failure leaves old sharing intact; no secret is exposed.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Log at DEBUG: number of shares validated, reshare new parameters (never log share values).
  • Metric: tss_shamir_validate_errors_total{reason=...} counter per error variant.
  • tss-cli split subcommand outputs share count and threshold to stderr; never prints share values to stdout by default.
  • Migration: reshare is the mechanism for changing n/t in production; document that all current holders must participate in the same epoch.

Phase 5 — elliptic-curve group

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement tss-group: a prime-order elliptic-curve group abstraction (Point, Scalar) with constant-time scalar multiplication, point addition/doubling, two independent generators g and h, hash_to_scalar, hash_to_point.
  • Reuse curve25519-dalek (portfolio project #90) as the underlying group where the curve is Ed25519/Ristretto255; wrap it behind tss-group trait objects so the curve can be swapped.
  • Provide GroupOps trait covering all operations needed by tss-vss, tss-dkg, tss-schnorr, tss-bls.
  • All scalar-mul paths constant-time (I9); no variable-time short-circuit for zero or one.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-05/ADR-06/P1/I9

Module: tss-group/src/lib.rs — re-exports; feature flags curve25519, bls12_381.

Trait GroupOps (tss-group/src/traits.rs):

pub trait GroupOps: Clone + Send + Sync {
    type Scalar: ScalarOps;
    type Point: PointOps;
    fn generator(&self) -> Self::Point;          // g
    fn alt_generator(&self) -> Self::Point;      // h, NUMS derivation
    fn scalar_mul(&self, p: &Self::Point, s: &Self::Scalar) -> Self::Point;
    fn hash_to_scalar(&self, data: &[u8]) -> Self::Scalar;
    fn hash_to_point(&self, data: &[u8]) -> Self::Point;
}

Trait ScalarOps: add, sub, mul, neg, inv, zero, one, is_zero, from_bytes, to_bytes; all constant-time.

Trait PointOps: add, sub, neg, identity, is_identity, compress, decompress, ct_eq.

Module: tss-group/src/ristretto.rs — concrete impl wrapping curve25519_dalek::RistrettoPoint and curve25519_dalek::Scalar:

  • scalar_mul: delegates to dalek's vartime disabled; uses RistrettoPoint::mul which is constant-time.
  • alt_generator h: derived via RistrettoPoint::hash_from_bytes::<Sha512>(b"tss-group/h/v1") — nothing-up-my-sleeve, domain-separated.
  • hash_to_scalar: Scalar::hash_from_bytes::<Sha512>(data) — 64-byte wide reduction, uniform.
  • hash_to_point: RistrettoPoint::hash_from_bytes::<Sha512>(data) — Elligator2 via dalek.

Module: tss-group/src/bls.rs (optional, behind feature = "bls12_381"):

  • Wraps bls12_381::G1Projective and bls12_381::Scalar.
  • hash_to_point: HashToCurve from bls12_381_hash_to_curve crate (IETF draft suite BLS12381G1_XMD:SHA-256_SSWU_RO_).
  • Pairing: bls12_381::pairing(g1, g2) exposed via PairingOps trait in tss-bls.

Module: tss-group/src/scalar_mul.rs — Montgomery ladder for generic fallback (not needed for dalek but available for test/reference):

  • 255 iterations; bit extraction via ct_select; no early exit.

Zeroization: Scalar values implement Zeroize; Point coordinates are public, no zeroize required.

5.3 Deliverable

  • tss-group crate compiles with curve25519 feature; GroupOps impl passes all unit tests.
  • g and h generators: distinct, reproducible, documented derivation.
  • scalar_mul(g, s) matches dalek reference for 1000 random scalars.
  • hash_to_scalar and hash_to_point produce uniform outputs; statistical bias tests pass.
  • bls12_381 feature compiles (may be gated behind pairing feature flag).

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Expected
scalar_mul(g, 0) = identity pass
scalar_mul(g, 1) = g pass
scalar_mul(g, order) = identity pass
scalar_mul(g, a) + scalar_mul(g, b) = scalar_mul(g, a+b) linearity; 500 random pairs
g != h and h not identity pass
hash_to_scalar uniform: chi-squared over 10000 outputs p > 0.01
compress / decompress round-trip identity for 1000 random points
Constant-time audit: dudect or criterion timing with secret vs non-secret scalars no timing difference > 3 sigma
alt_generator derivation is deterministic across builds hash of serialized h is fixed

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose
fixtures/scalar_mul_vectors.json 20 (scalar, expected_point) pairs from trusted reference
fixtures/hash_to_scalar_vectors.json 10 (input_bytes, expected_scalar_hex) from RFC / test suite
fixtures/hash_to_point_vectors.json 10 (input_bytes, expected_point_hex) — Elligator2
fixtures/generator_coords.json compressed g and h as hex; assert across versions
fixtures/point_arithmetic.json add, double, negate test vectors

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Using vartime scalar mul timing side-channel on secret scalars dalek exposes both vartime and constant-time; wrong one called grep for vartime; CI lint rule
h derived from g (DLOG known) Pedersen commitment collapses to Feldman; hiding lost using h = n*g for a known n verify derivation is hash-based with no known relation
Point deserialization of low-order point cofactor attack in non-Ristretto groups small subgroup element passes some checks Ristretto eliminates cofactor; BLS12-381 requires explicit subgroup check
hash_to_scalar bias from 32-byte truncation non-uniform scalars -> biased keys/nonces hash output modded naively use 64-byte hash + wide reduction (Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide)
Scalar serialization endianness mismatch cross-crate mismatch in share values little-endian dalek vs big-endian external format pin encoding in to_bytes/from_bytes docs
Identity point in commitment product VSS check trivially passes any share a malicious dealer sends g^0 for a coefficient is_identity check on every commitment point before accepting

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Scalar mul (Ristretto): ~60 µs on modern hardware via dalek's vectorized backend; for DKG with n=100 parties, 100 scalar muls per party = ~6 ms; acceptable.
  • Batch scalar mul: dalek provides RistrettoPoint::multiscalar_mul (Pippenger); use in VSS commit verification to reduce O(t) individual muls to one batch call.
  • hash_to_point: involves SHA-512 + Elligator; ~10 µs; avoid calling in tight loops.
  • Point compression/decompression: ~10 µs each; minimize by keeping points in projective form internally; compress only for serialization.
  • BLS pairing: ~1 ms for bls12_381::pairing; not on the hot path (only final signature verify).

5.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Scalar = 0 passed to scalar_mul returns identity; no special branch (constant-time)
Identity point passed to scalar_mul returns identity
Decompressing invalid bytes Err(InvalidPoint)
hash_to_scalar(b"") valid; produces deterministic scalar
Scalar > field order in from_bytes reduce mod order; document behavior
BLS feature not compiled, BLS function called compile error via trait bound; no runtime panic

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong generator h: if h = k*g for known k, Pedersen binding holds but hiding is lost; catastrophic for VSS. Detection: audit derivation at compile time via fixture.
  • Variable-time scalar mul on nonce: nonce leaks partial bits of secret share via timing. Detection: dudect CI pass. Recovery: switch all paths to dalek's constant-time API.
  • Point not on curve after deserialization: downstream arithmetic produces garbage. Detection: decompress() returns None; all callers check. Blast radius: single party's share invalid, not global failure.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Feature flag curve25519 is the default; bls12_381 is opt-in.
  • Log at TRACE: generator derivation hash (not secret values).
  • Metric: tss_group_scalar_mul_count_total — helps profile DKG/signing rounds.
  • No unsafe in tss-group beyond what curve25519-dalek internally uses; document this in crate README.
  • Dependency audit: pin curve25519-dalek version; check for vartime API changes on upgrade.

Phase 6 — Feldman VSS

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement Feldman VSS in tss-vss: dealer publishes commitments C_j = g^{a_j} for each polynomial coefficient a_j; recipients verify shares via g^{s_i} == prod_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j^{i^j}.
  • Commitments are binding (collision resistance of DLP) but NOT hiding: C_0 = g^secret is published, leaking g^secret to all parties.
  • Detect an inconsistent or malicious dealer: a recipient whose share fails the check can broadcast a complaint.
  • Output: FeldmanCommitment (Vec<Point>), share verification function, and serialization for broadcast.
  • Rely on tss-group::GroupOps for all EC operations (I5, P2).

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P2/I5/ADR-15/ADR-16 — Feldman binding commitment; B4 Ch.4

Module: tss-vss/src/feldman.rs

pub struct FeldmanCommitment(pub Vec<Point>);  // length = t; C[j] = g^{a_j}

pub fn commit(poly: &Polynomial, group: &dyn GroupOps) -> FeldmanCommitment;
// For each coefficient a_j: C_j = scalar_mul(g, a_j). O(t) scalar muls.

pub fn verify_share(
    share: &Share,
    commitment: &FeldmanCommitment,
    group: &dyn GroupOps,
) -> Result<()>;
// Compute lhs = scalar_mul(g, share.value).
// Compute rhs = sum_{j=0}^{t-1} scalar_mul(C[j], id^j mod p).
// Use batch multiscalar_mul for rhs for efficiency.
// Return Ok(()) iff lhs == rhs (ct_eq); else Err(VssShareInvalid).

Algorithm detail — commit: iterate poly.coeffs (index j = 0..t-1) with g = group.generator(), computing C_j = group.scalar_mul(g, &a_j) for each, and collect into FeldmanCommitment(vec![C_0, ..., C_{t-1}]). No zeroization needed on the commitment itself (it is public); the source poly must still be zeroized by the caller per the Phase 2 contract.

Algorithm detail — verify_share: take x = share.id.0 as a Scalar, compute powers x^0, x^1, ..., x^{t-1} iteratively (constant-time multiplication), then rhs = multiscalar_mul(powers, commitment.0) (Pippenger batch) and lhs = scalar_mul(g, share.value); return Ok(()) iff lhs.ct_eq(&rhs), else Err — the equality check itself must be constant-time.

Module: tss-vss/src/lib.rs — re-exports FeldmanCommitment, verify_share, commit, PedersenCommitment (Phase 7).

Error variants: VssShareInvalid, VssCommitmentLenMismatch, VssIdentityCommitment (C_j is identity — dealer cheated).

Serialization (tss-vss/src/serde.rs): each Point compressed to 32 bytes; FeldmanCommitment serializes as [t * 32] bytes; include version byte.

Integration with tss-shamir: split_with_commitment(secret, params, rng, group) -> (Vec<Share>, FeldmanCommitment) — one-call API combining split + commit.

6.3 Deliverable

  • tss-vss compiles; commit and verify_share exported.
  • split_with_commitment API available in tss-shamir with Feldman integration.
  • Honest shares always verify; tampered share values fail verification.
  • Commitments serialize/deserialize round-trip.
  • cargo test -p tss-vss passes all positive and negative cases.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Expected
Honest share passes verify_share Ok(()) for all n shares, 200 random (t,n)
Share value flipped by 1 Err(VssShareInvalid)
Share id swapped Err(VssShareInvalid)
Wrong commitment (different poly) used Err(VssShareInvalid)
commitment.len() != t Err(VssCommitmentLenMismatch)
Identity point in commitment Err(VssIdentityCommitment)
verify_share timing: 1000 calls, valid vs invalid no measurable timing difference (ct_eq enforced)
Serialize + deserialize commitment round-trip identity for 100 random polys
g^secret visible in C_0 document: Feldman is NOT hiding; C_0 = g^secret

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose

fixtures/feldman_tampered_share.json one share value incremented; verify fails
fixtures/feldman_wrong_id.json share with id of another party; verify fails
fixtures/feldman_commitment_serialization.json commitment hex round-trip

fixtures/feldman_t_equals_n.json fully-determined polynomial (t=n); all shares must verify

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Naive power computation overflows wrong rhs; share never verifies id^j computed as u64 before converting to Scalar always compute powers as Scalar from the start
Variable-time equality check timing leaks share validity using == on Point structs enforce ct_eq; lint ban on PartialEq for Point in verify path
Feldman used where hiding required leaks g^secret to all parties caller conflates Feldman with Pedersen document prominently: use Pedersen (Phase 7) when hiding is needed
Off-by-one in commitment length last coefficient not committed loop bound 0..t vs 0..=t-1 edge property test: commitment length == poly.coeffs.len()
Accepting identity commitment point any scalar s satisfies g^s == identity^x * ... trivially for zero-coefficient terms dealer sets some C_j = identity to allow arbitrary share values check all C_j != identity before calling verify
Batch mul with wrong scalar order rhs computed for wrong powers multiscalar_mul expects scalars and points in matched order unit test: single-point batch matches individual scalar_mul

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • commit: O(t) scalar muls; for t=100, ~6 ms with dalek scalar mul; use batch if commitment construction becomes a bottleneck.
  • verify_share: O(t) scalar muls + batch; dalek multiscalar_mul with Pippenger reduces this to O(t / log t) effective muls for large t.
  • Power computation x^j: Horner-style or square-and-multiply in Scalar; O(log j) muls per power but sequential; precompute all t powers in O(t) multiplications.
  • Verification in DKG: n parties each verify n sub-shares; O(n^2 * t) total scalar muls; for n=50, t=26, ~65000 muls (~4 s); batch across parties to amortize.
  • Commitment serialization: t * 32 bytes; for t=100 = 3200 bytes; negligible I/O overhead.

6.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
t=1 (secret with no randomness) valid; single commitment C_0 = g^secret; any 1 share reconstructs
ShareId = field order - 1 (max valid) power computation stays in field; no overflow
Commitment with extra trailing points VssCommitmentLenMismatch
Dealer sends commitment then changes polynomial share fails verify; complaint valid
Two shares with same id presented for verification both would verify if honest; duplicate id caught upstream by Phase 4
hash_to_scalar(id_bytes) vs raw integer for powers use raw integer cast to Scalar; document this choice

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Malicious dealer sends inconsistent shares: detected by verify_share -> VssShareInvalid; recipient broadcasts complaint; dealer is identified and excluded; I5 holds for honest parties.
  • Commitment deserialization of invalid point: decompress fails; VssShareInvalid or InvalidPoint; no downstream computation on bad point.
  • Blast radius of bad dealer: only that dealer's contribution is excluded; honest parties proceed with qualified set in Phase 8 (DKG).
  • C_0 leaks g^secret: not a failure for Feldman (by design); a system failure if code using Feldman expected hiding. Mitigation: use Pedersen in Phase 7 for any scenario requiring secrecy of the shared value.
  • Postmortem hook: log commitment hash (not content) at INFO on each DKG round; allows reconstruction of which dealer's commitment was present.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Log at DEBUG: "feldman::commit t={} n_commitments={}" (no secret values).
  • Log at WARN: "feldman::verify_share failed id={}" — triggers complaint logic upstream.
  • Metric: tss_vss_feldman_verify_total{result=ok|fail} counter.
  • FeldmanCommitment is a public broadcast value; include in the protocol transcript (Phase 13).
  • Upgrading from Feldman to Pedersen (Phase 7): the commitment format changes; round versioning in tss-protocol must distinguish them.
  • No deprecated code paths from Phase 3 are called inside tss-vss; tss-vss depends on tss-group and tss-core only.

Phase 7 — Pedersen VSS

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement Pedersen VSS in tss-vss: two independent generators g, h; dealer commits C_j = g^{a_j} * h^{b_j} for each polynomial coefficient j = 0..t-1.
  • Provide perfectly-hiding, computationally-binding commitments (unconditional secrecy of the secret during dealing).
  • Expose pedersen_deal and pedersen_verify_share in tss-vss; reuse tss-group points and tss-field arithmetic.
  • Establish the VSS primitive that tss-dkg will use in Phase 8.
  • Enforce: discrete-log relation between g and h must remain unknown (NUMS construction); hardcoded in tss-group.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P2/P3/I5/ADR-16 — Pedersen hiding+binding; blinding poly b(0) must be non-zero random; B4 Ch.4 Modules / files touched: tss-vss/src/pedersen.rs (new module, re-exported from tss-vss/src/lib.rs alongside feldman::*); tss-group/src/generators.rs (extended to expose secondary generator h, NUMS derivation).

Data structures (signatures only):

pub struct PedersenDealing {
    pub commitments: Vec<Point>,          // C_j = g^{a_j} * h^{b_j}, length t
    pub shares: Vec<Share>,               // a_j evaluated at each id
    pub blinding_shares: Vec<Share>,      // b_j evaluated at each id (companion)
}

pub fn pedersen_deal(
    secret: &Scalar,
    params: &ThresholdParams,
    ids: &[ShareId],
    rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng,
) -> Result<PedersenDealing, TssError>;

pub fn pedersen_verify_share(
    id: ShareId,
    share: &Share,
    blinding: &Share,
    commitments: &[Point],
) -> Result<(), TssError>;

Algorithm — pedersen_deal: sample random degree-(t-1) polynomial a(x) with a(0) = secret (via tss-shamir::sample_polynomial) and an independent random degree-(t-1) blinding polynomial b(x) with unconstrained random b(0); compute C_j = g^{a_j} + h^{b_j} for each coefficient (tss-group point add, scalar mul); evaluate both polynomials at each ShareId via Horner's method to output paired (share_i, blinding_i); zeroize both coefficient vectors after evaluation. Algorithm — pedersen_verify_share: for recipient i, compute LHS = g^{share.value} + h^{blinding.value} and RHS = sum_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j * i^j (Point linear combination in the exponent); verification condition LHS == RHS, else Err(CommitmentMismatch). NUMS generator derivation: h = hash_to_point("tss-pedersen-h-v1") via a deterministic hash-to-curve function from tss-group, documented in tss-group/src/generators.rs; g and h never share a known DL relation by construction.

Tests:

  • Unit: pedersen_deal + pedersen_verify_share roundtrip for random (t,n) with all ids.
  • Tamper: flip one share.value bit → pedersen_verify_share returns Err(InvalidShare).
  • Tamper: flip one blinding.value bit → same rejection.
  • Tamper: alter commitment C_0 → all share verifications fail.
  • Property test: 100 random (t, n, ids) — every share verifies.

7.3 Deliverable

  • tss-vss compiles with both feldman and pedersen modules.
  • pedersen_deal + pedersen_verify_share roundtrip for all n shares.
  • Tampered share/blinding/commitment correctly rejected.
  • All unit and property tests pass; cargo test -p tss-vss green.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
All n shares verify after honest dealing 100% pass
Tampered share.value detected Err(InvalidShare) every case
Tampered blinding.value detected Err(InvalidShare) every case
Corrupted commitment C_j detected Err(CommitmentMismatch) every case
Wrong id in verify call Err(ShareIdMismatch)
Polynomial zeroized post-deal Confirmed via zeroize drop impl
pedersen_deal for (3,5) < 2 ms on reference hardware

tests/fixtures/pedersen_tampered_share.json share.value +1 InvalidShare
tests/fixtures/pedersen_tampered_blinding.json blinding.value +1 InvalidShare

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
g and h derived with known DL Hiding broken; attacker extracts secret from commitments Naive generator selection (e.g., h = 2*g) Audit tss-group/generators.rs; assert h uses NUMS hash-to-curve
Blinding polynomial b(0) not random Reduces to Feldman (no longer perfectly hiding) Accidentally fixing b(0) = 0 Check pedersen_deal: b.coeffs[0] must be independently sampled
Not zeroizing blinding polynomial Blinding scalars persist in heap memory Missing zeroize impl on Polynomial Valgrind / cargo-valgrind post-deal; heap scan
Mismatch between share and blinding ids verify passes wrong pair Caller mixes share ordering ShareId mismatch error; fixture pedersen_wrong_id.json
Point compression format inconsistency Commitment deserialization fails round-trip Mixed compressed/uncompressed encoding Enforce Point::to_bytes() = compressed always in tss-group
Integer overflow in exponent sum i^j Wrong RHS in verify Exponent computed as u64 not Scalar Always compute i^j in field arithmetic via Scalar::pow

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • pedersen_deal cost: 2t scalar muls for commitments + 2tn scalar evaluations (Horner). Dominant term: 2t multi-scalar-mul for commitments; batch with Pippenger if t > 16.
  • pedersen_verify_share: t scalar muls for RHS + 2 scalar muls for LHS = t+2 total. Acceptable per-share.
  • Allocation: 2t Point allocations for commitments; 2n Scalar allocations for shares. Avoid heap per-eval; reuse buffers.
  • Serialization: hex-encode points for fixtures; compressed encoding (33 bytes on secp256k1) reduces wire size vs. uncompressed.

7.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected behavior
t = 1 Commitment is one point C_0 = g^secret * h^r; all shares are trivially the secret
t = n Requires all n shares to reconstruct; normal path
n = 1 Single share; verify with single commitment
ids not distinct, or ids contains ShareId(0) Err(DuplicateShareId) from tss-shamir, or Err(InvalidShareId) — zero is the secret point
secret = 0 Valid; C_0 = h^{b_0}; reconstruction yields Scalar::ZERO
Commitment vector wrong length (!= t) Err(CommitmentLengthMismatch) in verify

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Dealer sends inconsistent share/blinding pedersen_verify_share returns Err Single recipient Reject share; escalate complaint in Phase 8 DKG Re-request from dealer; disqualify if repeated
h generator known DL compromised Hiding property lost; external audit All historical dealings Cannot fix retroactively; requires protocol restart Rotate to new NUMS h; re-deal all secrets
Blinding share leaked Attacker can extract secret from commitments if DL known One share-holder Rotate blinding polynomial; proactive refresh (Phase 12) Trigger share refresh before next epoch

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: tss_vss_pedersen_deal_duration_ms — histogram; alert if p99 > 10 ms for t<=20.
  • Metric: tss_vss_pedersen_verify_failure_total — counter by error type (InvalidShare, CommitmentMismatch, ShareIdMismatch).
  • Log (DEBUG): pedersen_deal params={{t,n}} ids={{ids}} — no secret values.
  • Log (WARN): any pedersen_verify_share failure with party_id, error type.
  • Deployment: tss-vss is a library crate; no binary. Embed in tss-dkg as a dependency.
  • Migration: if h generator changes (version bump), all existing dealings are invalid; version-tag the generator in PedersenCommitment serialization.

Phase 8 — distributed key generation (DKG)

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement tss-dkg: a multi-round DKG where every party acts as both dealer and recipient, no trusted dealer.
  • Each party i runs Pedersen VSS to deal a sub-secret s_i; distributes sub-shares to all parties.
  • Complaint round: parties broadcast complaints against dealers whose shares fail verification.
  • Qualified set: parties who receive no valid complaints advance; their constant-term commitments define the joint public key.
  • Final share for party i = sum of sub-shares from all qualified dealers; joint public key = sum of constant-term commitments C_{0,j} over qualified dealers.
  • Invariant I6: no single party learns the joint secret; I7: joint key aggregation is correct.
  • State machine type DkgRound drives protocol; tss-protocol will orchestrate in Phase 13.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P4/P3/P6/I6/ADR-30 — GJKR DKG four rounds; Pedersen VSS R1; complaint/qualified-set logic; identifiable abort; 2007 bias correction

Modules / files touched: tss-dkg/src/lib.rs, tss-dkg/src/party.rs, tss-dkg/src/round.rs, tss-dkg/src/messages.rs. Uses: tss-vss::pedersen::*, tss-core::*, tss-field::*, tss-group::*.

State machine:

pub enum DkgRound { Commit, Share, Complaint, Finalize }
pub struct DkgParty { pub id: ShareId, pub params: ThresholdParams, state: DkgRound, dealing: Option<PedersenDealing>, received_shares: HashMap<ShareId,(Share,Share)>, complaints: HashMap<ShareId,Vec<ShareId>>, qualified: BTreeSet<ShareId> }

Messages:

pub struct DkgCommitMsg { pub sender: ShareId, pub commitments: Vec<Point> }
pub struct DkgShareMsg  { pub sender: ShareId, pub recipient: ShareId, pub share: Share, pub blinding: Share }
pub struct DkgComplaint { pub complainant: ShareId, pub accused: ShareId, pub share: Share, pub blinding: Share }
pub struct DkgFinalizeMsg { pub sender: ShareId, pub key_share: KeyShare }

Four-round protocol (state-transition table):

Round Step Action Anchor
1 Commit a pedersen_deal(sub_secret_i, params, all_ids, rng); sub_secret_i freshly sampled per party P4 (GJKR 1999)
1 Commit b Broadcast DkgCommitMsg{sender:i, commitments} — perfectly hiding, no sub-secret leaks pre-qualification P3
1 Commit c Transition → DkgRound::Share
2 Share a Per peer j: send encrypted/authenticated DkgShareMsg{sender:i, recipient:j, share, blinding}
2 Share b On receipt: pedersen_verify_share(j, share, blinding, commitments_round1) I5, P3
2 Share c Verify fail → enqueue DkgComplaint with offending share/blinding as evidence P6, ADR-30
2 Share d Transition → DkgRound::Complaint
3 Complaint a Broadcast all complaints (empty message if none)
3 Complaint b Complaint (c,d,s,b): re-run pedersen_verify_share(c,s,b,commitments_d); invalid → disqualify d; valid → disqualify c (lying)
3 Complaint c qualified = all_ids − disqualified; assert |qualified| ≥ t else Err(InsufficientQualified)
3 Complaint d Transition → DkgRound::Finalize
4 Finalize a key_share.secret_share = Σ_{d∈qualified} sub_share_{d,i} (Scalar addition)
4 Finalize b key_share.public_share = g^{secret_share}; joint_public_key = Σ_{d∈qualified} commitments_d[0]
4 Finalize c Output KeyShare{id, secret_share, public_share, group_public: joint_public_key}

Verification of joint key: each party independently checks public_share == Σ_{d∈qualified} C_{0,d} · lambda_i(j) (Lagrange interpolation of individual public shares reproduces the group key).

Tests: honest execution (t=3,n=5) — all parties output consistent KeyShare; joint keys and public-share consistency match. One bad dealer (tampers share to one recipient) — complaint round disqualifies dealer, |qualified|=n-1≥t completes. t bad dealers — Err(InsufficientQualified). Lying complainant — complainant disqualified, accused survives. Sub-secret sum check — reconstruct joint secret from t final shares via Lagrange, equals Σ sub-secrets.

8.3 Deliverable

  • tss-dkg compiles; DkgParty drives 4-round protocol for n simulated parties.
  • Honest run: all parties output matching group_public keys.
  • Complaint handling: bad dealer disqualified; honest majority produces valid joint key.
  • cargo test -p tss-dkg green.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Honest (3,5) DKG: all group_public keys match Must pass
Joint key consistency: sum(C_{0,d} for d in qualified) Matches every party's group_public
Lagrange check: reconstruct secret from t final shares Equals sum(sub_secrets)
One bad dealer, complaint round: qualified = n-1 Completes if n-1 >= t
Lying complainant disqualified Accused party remains qualified
` qualified
DKG (3,5) full round-trip < 50 ms simulated (in-process)

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Joint key depends on disqualified dealer's constant term Joint key inconsistent across parties Forgetting to filter to qualified set Assert all parties compute identical qualified set; diff group_public values
Sub-secret s_i = 0 for all dealers Joint secret = 0; trivially insecure Bug: constant polynomial or zeroized secret Property test: joint key != identity with overwhelming probability

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dominant cost: n Pedersen dealings (2t scalar muls/commitment + 2n evals) → O(n²t) scalar ops; complaint round adds ≤ n² re-verifications × O(t) scalar muls, bounded by n²t worst case.
  • Message complexity: O(n²) point-to-point share messages + O(n) broadcast commitments; batch commitment verification via multi-scalar-mul (Pippenger) for t points; Finalize summation O(n) Scalar adds — negligible.

8.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected behavior
n = t All parties must contribute; any one absence aborts
n = 1, t = 1 (trivial) or params.t = 0 Single party is dealer+recipient, joint key = g^{s_1}; zero threshold rejected Err(InvalidThreshold) at construction
All parties complain about each other Qualified set empty or < t; Err(InsufficientQualified)
Duplicate commitment from same sender (replay) Err(DuplicateDealer) on second receipt
Party absent in Round 2 (no shares sent) Treated as bad dealer; complaints from all recipients; disqualified
Party sends shares to only subset of recipients Partial complaint; disqualified by recipients who did not receive

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Qualified set < t Err(InsufficientQualified) in Round 3 Entire DKG session Abort; do not output any key material Restart DKG with new parameter set or different participants
Party outputs different group_public Consistency check fails at application layer Split-brain on group key Require transcript hash agreement before finalize Compare all group_public values; abort if mismatch
Leaked sub-secret s_i Attacker gains partial information about joint secret Proportional to number of leaks; < t leaks insufficient Trigger proactive refresh (Phase 12) before next epoch Count leaked parties; if < t, refresh shares
Network partition during complaint round Some parties miss complaints Qualified sets diverge Synchronous timeout; re-broadcast before finalize Replay complaint round with extended timeout

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: tss_dkg_round_duration_ms{round} histogram per round (Commit/Share/Complaint/Finalize); tss_dkg_disqualified_count gauge (alert if > n - t); tss_dkg_session_result{status: ok|insufficient_qualified|abort} counter.
  • Logs: INFO session start/end (params, qualified_count); WARN each disqualification (accused_id, reason); ERROR InsufficientQualified (n, t, qualified_count); no secret values logged — only ShareId and error types.
  • Deployment: tss-dkg is a library; tss-protocol (Phase 13) provides network transport; tss-cli wraps for testing. Transcript: all broadcast messages hash into a running Transcript (Phase 13 type); parties must agree on transcript before Finalize.

Phase 9 — threshold Schnorr (FROST) round 1

9.1 Objectives

  • Implement tss-schnorr Round 1: nonce generation, commitment publication, and binding factor derivation per FROST (RFC 9591).
  • Each signer i samples two nonces (d_i, e_i), computes hiding/binding commitments (D_i, E_i) = (g^{d_i}, g^{e_i}).
  • Coordinator collects all (D_i, E_i) pairs into a commitment list; each signer derives per-signer binding factor rho_i = H("rho" || i || msg || commitment_list).
  • Compute group nonce R = sum_i (D_i + rho_i * E_i).
  • Thwarts rogue-nonce (Wagner/Drijvers) attacks: binding factors tie nonces to message and full commitment set.
  • Types: NonceCommitment, BindingFactor; functions: round1_commit, compute_binding_factors, compute_group_nonce.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P5/I7/ADR-18/ADR-19 — FROST RFC 9591 nonce commit; binding factor prevents Drijvers/ROS; P6

Modules / files touched: tss-schnorr/src/round1.rs (new; re-exported from lib.rs), tss-schnorr/src/nonce.rs (nonce sampling with zeroize), tss-schnorr/src/transcript.rs (H("rho") binding factor hash — reuse tss-protocol::Transcript or implement directly). Uses: tss-core::*, tss-group::*, tss-field::*.

Types (signatures):

pub struct NonceCommitment { pub signer_id: ShareId, pub hiding: Point, pub binding: Point }  // D_i=g^{d_i}, E_i=g^{e_i}
pub struct BindingFactor { pub signer_id: ShareId, pub value: Scalar }  // rho_i
pub struct Nonce { hiding: Scalar, binding: Scalar }  // d_i, e_i — secret, zeroize on drop
impl Zeroize for Nonce;   // zeroizes hiding, binding
impl Drop for Nonce;      // drop() invokes zeroize()

Function signatures:

pub fn round1_commit(signer_id: ShareId, rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng) -> (Nonce, NonceCommitment);
// Contract: sample d_i, e_i from CSPRNG; compute D_i=g^d_i, E_i=g^e_i; nonce stays secret.
pub fn compute_binding_factors(signer_id: ShareId, msg: &[u8], commitment_list: &[NonceCommitment]) -> BindingFactor;
// Contract: rho_i = H("rho" || encode(signer_id) || msg || encode_sorted(commitment_list)).
pub fn compute_group_nonce(binding_factors: &[BindingFactor], commitments: &[NonceCommitment]) -> Result<Point, TssError>;
// Contract: R = sum_i (D_i + rho_i * E_i) over signers in commitment_list; returns Err if sets mismatch.

Binding factor hash construction (RFC 9591 sec. 4.3): (1) sort commitment_list by signer_id ascending (deterministic); (2) encode each NonceCommitment as encode(signer_id) || D_i.to_bytes() || E_i.to_bytes() (32+33+33=98 bytes/entry on secp256k1/Ed25519); (3) concatenate with msg length-prefixed, hash domain-separated with "FROST-{CiphersuiteName}-v1"; (4) per signer i: rho_i = H("rho" || encode(i) || msg || encode_list). Group nonce computation: per signer i, contribution_i = D_i + rho_i * E_i (point-scalar mul + point add); R = sum_i contribution_i (iterative point addition).

Tests: RFC 9591 test vector round 1 (seeded nonces) — (D_i,E_i), rho_i, R match spec exactly. Two-party round 1 — R is the sum of individual contributions. Signer absent from commitment list but queried for binding factor — Err(UnknownSigner); duplicate signer_id in list — Err(DuplicateSignerId). Nonce zeroization — memory zeroed after drop (checked with zeroize test hook).

9.3 Deliverable

  • tss-schnorr::round1 compiles; round1_commit, compute_binding_factors, compute_group_nonce callable.
  • RFC 9591 test vector for Round 1 passes.
  • Nonce values zeroized on drop.
  • cargo test -p tss-schnorr green (round 1 tests only; round 2 added in Phase 10).

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
