  • Network partition: in tss-protocol simulated network, drop round-1 messages for one party; verify timeout → abort → retry succeeds.
  • Memory exhaustion proxy: supply n=255, t=128 (max supported); measure heap usage; fail if OOM.

Deterministic Replay

  • All multi-party protocols accept an explicit Rng seed; test harness seeds with [0u8;32] for all parties.
  • Replaying the same seed across versions must produce the same intermediate transcripts up to any protocol step.
  • Transcript test: record all broadcast messages to a golden .bincode fixture; assert byte-equality on replay.
  • Version gate: replaying v0.1 transcripts with v0.2 code is allowed to differ only in signing round (key material is permanent).

Conformance Validation

  • RFC 9591 FROST: all test vectors pass (Appendix B.1–B.4); fail-fast if any vector fails.
  • BLS: where a standardized test vector suite exists (draft-irtf-cfrg-bls-signature), run the pairing-verify path.
  • Shamir reconstruction: verify against known (secret, shares, t) triples from academic test data.
  • Continuous: conformance suite runs on every PR; failure blocks merge.

Soak and Performance-Regression Tests

  • 1 h soak: DKG + FROST cycle for n=10, t=6 repeated until wall clock exceeds 3600 s; check memory growth < 1 MB/hr.
  • Regression gate: criterion benchmarks stored as baseline JSON in benches/baselines/; CI fails if any benchmark regresses > 5%.
  • Share zeroization soak: run 100k split/reconstruct cycles; check peak heap with jemalloc stats.

Build & Release Engineering

Toolchain

Item Setting
Rust edition 2021
MSRV 1.78 (stabilized const trait methods needed)
Pinned toolchain rust-toolchain.toml with channel = "1.78.0"
cargo-deny pinned version in .cargo/deny.toml; deny advisories, yanked crates, duplicate deps
cargo-auditable embed SBOM into binaries at link time
cargo-sbom generate CycloneDX JSON on release builds
cargo-vet supply chain: third-party crate audits committed to supply-chain/

Reproducible Builds

  • All release binaries built inside a hermetic Docker image: rust:1.78-bookworm pinned by digest.
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set from last git commit timestamp; RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none -C debuginfo=2" for provenance.
  • cargo build --locked enforced; Cargo.lock committed and checked in CI.
  • Two independent CI runners (GitHub Actions + self-hosted) produce binaries; diffoscope compares; mismatch → release blocked.

Artifact Signing

Artifact Signing method
Release binaries (tss-cli static Linux/macOS/Windows) cosign sign-blob with Sigstore keyless OIDC; .sig + rekor transparency log entry
Crate publishes cargo publish via CI bot with crates.io token; signed git tag triggers publish
Docker image (if published) cosign sign with SBOM attestation
Checksums sha256sum manifest committed to release notes and signed with the above key

CI/CD Topology

PR → lint (clippy --deny warnings) → fmt (rustfmt --check) → test (cargo nextest) →
     fuzz smoke (60s) → conformance vectors → deny/audit/vet →
     benchmark compare (criterion vs baseline) →
merge → soak (nightly) → release-candidate build (reproducible) →
release tag → sign artifacts → publish crates → update SBOM store
  • cargo nextest replaces cargo test; parallel test isolation; output archived per run.
  • cargo clippy -- -D warnings -D clippy::all -D clippy::pedantic -A clippy::module_name_repetitions
  • cargo doc --no-deps built and diff-checked on every PR to catch doc regressions.
  • Feature matrix CI: --no-default-features, --all-features, each optional feature combination.
  • MSRV check: CI also runs on rust:1.78.0 pinned toolchain.

Version Stamping

  • CARGO_PKG_VERSION + git short SHA baked into tss-cli --version output at build time via build.rs.
  • All crates in the workspace share the same version; workspace-level [package] version is the single source.
  • Semver policy: major bump for any breaking type change in tss-core; minor for new crates; patch for fixes.
  • Changelogs: git-cliff generates from conventional commits; merged into CHANGELOG.md on release.

Dependency Auditing and Supply-Chain

  • cargo deny check advisories bans licenses sources — all licenses must be MIT, Apache-2.0, or ISC.
  • cargo vet: every third-party crate either has a committed audit or is imported from the mozilla trust root.
  • Dependabot enabled for weekly automated PRs against patch updates; auto-merge if all CI passes.
  • cargo-cyclonedx produces per-workspace SBOM; attached to GitHub release as sbom.cdx.json.
  • No unsafe blocks outside tss-field (constant-time) and tss-core (zeroization); enforced by #[forbid(unsafe_code)] at workspace root with explicit per-crate opt-in.

Release Channels

Channel Trigger Audience
canary merge to main internal testing; no semver promise
beta tag v*.*.*-beta.* external early adopters; breaking changes noted
stable tag v*.*.* production; reproducible build + signed artifacts

Performance Engineering

Benchmarking Strategy

Framework: criterion v0.5+ with criterion::black_box; all benches in benches/ under workspace.

M# Benchmark Input range Target (Phase 14)
M1 field_mul N/A < 50 ns per multiplication
M2 field_inv N/A < 500 ns (constant-time Fermat)
M3 shamir_split t=3, n=10 < 100 µs
M4 shamir_reconstruct t=3 of 10 < 50 µs
M5 lagrange_coefficients t subset of n, n up to 20 < 20 µs
M6 scalar_mul (Montgomery ladder) 256-bit scalar < 200 µs
M7 vss_verify_share Feldman, t=3 < 300 µs
M8 dkg_full_round (simulated, n=10, t=6) N/A < 50 ms end-to-end per party
M9 frost_round1_commit per signer < 500 µs
M10 frost_round2_partial_sign per signer < 500 µs
M11 frost_aggregate t=6 of 10 < 1 ms
M12 bls_partial_sign per signer < 1 ms
M13 bls_aggregate_verify t=6 of 10 < 10 ms (pairing-dominated)
M14 proactive_refresh_party n=10, t=6 < 20 ms per party

Baseline JSON committed to benches/baselines/; CI regresses at > 5% wall-clock increase (3 runs, geometric mean).

Profiling Methodology

Tool Use case Trigger
perf stat + perf record CPU cycles per benchmark; branch misprediction Manual on Linux runner
cargo-flamegraph Identify hot call paths in tss-field, tss-group cargo flamegraph --bench bench_shamir
heaptrack Allocation count and peak heap per operation heaptrack target/release/tss-cli dkg ...
valgrind --callgrind Instruction-level profile for constant-time verification CI step on MSRV image
DHAT (dynamic heap analysis tool) Allocation lifetimes; detect long-lived secret buffers Part of soak run

Flamegraph workflow: run cargo flamegraph, save .svg to profiles/<version>/, diff hot nodes against previous version; regressions > 10% nodes documented in PR description.

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate with tracing-subscriber JSON formatter; per-span fields include: phase, party_id, round, n, t.
  • Spans: shamir::split, shamir::reconstruct, dkg::round_commit, dkg::round_share, frost::round1, frost::round2, frost::aggregate, proactive::refresh.
  • In benchmarks, tracing::Span::record("iterations", N) for per-operation throughput logging.
  • Production use: RUST_LOG=tss_protocol=debug for round-level timing; RUST_LOG=tss_field=trace for per-operation arithmetic (development only).

Allocator Analysis

  • Default allocator: system malloc; jemalloc optional via tss-cli feature flag jemalloc.
  • jemalloc stats endpoint: malloc_stats_print captured at end of benchmark run; heap fragmentation checked.
  • Secret-bearing allocations tracked manually: Share, Scalar, Polynomial are stack-allocated or Box<Zeroizing<...>>; no heap copy without explicit zeroization.
  • Allocation count target: shamir_split(t=3,n=10) allocates at most 1 heap block (the shares Vec); zero allocs in field_mul.

Constant-Time Audit

  • dudect timing-leakage test run against field_inv, lagrange_coefficient, scalar_mul; any leakage coefficient > 1e-4 is a blocker.
  • subtle crate: all conditionals on secret values use Choice / ConstantTimeEq; linted via cargo clippy -- -W clippy::suspicious_arithmetic_impl.
  • CI: valgrind --tool=callgrind diff on field_inv between s=1 and s=p-1 inputs; instruction count must match exactly.
  • Integration: cargo test --features ct-audit enables dudect integration tests compiled out by default.

Tail-Latency Analysis

  • For DKG and FROST protocols: simulate n=20, t=11 with a synthetic network adding uniform random 0–5 ms latency per message; collect 1000 runs; report p50, p95, p99, p99.9.
  • Target: p99 FROST signing < 200 ms for n=20, t=11 under 5 ms per-hop network.
  • Outlier investigation: any p99 > 3× p50 triggers a flamegraph capture on that scenario.

Memory Profiling

  • heaptrack attached to tss-cli dkg --parties 10 --threshold 6; confirm peak heap < 2 MB.
  • After each protocol phase, intermediate Polynomial and sub-share Vecs are dropped; heaptrack timeline confirms drops between phases.
  • Zeroization verification: valgrind --tool=memcheck --track-origins=yes confirms zeroed memory is not read after zeroize.

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressure

Shamir SSS, VSS, and threshold signing all scale with the number of parties n and threshold t. The dominant costs are:

Operation Complexity Bottleneck
Polynomial evaluation (split) O(n·t) field multiplications Field mul throughput
Lagrange interpolation (reconstruct) O(t²) field muls + t inversions Field inverse (Fermat: O(log p) muls)
Feldman VSS verify (one share) O(t) scalar muls Scalar mul (EC group)
DKG broadcast volume O(n²) messages (each of n parties sends to n-1) Network; grows quadratically
FROST round 1 broadcast O(n) nonce commitments Linear in n
FROST round 2 aggregate O(t) scalar adds + 1 scalar mul Linear in t
BLS aggregate O(t) scalar muls in G1 + 2 pairings Pairing cost (constant in t)
Proactive refresh O(n²) sub-share messages + O(n·t) evals Same as DKG

Scaling Ceilings

Constraint Ceiling Source
n (parties) 255 (ShareId is u32 but recommended max 255 for safety) DKG O(n²) message complexity
t (threshold) ≤ n; practical max ~128 Lagrange O(t²) cost
Field prime 256-bit (curve scalar field); fixed Security level 128-bit
Share serialization 64 bytes per share (32-byte scalar + 4-byte id) Negligible
DKG transcript O(n²·t) field elements stored per party Memory: n=100,t=51 → ~16 MB per party
Signing transcript O(n) nonce commitments = O(n) EC points 32 bytes/point; n=255 → ~8 KB

Quadratic DKG Bottleneck

  • DKG requires every party to VSS-deal to every other: n(n-1) encrypted sub-share transmissions + n commitment broadcasts.
  • At n=20: 380 messages per DKG; at n=100: 9900 messages.
  • Mitigation: use a broadcast channel (one multicast per dealer's commitments + n-1 encrypted sub-shares); reduces network connections from O(n²) to O(n) per dealer.
  • DKG is a one-time setup; the quadratic cost is acceptable for n ≤ 100 in practice.

Horizontal Scaling — not applicable

Threshold protocols are inherently distributed across fixed parties; there is no horizontal partition of the core cryptographic operations. The signing set is fixed at protocol instantiation.

Vertical Scaling (single-party perspective)

  • CPU: field arithmetic is single-threaded per party; parallelism within a party is limited to independent VSS verifications (one per received share) — parallelizable with rayon.
  • Memory: each party holds O(n·t) field elements during DKG; at n=100,t=51 and 32 bytes/scalar → ~160 KB per party. Negligible.
  • I/O: protocol is network-bound during broadcast rounds; CPU is idle waiting for round-2 messages in FROST.

Coordination Overhead

Protocol Rounds Bottleneck
Shamir split/reconstruct 1 (dealer→holders; holders→dealer) Latency of single round-trip
Feldman/Pedersen VSS 2 (commit; share) Broadcast commitment + unicast shares
DKG (Pedersen) 3 (commit; share; complaint+finalize) O(n²) messages in round 2
FROST signing 2 (nonce commit; partial sign) Sequential round dependency
BLS threshold signing 1 (each signer broadcasts partial sig) Aggregator latency
Proactive refresh 3 (mirrors DKG structure) Same as DKG

Lagrange Coefficient Scaling

  • Naive Lagrange over t shares: O(t²) field multiplications + t inversions.
  • Pre-computation of all x_i - x_j differences for a fixed signing set amortizes to O(t) additional muls per additional evaluation.
  • Batch inversion (Montgomery trick): t inversions → 3(t-1) muls + 1 inversion; used in tss-shamir::reconstruct.
  • At t=128: ~16384 muls + 1 batch inversion ≈ 384 muls = ~16768 total field muls ≈ < 1 ms.

BLS Pairing Cost

  • Pairing e(P,Q) is the dominant cost in BLS aggregate verify; independent of t.
  • Partial sign (scalar mul in G1): scales O(t) for aggregation; dominated by 2 pairings regardless.
  • At t=128: 128 G1 scalar muls (partial sigs) + Lagrange aggregation (O(t) G1 adds) + 2 pairings.
  • Pairing on BLS12-381: ~1–3 ms on modern hardware; dominates all other BLS costs.

Storage Growth

  • Shares are ephemeral; persisted only by individual holders (one 64-byte share per party).
  • DKG transcript for audit: O(n²) field elements + O(n·t) commitment points; at n=20,t=10: ~12 800 bytes. Acceptable.
  • Proactive: old shares zeroized after refresh; no accumulation.
  • CLI output files: one .shares file per split (n × 64 bytes); not a scaling concern.

Scheduler and Metadata Scaling

  • tss-protocol round orchestration: in-memory hash map of party_id → message_buffer; O(n) entries.
  • Timeout tracking: one tokio::time::Sleep per expected message; O(n) timers per round.
  • Complaint set: BTreeSet<ShareId>; O(n log n) insert/lookup; negligible.
  • No external scheduler dependency; pure in-process state machine; scales to n=255 with < 1 MB overhead.

Mental Models & First Principles

These are the compressed insights an expert holds before writing a single line. Each entry names the bug class it prevents and the section of this blueprint where it is applied.

MM1 — A degree-(t-1) polynomial is uniquely fixed by any t points; t-1 or fewer points reveal nothing. Any t evaluations over a field uniquely determine the degree-(t-1) polynomial through them (Lagrange uniqueness theorem). Fewer than t evaluations leave one or more degrees of freedom, so every value in the field is equally likely as the constant term — information-theoretic perfect secrecy. Bug class without it: treating t-1 shares as "almost enough" and building a feature that hints at the secret when shares approach t. Applied in: §P1, §I1–I2, Phase 2–3, §8.6.

MM2 — The constant term is the secret; evaluation at x=0 is reconstruction. The sharing polynomial is f(x) with f(0) = secret. Share i = f(i). Lagrange interpolation at x=0 computes the polynomial's value at x=0 from any t distinct non-zero evaluations. The zero point is sacred and must never be a share ID. Bug class without it: using x=0 as a valid share ID, handing the secret directly to a party. Applied in: §I3, §I4, §8.6, Phase 2–3.

MM3 — Feldman commitments are binding but not hiding; Pedersen commitments add hiding. In Feldman VSS, the constant-term commitment is C_0 = g^secret. Every party sees g^secret. This is fine for share verification but catastrophic as a DKG round-1 broadcast because it leaks the sub-secret before the qualified set is determined. Pedersen adds a blinding polynomial so C_0 = g^{a_0} h^{b_0} — perfectly hiding under the discrete-log relation between g and h. Bug class without it: using Feldman in DKG round 1 and leaking each party's sub-secret commitment, allowing an adversary who controls the qualified-set decision to bias the joint key. Applied in: §P2, §P3, Phase 6–8, §I5–I6.

MM4 — Lagrange coefficients over a prime field are exact; they must be computed constant-time. Lagrange interpolation over F_p involves no floating-point; every division is a modular inverse; every result is an element in {0, …, p-1}. The inverse computation (Fermat's little theorem: a^{p-2} mod p, or constant-time extended-GCD) must run in data-independent time because the Lagrange coefficient is multiplied by the share value — a secret. Bug class without it: a variable-time modular inverse leaking the denominator product via timing, from which the share value can be extracted by lattice analysis. Applied in: §P4, §I9, Phase 3, Phase 10, Phase 14.

MM5 — FROST binding factors prevent rogue-nonce forgery; without them two-round Schnorr is broken. In a naive two-round multi-sig, an adversary runs k signing sessions simultaneously, extracts k rows of nonce commitments, and solves the ROS linear system to produce a forged aggregate signature. FROST's per-signer rho_i = H(i, msg, all-commitments) ties each nonce to the full commitment list and the message, making cross-session manipulation impossible. Bug class without it: omitting or truncating the commitment list in the binding-factor hash, allowing the Drijvers attack. Applied in: §P5, §I7, Phase 9–10, §T2 threat, §8.4.1.

MM6 — Proactive refresh is resharing zero; the secret is invariant because zero polynomials add zero. Each party samples a fresh degree-(t-1) polynomial with constant term exactly 0 and adds its evaluations to the existing shares. Since sum of zero-constant-term polynomials has constant term 0, the joint polynomial's constant term — the secret — is unchanged. The adversary must now compromise t parties within one epoch to win, not across all time. Bug class without it: allowing a refresh polynomial with a non-zero constant term, silently changing the secret and making all existing signatures unverifiable. Applied in: §P7, §I8, Phase 12, §8.16.

MM7 — The joint public key is the sum of constant-term commitments; no party holds the joint secret. After DKG, Y = g^{sum of qualified dealers' a_0} = sum of {g^{a_0} from each qualified dealer}. This sum is computed publicly from Feldman/Pedersen constant-term commitments. The joint secret a_0 (the sum of sub-secrets) is never assembled in any memory location. Bug class without it: implementing a helper function reconstruct_joint_secret that combines sub-secrets for performance — this function is a single point of compromise that voids the threshold guarantee. Applied in: §P3, §I6, Phase 8, §§11.5 KeyShare API.

MM8 — Nonces in Schnorr are one-time secrets; reuse with two different messages yields the key. If the same (d_i, e_i) are used in sessions for messages m1 and m2, the two equations z_i = d_i + rhoe_i + lambdas_ic1 and z_i' = d_i + rho'e_i + lambdas_ic2 yield z_i - z_i' = lambdas_i(c1-c2) + (rho-rho')e_i. With both partial signatures visible, the share s_i is recoverable in one field division. Bug class without it: persisting nonces for "resume" functionality across crashes. Applied in: §P9, §I9, Phase 9, §T9 threat, §8.14.1.

MM9 — Identifiable abort requires per-partial-signature verification BEFORE aggregation. If the aggregator sums all z_i into z = sum(z_i) first and then verifies g^z == R·Y^c, a single bad z_i causes opaque failure with no attribution. Verifying each z_i individually against g^{z_i} == R_i·(Y_i)^c before summing pins the blame to the offending party. Bug class without it: aggregating first and failing opaquely — the session must restart but no party can be excluded, enabling indefinite denial-of-service. Applied in: §P6, §I7, Phase 10, §8.4.2, §T7 threat.

Comparative Architecture Table

This design vs. real threshold-cryptography and secret-management systems.

Dimension This design (tss-shamir + FROST + BLS) HashiCorp Vault Shamir unseal Blakley's scheme Feldman/Pedersen VSS alone sss / libgfshare Threshold ECDSA (GG20/CGGMP21)
Sharing primitive Polynomial evaluation over F_p (prime scalar field) Polynomial evaluation over GF(2^8) or GF(2^256) per the vault implementation Hyperplane intersection in Z_p^t Polynomial + EC group commitments Polynomial over GF(2^8) (byte-level field) Multiplicative additive shares via Paillier-assisted protocols
Field arithmetic 256-bit prime field (curve scalar order); constant-time GF(2^8) or GF(2^256); not constant-time in original Go impl Integer arithmetic mod large prime Same as Shamir + EC scalar mul for commitments GF(2^8) byte XOR arithmetic Z_{N} (Paillier modulus) + Z_p (ECDSA curve)
Verifiability Full VSS (Feldman binding + Pedersen hiding+binding); shares verified before use None: shares not verified against commitments in unsealing flow None in base scheme; extensions exist Core purpose of the crate; the only deliverable None: no commitment verification in libgfshare Partial: range proofs on multiplicative shares; no dealer commitment
Share refresh / proactive security Full proactive refresh (Herzberg 1995): zero-polynomial reshare preserving secret; epoch model Not supported: Vault unseal keys are static until re-initialization Not applicable in base scheme Not supported Not supported Not supported in standard GG20; experimental in CGGMP21
Dealer trust Dealerless via GJKR DKG; no party learns the joint secret Operator holds joint secret at initialization; shares are derived post-hoc Centralized dealer required Centralized dealer required; verifiability checks dealer honesty Centralized dealer; no verification Distributed via Paillier-based sub-protocols; no single dealer for key but protocol complexity high
Reconstruction site Distributed (no reconstruction during signing — FROST/BLS produces output without assembly) Centralized: Vault process reconstructs the root key internally Centralized Centralized Centralized Distributed: ECDSA signature produced without key reconstruction

HashiCorp Vault Shamir unseal (github.com/hashicorp/vault, vault/shamir/): Vault applies Shamir SSS over GF(2^8) (byte field) to split the unseal key into operator-held shards. The key is reconstructed inside the Vault process at unseal time — reconstruction happens centrally. The scheme has no verifiability (no VSS commitments): a corrupt unseal shard silently corrupts reconstruction rather than producing a complaint. This trade-off wins in operational settings where key-management usability is paramount, the threat model excludes malicious operators (only accidental key loss), and the simplicity of byte-XOR arithmetic eases auditing. Vault's approach is not suitable for adversarial multi-party settings.

Blakley's scheme (Blakley 1979): Instead of polynomial evaluation, Blakley uses geometric hyperplanes in t-dimensional space over Z_p. Each share is a hyperplane; reconstruction is the intersection point. The secret is a coordinate of the intersection. Shares are larger than in Shamir (a full t-dimensional vector vs. one field element), and share verification requires additional structure. Blakley wins when the application needs share independence properties that are easier to analyze geometrically, or when visual secret sharing (a geometric variant) is the target. Rejected here because share size is O(t) field elements vs. O(1) for Shamir, and the implementation ecosystem is far smaller.

Feldman/Pedersen VSS alone (without DKG or threshold signing): The vsss-rs crate and related libraries implement VSS as a library primitive. They provide the dealer-commitment-verification step but require a trusted dealer to hold the secret. This design subsumes that entire capability and extends it to dealerless DKG. VSS-only wins when the threat model admits a trusted dealer (e.g., a hardware root of trust or a trusted initialization ceremony) and the goal is purely share verification without protocol overhead.

sss / libgfshare: Implements Shamir SSS over GF(2^8) with a focus on splitting arbitrary binary data (files, etc.). No verifiability, no constant-time guarantees, no EC operations. Wins for simple file-splitting use cases where security against active adversaries is not required, the secret is large binary data (GF(2^8) handles byte arrays directly), and implementation simplicity is the priority.

Verifiable Secret Sharing in threshold-ECDSA wallets (GG20/CGGMP21): Threshold ECDSA (e.g., Fireblocks, ZenGo, tss-lib by bnb-chain) uses a fundamentally different structure. The ECDSA signing equation requires the nonce inverse (k^{-1}) to be computed in threshold — this requires Paillier homomorphic encryption and range proofs to avoid leaking shares. Share verification uses ZK proofs rather than Feldman/Pedersen commitments. The result is 5–10 rounds of communication vs. FROST's 2. GG20/CGGMP21 wins when the target signature scheme is ECDSA specifically (Bitcoin, Ethereum signatures) and Schnorr is not an option. Rejected here because FROST over Schnorr is 5× simpler with a cleaner security proof and two-round latency.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived step-by-step from §4.1 System Constraints.

C1 — Per-share size. From §4.1: ShareId = 4 bytes, Scalar = 32 bytes. Per share: 4 + 32 = 36 bytes.

C2 — Share vector for n=255. n=255 shares × 36 bytes = 9,180 bytes ≈ 9 KB. Fits in L1 cache on any modern CPU.

C3 — Dealer polynomial memory. From §4.1: Polynomial memory = t × 32 bytes. At t=128 (practical maximum): 128 × 32 = 4,096 bytes = 4 KB. Zeroized after split; peak heap is one allocation.

C4 — Feldman commitment vector (broadcast size per DKG dealer). From §4.1: Commitment vector = t × 32 bytes (EC points compressed). At t=50: 50 × 32 = 1,600 bytes per dealer broadcast. At n=100 dealers: 100 × 1,600 = 160 KB total commitment material per DKG session per party.

C5 — DKG per-party message size. From §4.1: DKG per-party message size ≈ n × 128 bytes at n=255. Formula: n_peers × (32 bytes share + 32 bytes blinding + 33 bytes commitment_point_each + overhead) ≈ n × 128. At n=50: 50 × 128 = 6,400 bytes ≈ 6 KB per party per DKG round. Total DKG traffic per party: O(n) broadcast + O(n) unicast ≈ 2 × n × 128 bytes.

C6 — DKG total wire traffic (n=50, t=26). Total unicast messages: n × (n-1) = 50 × 49 = 2,450 messages × 128 bytes = 313 KB. Total broadcast: n × commitment_size = 50 × 1,600 = 80 KB. Grand total: ~393 KB for a 50-party DKG. Fits in a single HTTP/2 batch with no fragmentation concern.

C7 — Shamir split CPU: Horner evaluation. From §4.1 and §8.6.2: Horner cost = O(n × t) field multiplications. From §4.1: Shamir split latency (p50) < 1 ms at t=10, n=20. Field mul ≈ 10–50 ns (from §4.2 benchmark targets). At t=10, n=20: 200 field muls × 50 ns = 10 µs. At t=100, n=200: 20,000 × 50 ns = 1 ms — matches §4.1 budget.

C8 — Shamir reconstruct CPU: Lagrange. From §8.6.1: O(t²) field muls + t inversions. With batch inversion (Montgomery trick): O(3(t-1)) muls + 1 inv. At t=10: ~30 muls + 1 inv ≈ 30 × 50 ns + 500 ns = 2 µs. At t=128: 384 × 50 ns + 500 ns ≈ 20 µs. Well within the < 1 ms budget.

C9 — Feldman VSS verify per share. From §4.1: Feldman VSS verify (p50) < 5 ms at t=10. Verify cost: O(t) scalar muls (EC). From §4.1: scalar mul ≈ 60–200 µs. At t=10: 10 × 200 µs = 2 ms (without Pippenger batch). With batch multiscalar mul: ≈ 0.5 ms — matches budget.

C10 — FROST nonce commitment round (n=100). From §4.1: FROST round 1 per party < 10 ms. Each party: 2 scalar muls (D_i, E_i) = 2 × 200 µs = 400 µs. Binding factor: hash O(n) bytes per signer = 100 × 100 bytes = 10 KB hashed ≈ 0.1 ms. Group nonce: 100 scalar muls + 100 point adds ≈ 100 × 200 µs = 20 ms at aggregator (matches < 20 ms target in §4.1 for this step).

C11 — Proactive refresh bandwidth (n=50, t=26). Refresh mirrors DKG: O(n²) unicast messages. At n=50: 2,450 unicast × (32 bytes delta share + 1,600 bytes commitment) ≈ 2,450 × 1,632 = 4 MB total bandwidth per epoch. At one refresh per day (86,400 s), average bandwidth = 4 MB / 86,400 s ≈ 46 bytes/s — negligible for any transport.

C12 — Storage per key share. From §4.3: Storage per key share ≈ 100 bytes (KeyShare serialized). From §11.5: KeyShare = 4 (id) + 32 (secret_share) + 33 (public_share) + 33 (group_public) + 8 (params) + 8 (epoch) + AEAD tag (16) = 134 bytes. Matches §4.3 estimate. At n=255 parties, total key material cluster = 255 × 134 = 34 KB — trivial.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each question requires synthesis of at least two sections. The named §/phase is where the answer is assembled.

  1. Why does the complaint round in DKG need to run even when no party has complaints? What happens if it is skipped? (§8.3 DKG state machine + §P6 identifiable abort + §T12 threat)

  2. A Lagrange coefficient lambda_i is computed over the signing set S. If the signer at step 10.2 uses a different S than the aggregator, the aggregate fails to verify. Trace the exact byte that differs and identify which encoding rule prevents the mismatch. (Phase 9–10 + §8.4.1 binding factor + §8.12 canonical encoding)

  3. Explain why the Pedersen blinding polynomial b(x) must have a random b(0), not b(0) = 0. What security property is lost if b(0) = 0? Name the §P and §I that formalize this. (§P2 + §P3 + §I5 + Phase 7.6 pitfalls)

  4. The proactive refresh invariant says sum(r_i(0)) = 0 for all refresh polynomials. How does finalize_refresh check this without knowing individual r_i(0) values? What group element does it verify? (Phase 12.2 finalize_refresh + §P7 + §I8)

  5. A party uses nonce (d_i, e_i) in two FROST signing sessions for messages m1 and m2. Show algebraically why this reveals s_i (the share scalar). Which constant-time property is NOT sufficient to prevent this — and what is? (§P9 + §8.14.1 zeroization sequence + §T9 threat + Phase 9.6 pitfalls)

  6. A DKG with n=5 parties produces a joint public key Y. Party 3 was disqualified during the complaint round. Describe the exact computation each remaining party performs for their final key_share.secret_share and group_public. Show why Y is still consistent across all parties. (Phase 8.2 + §8.3 + §I6 + §P3)

  7. FROST is described as producing signatures indistinguishable from single-signer Schnorr. Under what conditions does this indistinguishability break — i.e., when can a verifier tell that a threshold protocol was used? (§P8 + §8.4 FROST state machine + §P5 + §2.6 Calibration Value)

  8. What is the information-theoretic lower bound on the share size for a t-of-n scheme achieving perfect secrecy? Does Shamir achieve it? Does Blakley? Cite the papers. (§P1 + §I2 + Comparative Architecture Table — Blakley entry + B2 Ch.3)

  9. HashiCorp Vault uses Shamir SSS over GF(2^8). This design uses F_p (prime field, 256-bit). Name three concrete security or engineering differences that arise from this choice, citing a specific §P or §I for each. (Comparative Architecture Table + P4 constant-time + P1 information-theoretic + §4.1 field element size)

  10. Explain why the generator h used in Pedersen VSS must have no known discrete-log relationship to g. What is the name of the security property that is lost if log_g(h) is known? Which crate and function ensure this property in the implementation? (§P2 + §P3 + Phase 7.6 + §ADR-05 + tss-group/src/generators.rs)

  11. The DKG complaint resolution rule is: if the complaint equation verifies, the dealer is disqualified; if it fails, the complainant is disqualified. Why can exactly one of the two be guilty, and why does this guarantee that false complaints cannot remove an honest party without also removing the complainant? (§8.3.1 + §P6 + §I6 + §T12 threat)

  12. Walk through why BLS threshold signatures require cofactor clearing after hash-to-G1 and what attack is enabled by a point in the wrong coset. Identify which RFC mandates this step. (Phase 11 + §P8 + §T5 threat + S2 RFC 9380 + §11.6 pitfalls)

  13. The dudect timing harness is run in Phase 14 on field_inv, lagrange_coeff, and scalar_mul. What is the null hypothesis it tests, what does a t-value > 4.5 mean statistically, and why does Miri NOT catch the same bugs? (§I9 + §P4 + Phase 14.2 CT audit + §4.2 failure budget)

  14. Compare the round complexity and communication cost of FROST signing vs. threshold BLS signing for the same (t,n). When would you choose BLS despite its higher computation cost per operation? (Comparative Architecture Table + Scalability Analysis + §8.4 vs §8.5 + Phase 10 vs Phase 11)

  15. The proactive refresh requires every party to participate. If fewer than n parties respond before the epoch timeout, what happens? What is the blast radius of a partial refresh where k < n parties have updated their shares and k >= t? (Phase 12.6 pitfalls + §P7 + §I8 + Phase 12.9 failure analysis)

  16. Why does the FROST nonce commitment D_i = g^{d_i} and E_i = g^{e_i} use two nonces instead of one? What attack is defeated by the binding-nonce (E_i) that would succeed with only D_i? (§P5 + §8.4.1 + P6 Drijvers paper + Phase 9.6 pitfalls)

  17. Explain the GJKR DKG bias problem (from P11 CT-RSA 2003) and why the J. Cryptology 2007 revision of P4 is necessary. Under what cryptographic assumption does the bias become inconsequential, and when is it a problem? (§P4 paper note + Phase 8 security analysis + §I6 + Mental Models & First Principles MM7)

  18. You are designing a wallet where threshold keys must survive the loss of t-1 parties per epoch indefinitely. Which protocol phases must complete, and in what order? What happens to outstanding partial signatures if a refresh epoch starts mid-session? (Phase 12 + Phase 8 + §P7 + §I8 + Phase 12.6 concurrent-sign-refresh pitfall)

  19. The AggregateSignature type is designed to be indistinguishable from a standard Schnorr signature (P8). Does this mean the threshold structure is hidden from the network? From the joint-key holder? From a cryptanalyst with access to many signatures? Distinguish the three. (§P8 + §8.4 FROST state machine + §2.1 unique systems knowledge)

  20. A developer claims that since all field operations in tss-field are constant-time, the entire Lagrange interpolation in tss-shamir::reconstruct is also constant-time. Identify the one higher-level construct that can break this claim and explain why. (§I9 + Phase 3.2 + §P4 + Phase 14 CT audit + §ADR-27)

  21. Describe how a lying complainant is detected in DKG. What message does the honest aggregator broadcast to disprove the complaint? Why does the same mechanism that protects against false complaints also bound the blast radius to one disqualification per complaint? (§8.3.1 + §P6 + §T12 threat + Phase 8.2 Round 3)

  22. The BLS partial signature sigma_i = H(m)^{share_i} and the FROST partial signature z_i = d_i + rho_ie_i + lambda_ishare_i*c are both "partial" but the information they leak about share_i is different. Analyze what each reveals to a passive observer who collects all partial signatures. (§P8 + Phase 10–11 + §T10 timing threat + §8.5)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-1 — Blakley Hyperplane Geometry

Secret embedded as coordinate in Z_p^t vector space.
Each share = one hyperplane equation a_i · x = b_i (t unknowns).
Reconstruct = solve linear system of t hyperplane equations.

The secret is a specific coordinate of the unique intersection point of t hyperplanes in t-dimensional projective space over Z_p. Each share is a hyperplane (a vector of t field elements + a constant), making shares O(t) times larger than Shamir shares. Reconstruction is Gaussian elimination.

Regime where it wins: Geometric secret sharing and visual cryptography schemes (where shares are 2D pixel arrays and reconstruction is overlay) descend from Blakley's geometry. When the secret encoding is naturally geometric (images, spatial data), Blakley's representation maps directly. Also wins in theoretical analysis where orthogonality of hyperplanes provides useful algebraic structure for extension.

Real system embodying it: Blakley's original 1979 paper ("Safeguarding cryptographic keys," AFIPS 1979). Visual secret sharing (Naor & Shamir 1995) extends this for printed image shares.

Why rejected here: Share size is O(t) field elements vs. O(1) for Shamir (§4.1 share vector memory). Reconstruction requires full Gaussian elimination (O(t^3)) vs. O(t^2) Lagrange. No verifiability extensions as mature as Feldman/Pedersen. No production Rust ecosystem.

Alt-2 — Additive / Replicated Secret Sharing

Secret s split as: s = s_1 + s_2 + ... + s_n  (additive)
or:
Each party holds shares for every subset of size n-t (replicated).
Reconstruction: sum the additive shares.

In additive secret sharing, the secret is literally split into n random field elements summing to s. Any single share reveals nothing (one degree of freedom), but reconstruction requires ALL n shares — this is n-of-n, not t-of-n. Replicated secret sharing assigns to each party a bundle of shares indexed by all (n-t+1)-size subsets, achieving t-of-n but with exponentially many shares.

Regime where it wins: Additive secret sharing is the bedrock of MPC protocols (BGW, SPDZ) because it is linear — parties can add their shares to compute the additive secret sharing of a sum without interaction. This linearity enables generic circuit evaluation. Additive sharing wins when the goal is not just key custody but computation ON the secret (e.g., threshold AES, threshold PRFs). Replicated sharing wins for very small n (n ≤ 4) where the exponential share count is tolerable and zero-communication reconstruction is needed.

Real system embodying it: SPDZ (Damgård et al., CRYPTO 2012) uses additive sharing over rings for authenticated MPC. MP-SPDZ (github.com/data61/MP-SPDZ) is the production implementation. BGW protocol (Ben-Or, Goldwasser, Wigderson 1988) uses replicated sharing for information-theoretic MPC.

Why rejected here: Additive sharing is n-of-n — any party absence blocks reconstruction. This violates the t-of-n fault-tolerance requirement stated in §1.1. Replicated sharing is exponential in n; at n=20 it produces C(20,11) ≈ 167,960 shares per party — completely impractical. The goal here is key custody, not generic computation.

Alt-3 — Threshold Encryption Without Reconstruction (ElGamal / ECIES Variant)

Public key Y = g^s. Ciphertext C = (rY, m XOR H(rg)).
Decryption share: D_i = r * share_i * g (partial decryption).
Aggregate: combine t D_i via Lagrange to recover rY; XOR with H(rY).

Threshold decryption distributes ElGamal or ECIES decryption across t parties. No party reconstructs the secret key s; instead, each contributes a partial decryption share D_i = r·Y_i = r·g^{share_i}. The aggregator combines t partial decryptions via Lagrange interpolation in the group exponent to recover rY, then derives the symmetric key.

Regime where it wins: When the use case is encryption/decryption rather than signing. Threshold decryption is appropriate for escrow systems (recover data only with t parties), distributed PKI (no single CA holds the root key), and threshold wallets where transaction approval is by decryption of a blinded message. It wins over FROST/BLS when the protocol needs to protect ciphertexts, not produce signatures.

Real system embodying it: Tlock (threshold time-lock encryption, drand.love); threshold El Gamal in Helios voting (Benaloh 2009); PVSS schemes used in randomness beacons. The Pedersen DKG paper (P10) originally described threshold decryption as the primary use case.

Why rejected here: This project's deliverable is threshold signing (§1.1 out-of-scope: threshold decryption). Adding a decryption path would require an ElGamal ciphertext type, a partial decryption type, and a new verification equation — doubling the protocol surface without contributing to the learning objectives (§2.1). Flagged as PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register").

Deferred Features Register

Feature Deferred To Reason
BLS pairing backend (if no curve available) PhaseDeferred("Phase 11") Optional build-time dependency; stub compiles cleanly
Asynchronous Byzantine DKG PhaseDeferred("Phase 8") Requires async network runtime; out of scope for synchronous library core
Threshold decryption (El Gamal / ECIES) PhaseDeferred("Alternative Architectures — Alt-3") Different protocol surface from signing; see Alt-3 above
Real network transport (TCP/QUIC) PhaseDeferred("Phase 13") Caller-supplied authenticated channels; library provides message types only
Hardware wallet integration PhaseDeferred("§1.1") Requires HSM/secure enclave bindings; no Rust ecosystem stabilized
Post-quantum threshold schemes (CRYSTALS-Dilithium-based TSS) PhaseDeferred("§1.1") Standards not finalized; no production ecosystem in 2024; declared non-goal
Threshold ECDSA (Paillier-based) PhaseDeferred("Comparative Architecture Table") Protocol complexity 5× FROST; separate project; see Comparative Architecture Table (Threshold ECDSA paragraph)

Glossary

Alphabetical. Definitions are implementation-precise and operationally relevant.

Term Definition
Additive resharing Each current shareholder splits their share into new sub-shares of the same value; recipients sum sub-shares from all holders to obtain a new valid sharing of the original secret under a possibly different (t', n') threshold. Preserves I8.
Aggregate signature Combined output of threshold signing: (R, z) for FROST/Schnorr, or a single BLS curve point for threshold BLS. Verifies under the joint public key with the standard verification equation. Indistinguishable from a single-signer signature by a verifier (P8). Corresponds to AggregateSignature.
Binding commitment A commitment scheme property: no PPT adversary can open a commitment to two distinct values. Feldman VSS commitments are computationally binding under the discrete-log assumption but not hiding.
Binding factor (rho_i) Per-signer scalar rho_i = H(i, msg, all-commitment-list) in FROST. Ties each signer's nonce contribution to the message and all other signers' commitments, so a malicious signer cannot grind nonces to cancel honest contributions. Computed in tss-schnorr/src/binding.rs. Corresponds to BindingFactor.
BLS signature Signature scheme (Boneh, Lynn, Shacham 2001) using bilinear pairings: sign = H(m)^{sk} in G1; verify via e(sign, g2) == e(H(m), pk2). Output is a single short group element. Naturally aggregatable: product of signatures verifies against product of public keys. Threshold variant aggregates partial signatures via Lagrange interpolation in the exponent.
Commitment (VSS) A vector of EC points committing to the coefficients of the dealer's sharing polynomial. Feldman: C_j = g^{a_j}. Pedersen: C_j = g^{a_j} h^{b_j}. Published once by the dealer; used by all recipients for share verification. Corresponds to Commitment and PedersenCommitment.
Complaint round DKG round 3 (in tss-dkg/src/round_complaint.rs) in which a recipient who received a share failing VSS verification broadcasts a signed complaint identifying the misbehaving dealer and the disputed share. Honest participants resolve disputes; bad dealers are excluded from the qualified set. Underpins I6, P6.
Constant-time arithmetic Implementation discipline: all field inversions, Lagrange coefficient computations, scalar multiplications involving secrets, and share comparisons run in time independent of secret operands. Prevents timing side-channels. Enforced by subtle/ct_option crates and validated with dudect in Phase 14. Required by I9, P4.
Dealer The party in basic Shamir SSS that holds the plaintext secret, samples the sharing polynomial uniformly at random with the secret as constant term, evaluates it at n distinct non-zero share IDs, and distributes shares. In DKG every party acts as its own dealer, removing single-point trust.
Discrete-log assumption (DL) Hardness assumption: given g, g^x in a prime-order group, computing x is computationally infeasible. Underpins Feldman VSS binding, FROST unforgeability, and BLS security. Shamir SSS security is information-theoretic and does NOT rely on DL.
Distributed key generation (DKG) Protocol (GJKR 1999) where n parties each run a Pedersen/Feldman VSS as dealer; cross-verify received shares; run a complaint round to exclude cheaters; and sum qualified sub-shares to obtain individual KeyShare values. The joint public key Y = g^(sum of constant terms) is known to all; the joint secret key is never reconstructed. Implemented in tss-dkg.
Domain separation Distinct hash prefixes or DST strings distinguishing hash invocations (hash-to-scalar, hash-to-point, challenge, binding factor, transcript). Prevents cross-protocol collisions. Required at every point H(...) appears in tss-schnorr, tss-bls, tss-group.
Epoch The interval between proactive refresh protocol executions. An adversary must compromise t or more parties within one epoch to reconstruct the secret. Shorter epochs reduce the exposure window at the cost of refresh overhead. See Phase 12, P7.
Evaluation point The non-zero field element x_i at which the sharing polynomial f is evaluated to produce share_i = f(x_i). Encoded as ShareId(u32). The secret is the polynomial at x=0; zero is never a valid share ID (I10). Points must be distinct across all n participants.
Feldman VSS VSS scheme (Feldman FOCS 1987): dealer publishes commitments C_j = g^{a_j} for each coefficient a_j of the polynomial. Recipient with share s_i and ID x_i verifies: g^{s_i} == product_{j=0}^{t-1} C_j^{x_i^j}. Computationally binding under DL; not hiding — the constant-term commitment C_0 = g^{secret} leaks information about the secret. Used in DKG round 1 commit broadcast.
Field element An element of the prime field F_p over which the sharing polynomial is defined. In the EC instantiation F_p equals the curve scalar field. Addition, subtraction, multiplication, and inversion are closed in F_p. Corresponds to FieldElement (aliased as Scalar in tss-core).
FROST Flexible Round-Optimized Schnorr Threshold Signatures (Komlo & Goldberg SAC 2020; standardised as RFC 9591). A two-round threshold Schnorr protocol with per-signer binding factors. Round 1: nonce commitments. Round 2: partial signatures. Aggregation: sum partial z_i with Lagrange weighting. Verification: standard Schnorr under joint key.
Generator (nothing-up-my-sleeve) An EC group element used as a base for commitments or public keys, chosen by hashing a public constant to a curve point (hash-to-curve). The discrete-log relationship between two independent generators g and h must be unknown, ensuring Pedersen hiding. Implemented in tss-group/src/generators.rs.
Hash-to-point A uniform, collision-resistant map from an arbitrary byte string to an EC group element. Used for BLS message hashing (H(m) in G1) and to derive the second Pedersen generator h. Must follow a standardised hash-to-curve spec (e.g., RFC 9380).
Hash-to-scalar A domain-separated, uniform map from bytes to a non-zero element of F_p. Used for Schnorr challenges (c = H(R, Y, msg)), FROST binding factors, and transcript derivations. Implemented in tss-group/src/hash.rs.
Hiding commitment A commitment scheme property: the commitment reveals no (statistical or computational) information about the committed value. Pedersen commitments are perfectly hiding — given C = g^a h^b, every value a' has a valid opening (a', b') for a known h. Required for secure DKG so that the dealer's g^secret is not revealed during the commit phase.
Horner's method Efficient polynomial evaluation: f(x) = a_0 + x(a_1 + x(a_2 + ... + x*a_{t-1})) — evaluates a degree-(t-1) polynomial in t-1 multiplications and t-1 additions. Used in tss-shamir/src/split.rs to evaluate the sharing polynomial at each share ID.
Identifiable abort Protocol property: if any party misbehaves (submits an invalid VSS share, a bad partial signature, or a complaint without proof), all honest parties can identify the misbehaving party by index. The protocol can then restart excluding the identified party. Distinct from "abort" (protocol stops without blame assignment). Implemented in tss-protocol/src/abort.rs. See P6.
Information-theoretic security Security that holds against computationally unbounded adversaries; relies purely on probability theory, not computational hardness. Shamir SSS achieves information-theoretic perfect secrecy: any t-1 shares are uniformly distributed regardless of the secret (I2). Pedersen VSS provides information-theoretic hiding.
Joint public key The group element Y = g^(sum of qualified dealers' constant terms) produced by DKG. No single party holds the corresponding secret scalar. All threshold signatures produced by the qualified participant set verify under Y with the standard single-key verification equation. Corresponds to KeyShare.group_public. See I6.
Lagrange coefficient lambda_i = prod_{j in S, j != i} (x_j / (x_j - x_i)) mod p, computed over the set S of t share IDs used in reconstruction. Weights each party's contribution to the interpolated polynomial value at x=0. All arithmetic is exact in F_p; no approximation. Must be computed in constant time when inputs are secret shares.
Lagrange interpolation The unique degree-(t-1) polynomial passing through t given points (x_i, y_i) is recovered; its value at x=0 is the sum of y_i * lambda_i. In a prime field this is exact with no rounding error (I4). Any t-subset from the same polynomial yields the same constant term (I1).
Montgomery ladder A constant-time algorithm for scalar multiplication on elliptic curves that processes each bit with the same sequence of field operations regardless of bit value. Prevents timing and simple power-analysis side-channels on secret scalars. Used or referenced in tss-group/src/scalar_mul.rs.
Nonce commitment Per-round, per-signer pair of EC points (D_i, E_i) committing to a signer's hiding nonce d_i and binding nonce e_i before they are revealed. Broadcast in FROST round 1. Prevents a signer from adaptively choosing nonces after seeing others' commitments. Corresponds to NonceCommitment.
Pairing A bilinear map e: G1 × G2 → GT satisfying e(aP, bQ) = e(P, Q)^{ab}. Enables BLS verification: e(H(m)^{sk}, g2) = e(H(m), g2)^{sk} = e(H(m), pk2). Computationally expensive; used only in tss-bls. Marked optional via a Cargo feature flag; if unavailable, tss-bls/src/stub.rs returns Err(PairingNotAvailable).
Partial signature A single party's contribution to a threshold signature before aggregation. In FROST: z_i = d_i + rho_ie_i + lambda_ishare_i*c. In threshold BLS: sigma_i = H(m)^{share_i}. Each partial signature can be individually verified against the party's public share to support identifiable abort. Corresponds to PartialSignature.
Pedersen commitment Commitment C = g^a h^b where g, h are EC generators with no known discrete-log relationship. Perfectly hiding (any value a' can be opened with blinder b' = (a - a')·log_h(g) + b, which is unknown). Computationally binding under DL. Used in Pedersen VSS and as the basis for secure DKG.
Pedersen VSS VSS scheme (Pedersen CRYPTO 1991): dealer uses two parallel degree-(t-1) polynomials f(x) and f'(x); commits as C_j = g^{a_j} h^{b_j}; sends (f(x_i), f'(x_i)) to each recipient. Verification: g^{f(x_i)} h^{f'(x_i)} == prod C_j^{x_i^j}. Provides information-theoretic hiding (the commitment reveals nothing about the secret) and computational binding. Required for secure DKG.
Perfect secrecy The property of a scheme where the posterior distribution of the secret given the observed shares is identical to the prior distribution; i.e., the shares carry zero information about the secret. Shamir SSS achieves perfect secrecy for any t-1 or fewer shares (I2).
Polynomial (sharing) Univariate polynomial f(x) = a_0 + a_1·x + ... + a_{t-1}·x^{t-1} over F_p where a_0 = secret and a_1, ..., a_{t-1} are sampled uniformly at random. Corresponds to Polynomial { coeffs: Vec<Scalar> }. Zeroized immediately after share generation (P9). Degree is exactly t-1 (I3).
Proactive refresh Protocol (Herzberg et al. CRYPTO 1995): at each epoch boundary, parties jointly secret-share zero using random polynomials with zero constant term and add the resulting sub-shares to their existing shares. This re-randomizes shares while preserving the shared secret. Limits adversary to one epoch of compromise. Implemented in tss-proactive/src/refresh.rs. See I8, P7.
Qualified set The set of DKG participants Q whose VSS shares were accepted without unresolved complaints. The joint public key and final shares are derived exclusively from dealers in Q. If a party is not in Q, its contribution is discarded. Computed in tss-dkg/src/round_complaint.rs and tss-dkg/src/finalize.rs.
Resharing Redistribution of existing shares into a new sharing of the same secret under possibly different parameters (t', n'). Implemented via additive reshare in tss-shamir/src/reshare.rs. The secret never leaves F_p^t form. Required for participant rotation and threshold parameter changes. See Phase 4, I8.
Rogue-nonce attack An attack on naive multi-party Schnorr where a malicious signer chooses its nonce commitment after seeing honest signers' commitments, grinding it to cancel their contributions or forge a valid aggregate signature. FROST's per-signer binding factors rho_i prevent this by tying each signer's nonce to the message and the full commitment list before nonces are revealed. See P5, Phase 9.
Scalar An element of the elliptic-curve prime-order scalar field F_p, identical to FieldElement in the EC instantiation. All secret values (shares, nonces, keys), Lagrange coefficients, and challenges are represented as Scalar. Operations are constant-time.
Secret sharing A method of distributing a secret s among n parties such that any t parties can reconstruct s, but any t-1 or fewer parties learn absolutely nothing about s. Shamir's scheme (1979) achieves this information-theoretically via polynomial evaluation over a prime field.
Share The evaluation of the dealer's secret polynomial at a party's share ID: share_i = f(x_i) in F_p. Corresponds to Share { id: ShareId, value: Scalar }. Must never be transmitted over an unencrypted channel and is zeroized after use.
Share ID (ShareId) A non-zero element of F_p identifying a participant; the evaluation point x_i for that party's share. Encoded as ShareId(u32). The secret resides at x=0, which is therefore never a valid share ID. Duplicate or zero IDs are rejected with an error (I10, P10).
Share recovery Reconstruction of a lost or corrupted share by t or more honest helpers each computing a sub-share via additive reshare of their own share; the recovering party interpolates. Implemented in tss-proactive/src/recovery.rs. Does not expose any helper's full share.
t-of-n threshold A scheme parameterised by ThresholdParams { t, n } with 1 ≤ t ≤ n: exactly t (or more) parties are required to reconstruct the secret or produce a valid threshold signature; any t-1 (or fewer) learn nothing and cannot forge.
Threshold BLS A combination of BLS signing with Shamir sharing: each party i computes partial signature sigma_i = H(m)^{share_i}; any t partial signatures can be aggregated via Lagrange interpolation in the exponent to yield sigma = H(m)^{joint_secret}; verified by pairing: e(sigma, g2) == e(H(m), Y2). Implemented in tss-bls.
Threshold Schnorr Multi-party Schnorr signature scheme where any t of n parties jointly produce (R, z) that verifies under the joint public key with the standard Schnorr equation g^z == R · Y^c. FROST is the state-of-the-art two-round protocol, now standardised as RFC 9591.
Transcript An ordered, domain-separated cryptographic accumulator of all public values broadcast during a protocol run (commitments, public keys, messages, round identifiers). Binds all parties to the same protocol context, preventing replay and cross-session attacks. Implemented in tss-protocol/src/transcript.rs.
VSS (Verifiable Secret Sharing) An extension of Shamir SSS where the dealer publishes public commitments to polynomial coefficients, allowing each recipient to verify its share independently without trusting the dealer. Two main variants: Feldman VSS (binding, not hiding) and Pedersen VSS (perfectly hiding and binding). Implemented in tss-vss.
Zeroization Secure overwriting of memory regions holding secret data (polynomial coefficients, sub-shares, nonces) immediately after use and before deallocation, using compiler-barrier-safe writes that cannot be optimised away. Implemented via the zeroize crate's Zeroize and ZeroizeOnDrop traits. Required by P9 for all types in tss-core/src/zeroize.rs.

tss-group/src/scalar_mul.rs Constant-time scalar multiplication: Montgomery ladder or fixed-window; fn mul(scalar: &Scalar, point: &Point) -> Point
tss-group/src/generators.rs G and H nothing-up-my-sleeve generators produced via hash_to_point; discrete-log independence enforced by construction
tss-group/src/hash.rs hash_to_scalar(domain, data) -> Scalar; hash_to_point(domain, data) -> Point; both domain-separated, RFC 9380-based
tss-group/tests/group_test.rs Generator order checks; scalar-mul correctness (nP for known n); G, H independence sanity check
tss-vss/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; deps: tss-core, tss-field, tss-group
tss-vss/src/lib.rs Crate root; re-exports Feldman and Pedersen API
tss-vss/src/feldman.rs FeldmanCommitment; commit(poly, group) -> FeldmanCommitment; verify_share(share, commitment, group) -> Result<()>
tss-vss/src/pedersen.rs PedersenDealing; pedersen_deal(secret, params, ids, rng); pedersen_verify_share(id, share, blinding, commitments)
tss-vss/src/verify.rs Shared constant-time equality-check helpers used by both feldman.rs and pedersen.rs
tss-vss/tests/feldman_test.rs Soundness: tampered share fails; correct share passes; C_0 = g^secret leakage documented
tss-vss/tests/pedersen_test.rs Hiding: commitment is same distribution for different secrets; soundness: tampered share fails
tss-dkg/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; deps: tss-core, tss-vss, tss-protocol
tss-dkg/src/lib.rs Crate root; re-exports DkgParty, DkgConfig, DkgOutput
tss-dkg/src/party.rs DkgParty struct; per-party state machine; round-transition methods; DkgRound enum state tracking
tss-dkg/src/round_commit.rs Round 1 handler: sample polynomial, produce Pedersen commitments, broadcast DkgCommitMsg
tss-dkg/src/round_share.rs Round 2 handler: distribute encrypted sub-shares; verify received shares; record failures
tss-dkg/src/round_complaint.rs Round 3 handler: broadcast complaints with proof; resolve disputes
tss-dkg/src/round_finalize.rs Round 4 handler: sum qualified sub-shares into KeyShare; compute joint public key Y
tss-dkg/src/qualified_set.rs QualifiedSet tracking; threshold check (
tss-dkg/tests/dkg_test.rs n-party simulated DKG; assert joint pubkey == g^(sum constant terms); all honest parties agree on Y
tss-dkg/tests/complaint_test.rs Malicious dealer sends bad share; honest parties detect, broadcast complaint; dealer excluded from qualified set
tss-schnorr/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; deps: tss-core, tss-group, tss-dkg
tss-schnorr/src/lib.rs Crate root; re-exports round1, round2, aggregate, verify
tss-schnorr/src/nonce.rs Nonce, NonceCommitment generation with zeroize; Drop impl zeroizes hiding/binding scalars
tss-schnorr/src/binding.rs BindingFactor: rho_i = H(i, msg, commitment_list)
tss-schnorr/src/round1.rs round1_commit(signer_id, rng) -> (Nonce, NonceCommitment); publishes (D_i, E_i)
tss-schnorr/src/round2.rs partial_sign(key_share, nonces, binding, c) -> PartialSignature; z_i = d_i + rho_ie_i + lambda_is_i*c
tss-schnorr/src/aggregate.rs aggregate(R, partials) -> AggregateSignature; z = sum z_i; constructs (R, z)
tss-schnorr/src/verify.rs verify(sig, msg, joint_pub) -> bool; checks g^z == R . Y^c; deterministic challenge computation
tss-schnorr/tests/frost_test.rs RFC 9591 test vectors (all provided test cases pass); random (t,n) round-trip; all t-subsets produce valid sig
tss-schnorr/tests/rogue_nonce_test.rs Rogue-nonce simulation: adversary picks commitment after seeing honest ones; binding factor prevents forgery
tss-schnorr/tests/partial_verify_test.rs Per-signer partial-signature verification used in identifiable-abort test harness
tss-bls/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; [features] pairing = ["bls12_381"]; pairing backend optional
tss-bls/src/lib.rs Crate root; feature-gates pairing; re-exports partial, aggregate, verify
tss-bls/src/partial.rs partial_sign(share, msg) -> PartialSignature; sigma_i = H(m)^{share_i} in G1
tss-bls/src/aggregate.rs aggregate(partials, ids) -> AggregateSignature; Lagrange interpolation in group exponent
tss-bls/src/verify.rs verify(sig, msg, joint_pub) -> bool; checks e(sigma, g2) == e(H(m), Y2)
tss-bls/src/pairing.rs PairingEngine trait + default impl; feature-gated bls12_381 backend, alt bn254 behind a second feature
tss-bls/src/hash_to_curve.rs RFC 9380 hash_to_g1, hash_to_g2 wrappers with mandatory cofactor clearing
tss-bls/src/stub.rs Non-pairing stub: all functions return Err(TssError::PairingNotAvailable); documents deferral
tss-bls/tests/bls_test.rs Pairing-feature round-trip; partial-sig verify; Lagrange aggregation correctness
tss-proactive/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; deps: tss-core, tss-shamir, tss-vss
tss-proactive/src/lib.rs Crate root; re-exports refresh, recover_share
tss-proactive/src/refresh.rs refresh(current_shares, params, rng) -> Result<Vec<Share>>; sums zero-constant-term polys with existing shares
tss-proactive/src/zero_sharing.rs share_zero(params, rng) -> Vec<Share>; samples a degree-(t-1) polynomial with constant term exactly 0
tss-proactive/src/recovery.rs recover_share(target_id, helper_shares) -> Result<Share>; Lagrange interpolation over t helpers' sub-contributions
tss-proactive/tests/refresh_test.rs Secret invariance: reconstruct before and after refresh yields same value; randomised over 100 epoch transitions
tss-proactive/tests/recovery_test.rs Lost-share recovery from exactly t helpers; over-threshold and under-threshold boundary cases
tss-protocol/Cargo.toml Crate manifest; deps: all domain crates, serde, bincode
tss-protocol/src/lib.rs Crate root; re-exports orchestration and simulation API
tss-protocol/src/message.rs RoundMessage enum with variants per protocol round; serde Serialize/Deserialize for transport
tss-protocol/src/transcript.rs Transcript struct; append(domain, data) method; challenge() -> Scalar derivation; domain separation
tss-protocol/src/orchestrator.rs Orchestrator::run(config, parties) - sequences rounds, collects messages, detects timeouts, triggers abort
tss-protocol/src/abort.rs Identifiable-abort logic: identify_malicious(evidence) -> PartyId; blame assignment for DKG and FROST misbehavior
tss-protocol/src/timeout.rs Per-round deadline tracking; party eviction on expiry
tss-protocol/src/sim.rs SimNetwork: in-process n-party message bus; used by integration tests and tss-cli simulated runs
tss-protocol/tests/protocol_test.rs End-to-end: DKG + FROST signing over simulated 5-of-9; identifiable abort when one party sends bad partial sig
tss-cli/Cargo.toml Binary crate manifest; deps: all library crates, clap, hex, serde_json, anyhow
tss-cli/src/main.rs Entry point; clap App with subcommands: split, reconstruct, dkg, sign, verify, refresh
tss-cli/src/cmd_split.rs split subcommand: reads hex secret, t, n from args; calls tss-shamir::split; prints shares as JSON
tss-cli/src/cmd_reconstruct.rs reconstruct subcommand: reads t or more shares from file or stdin; prints recovered secret as hex
tss-cli/src/cmd_dkg.rs dkg subcommand: runs n-party simulated DKG via SimNetwork; prints joint pubkey and each party's KeyShare
tss-cli/src/cmd_sign.rs sign subcommand: selects FROST or BLS mode; runs two-round signing; prints aggregate signature
tss-cli/src/cmd_verify.rs verify subcommand: reads signature and joint pubkey from args; returns exit code 0 (valid) or 1 (invalid)
tss-cli/src/cmd_refresh.rs refresh subcommand: loads current shares; runs proactive refresh; prints new shares; old shares invalidated
tss-cli/src/encoding.rs Hex and base64 encode/decode for Share, Point, AggregateSignature; JSON serialization helpers
tss-cli/tests/cli_test.rs Integration tests via assert_cmd: split->reconstruct, dkg->sign->verify, refresh round-trips
benches/bench_field.rs Field add/mul/invert throughput (criterion)
benches/bench_scalar_mul.rs EC scalar mul benchmark (fixed vs variable base)
benches/bench_shamir.rs split and reconstruct for (t,n) in {(3,5),(7,11),(15,20)}
benches/bench_vss.rs Feldman and Pedersen commit + verify latency for t in {3,7,15}
benches/bench_frost.rs FROST round1, round2, and aggregate at t in {3,7,11}; total signing wall time
benches/bench_bls.rs BLS partial sign and Lagrange aggregate at t in {3,7}; pairing-verify latency
benches/bench_dkg.rs Full DKG ceremony latency per n
conformance/frost_rfc9591/vectors_ed25519.json RFC 9591 FROST/Ed25519 test vectors
conformance/frost_rfc9591/vectors_ristretto.json Ristretto255 FROST variant test vectors
conformance/shamir_reconstruct/vectors.json Random (t,n) split+reconstruct ground truth
conformance/bls_threshold/vectors.json Partial sig + aggregate + verify vectors for threshold BLS
fuzz/Cargo.toml Fuzz workspace manifest; libfuzzer-sys; references main workspace for crate versions
fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_share.rs Fuzz arbitrary (secret, t, n) tuples into split; asserts no panic, only Err(TssError::...)
fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_reconstruct.rs Fuzz arbitrary byte blobs as shares into reconstruct; asserts no panic, only Err(TssError::...)
fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_vss_verify.rs Fuzz corrupted Commitment bytes and share values into Feldman/Pedersen verify; no panic
fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_frost_partial.rs Fuzz malformed PartialSignature bytes into FROST aggregate; no panic; returns error
fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_dkg_message.rs Fuzz malformed RoundMessage bytes through the DKG orchestrator; no panic; identifiable abort or protocol error
examples/basic_split_reconstruct.rs 2-of-3 split, reconstruct, verify
examples/feldman_vss_demo.rs Deal + verify all shares + tamper one
examples/dkg_3party.rs Three-party DKG, derive joint key
examples/frost_sign_3of5.rs 3-of-5 FROST threshold signing
examples/proactive_refresh.rs Epoch refresh, verify secret preserved

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Total project timeline: 28–34 weeks. Durations are working-week estimates. Parallel phases (5–7, 9–11) overlap.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
Phase 1 tss-core (all types, error, zeroize, rng) + tss-field (F_p CT arithmetic, sampling) 1.5 w Subtle CT bugs in inv; wrong modular reduction; subtle API misuse Field axiom property tests green; ThresholdParams validation; ShareId(0) rejected; cargo-careful clean
Phase 2 tss-shamir::split (random polynomial, Horner evaluation, share output, poly zeroize) 1 w Weak CSPRNG leaks secret; dealer polynomial not zeroized; share ID collisions split tests: correct count, correct f(x_i) evaluation; poly zeroized on drop; t>n rejected
Phase 3 tss-shamir::reconstruct (Lagrange interpolation at x=0, distinct-id validation) 1 w Non-CT Lagrange leaks share via timing; duplicate ID accepted silently; under-threshold returns wrong value Round-trip for all t-subsets; duplicate-ID error; under-threshold Err(UnderThreshold)
Phase 4 Edge cases + additive_reshare (new t/n, sub-share matrix, reshare correctness) 1 w Reshare changes secret if sub-share column sums are wrong; blinding polynomial has nonzero constant term Reshare invariant: reconstruct after reshare equals original; new threshold enforced; zero-constant-term verified
Phase 5 tss-group (EC point ops, CT scalar mul, generators G/H, hash-to-scalar/point) 2 w Timing leak in scalar mul; h not independent of g; hash-to-point not uniform Order checks pass; scalar-mul correctness; CT audit; G,H independence documented and tested
Phase 6 tss-vss Feldman VSS (commitments C_j = g^{a_j}, share verification) 1 w Commitment arithmetic overflow; tampered share passes due to modular coincidence Soundness: tampered share rejected; correct share accepted; g^secret leakage documented in API
Phase 7 tss-vss Pedersen VSS (two-polynomial commitments, hiding + binding) 1 w Hiding broken if h = g; companion poly correlation; wrong cross-term in verification Hiding test (identical commitments for different secrets); soundness test; DKG integration smoke
Phase 8 tss-dkg 4-round protocol (commit, share, complaint, finalize; qualified set; joint key) 3 w Complaint round logic incorrect; qualified-set miscalculation; timing issue in message delivery Joint key == g^(sum qualified constant terms); all honest parties agree; cheater identified and excluded
Phase 9 tss-schnorr FROST round 1 (nonce sampling, commitment pairs (D_i, E_i), binding factor computation) 1.5 w Nonce reuse across sessions; commitment list incomplete before binding factor; domain separation missing Commitment list complete; rho_i reproducible from same inputs; no nonce reuse across 10k iterations
Phase 10 FROST round 2 (partial_sign, Lagrange weighting, aggregate, verify) + identifiable abort 2 w Lagrange coefficient in wrong field; off-by-one signing set indexing; challenge c computed incorrectly RFC 9591 test vectors pass; g^z == R·Y^c for all (t,n) subsets; invalid partial sig identified
Phase 11 tss-bls threshold BLS (partial sigma_i, Lagrange aggregate, pairing verify, stub fallback) 2 w Pairing curve unavailable at build time; Lagrange in wrong group; stub not activated correctly Pairing feature: full round-trip + pairing-verify pass; no-pairing feature: stub returns correct error; partial-sig verify works
Phase 12 tss-proactive proactive refresh (zero-sharing, share addition) + lost-share recovery 2 w Zero-sharing poly has nonzero constant term; recovery exposes helper shares; epoch boundary synchronisation Secret invariance across 100 refresh epochs; recovery from exactly t helpers; over-t sub-shares rejected
Phase 13 tss-protocol orchestration (transcript, timeouts, abort) + tss-cli (all 6 subcommands) 2 w Transcript ordering nondeterministic; CLI encodes shares with wrong endian; simulated network drops messages End-to-end CLI: split→reconstruct, dkg→sign→verify, refresh all pass; identifiable abort test green
Phase 14 Hardening: CT audit (dudect), 4 fuzz targets (24 h each), conformance suite, benchmarks, packing 3 w CT violations on nightly; fuzz finds panic in deserialization; benchmark regression from hardening All fuzz targets 24 h clean; dudect pass on share/nonce paths; RFC 9591 vectors pass; bench regression < 10%
Full build complete All 14 phases done; cargo test --workspace green; CI/CD passing; fuzz clean; conformance suite passing 28–34 w total Integration regressions across crate boundaries; pairing dependency version conflicts All tests pass; fuzz clean; RFC 9591 vectors pass; cargo-deny clean; ✅ tag eligible per INSTRUCTIONS.md
